# Own receipt-driven development (RDD) on our reviewer and verifier

Objective: replace gentle-ai's native review with our own mechanically enforced lifecycle (binding, validated results, verifier verdicts, typed evidence, delivery check, stop hook, CI gate, Codex launcher) before removing gentle. Branches `feat/own-rdd-t<n>`, one PR per task, merged in order (stacked-to-main, memory "one slice at a time"). Test runner: `go test` from `engine/` and `tools/<tool>/`; writer `builder` (sonnet, max), independent verification `verifier` (opus, high).

## Specs

- **S1 — Mechanical guarantees.** Owner: "necesitamos garantias mecanicas pruebas evidencias enontces necesitamos todo lo que nos las pueda otorga". Every rule of the review contract that prose states today (`skills/role-chain/SKILL.md`, `roles/reviewer/body.md`, `roles/verifier/body.md`, `odd/review-results/README.md`) is checked by engine code with a test; a check that cannot run refuses, never passes. This reverses the cut recorded at `docs/architecture/role-design/per-role-reviewer.md:76` (no engine verb, no schema), by the owner's answer above.
- **S2 — Review-result contract.** Each reviewer finding carries a stable `id`. An engine domain package parses a reviewer result and refuses it when: the echoed `base`, `head` or `diff_sha256` differ from the binding; `inspection.status` is not `completed`; the reviewed paths differ from `git diff --name-only base..head`; a severity or disposition is outside its enum; an `id` repeats; a BLOCKER or CRITICAL finding has no proof reference.
- **S3 — Binding and storage.** `labdrian review bind` computes the full base and head SHAs and `diff_sha256`, creates the detached worktree `~/labdrian-sdd-overlay-worktrees/review-<head12>` and prints the binding; `labdrian review record` validates a result against the recomputed binding (S2) and writes it atomically, never overwriting, under `odd/review-results/<feature>/<head12>-<n>.json`, then prints the role-handoff skeleton (`payload_kind: review-report`, evidence `review-diff` and `review-result` with their sha256). Paths derived from input cannot escape `odd/review-results/`.
- **S4 — Verifier verdicts.** The verifier returns one structured verdict per finding id (`reproduced`, `not-reproduced`, `not-run`, with command, output and base result); the engine parses and stores it beside the result, and one pure function decides whether the review blocks, per `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` (severe and reproduced, or unknown causality reproduced on head and not on base, blocks), with at most one correction round per candidate.
- **S5 — Typed evidence and delivery check.** `labdrian roles append` refuses a `review-report` record whose `review-diff` and `review-result` evidence is missing or whose sha256 differs from the stored files; `labdrian review check` recomputes `diff_sha256` for the head being delivered and refuses when no recorded, validated, non-blocking result binds it. The delivery role runs it. Owner (L15): "y buenos hay labros que no llevan review que ya cone l verifier basta por validacion del riesgo tambien"; design accepted in L16: `labdrian review exempt --feature F --base B --head H --risk <items>` reads the verifier's report on stdin and stores `<head12>-<n>.exempt.json` bound to the head's `diff_sha256`; it refuses a declared risk naming any high-risk item (1-6), a missing report, or a report that is not clear; `review check` clears a head with a non-blocking review result or a valid exemption bound to it, and a blocking review wins over any exemption. The risk is declared by the agent, not computed by the engine. Reviews are per commit, chained (L18): each review or exemption binds one commit's range from the head of the last reviewed commit, and `review check --base <delivery base>` clears a head only when clear results or exemptions cover `base..head` as an unbroken chain (each range starts where the one before ended); a gap, or any range not clear, refuses. Accepted limits (L19): a result that does not block but is not clear also stops a head with an exemption; the engine sets no minimum set of checks for an exemption report.
- **S6 — Stop hook of our own.** A Claude Code Stop hook installed by `labdrian install-hooks` detects a committed head on a non-default branch with no recorded result binding it and blocks the stop once per session and candidate with the exact `labdrian review bind` command; `status-hooks` reports it. Per L18 it asks only for the next unreviewed commit of the chain (`--base` = the head of the last reviewed commit), never the whole branch. Per L28 the hook also enforces the sweeper and the polisher by deterministic triggers: on the candidate commit it runs the sweeper's T1-T3 checks of `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` and reads the declared Risk line; when a sweeper check fires, or the declared risk names a high-risk item (1-6), and no record of that role binds the candidate, it blocks the stop once per session and candidate with the exact launch the parent must run; and `review check` refuses to clear a head whose trigger fired without that role's record. Per L34 (design accepted): (a) the risk is declared per commit as a git trailer `Risk: none` or `Risk: <items>` (same grammar as `review exempt --risk`), read with `git interpret-trailers`; a commit without the trailer is unknown risk and the hook asks for it, never a silent pass; (b) the polisher's record is its findings JSON stored as `odd/review-results/<feature>/<head12>-<n>.polisher.json` by `labdrian review record --role polisher`, reusing the binding and atomic store; (c) the sweeper's record is its `chore(sweep)` commit when it found something, or, when the engine's T1-T3 checks are clean, `<head12>-<n>.sweep.json`; the hook and `review check` look only for that record. Design of the L28 and L34 parts (shaper, L40; decisions D1-D8 of L40 are open and marked where they apply); acceptance criteria:
  - (a) **The Risk trailer.** The engine reads a commit's message raw (`git log -1 --format=%B <commit>`) and runs `git interpret-trailers --parse` on it, both through the Git port. The declaration is the one parsed trailer whose key equals `Risk` compared case-insensitively; its value is held to the grammar of `review exempt --risk` exactly (`none`, or the numbers 1 to 6 separated by commas with no space and none twice, e.g. `2,4`), through one parser `review.ParseRisk` that `review.CheckRisk` also calls, so every message and exit code of `review exempt` stays as it is. Failures, each an error wrapping `review.ErrRiskTrailer` and naming the commit: `it has no "Risk:" trailer in the final trailer block of its message`; `it has more than one "Risk:" trailer`; `its "Risk:" trailer wraps onto more than one line` (the parsed `<key>: <value>` is not one line of the raw message: git unfolded it, as for `4606a49f`, L39); `its "Risk:" trailer "<value>" is not "none" or a list of the numbers 1 to 6 separated by commas with no space (for example "2,4")`. A commit whose trailer fails is unknown risk: never a pass (L34 a, S1).
  - (b) **Where T1-T3 run and who pays T2.** The hook runs none of T1-T3: at every stop it reads only git (message, trailers, paths, first parent) and stored files, as T6a does, so its cost does not grow with the analyzers (open decision D1, against the L28 words "it runs the sweeper's T1-T3 checks"). T1-T3 run in the new verb `labdrian review sweep --feature F --base B --head H`, once per candidate, which the parent runs when the hook or the role-chain skill asks, and which stores the sweep record (c). B must be the first parent of H (`review.CheckOneCommit`, as `review exempt`). T1: the paths of `git diff --diff-filter=DR --name-only B..H`, and the removed names that `git grep -w -F` finds in the tree of H outside the paths of the diff; assumption (the skill's "removed name" is not defined): a removed name is a name declared on a removed line of a `.go` file (`func N`, `func (r T) N`, `type N`, a `const` or `var` name) or of a `.sh` file (`N()` or `function N`) that no added line of the diff declares again. Pinned in L52: a Go declaration is read only when it starts the line (column 0, where gofmt writes a package-level declaration), so a declaration local to a function (`var err error` indented in a body) and a name inside a grouped `const (` or `var (` block are not read (a known limit: a removed name of a grouped block is not swept); a `.sh` declaration may be indented, as today. Pinned in L55: the removed paths are listed with `git diff-tree -r --name-only --no-renames --diff-filter=DR B H`, so a rename lists its old path as removed (the paths of the binding are listed with `--no-renames` too); the removed and added lines are read from one `git diff-tree -p -U0 --no-renames B H -- <path>` (literal pathspec) per changed `.go` or `.sh` path of the binding, so a path is always the one git listed and never read from a diff header. Pinned in L59: the removed names are looked up together, at most 256 names per start of `git grep -I -z -o -w -F --no-color -e <name> [-e <name>...] <H>` (the Git port takes the list and returns the paths of each name; the adapter splits the list), whose records `<H>:<path>`, NUL, `<name>`, newline are read one after the other and grouped by name, each path once per name in the order git prints them; a record of another shape, or naming a name that was not asked, is an error; git's exit 1 with nothing printed is no mention; so a commit that removes n names starts ceil(n/256) greps, not n. T2: for each Go module (a directory holding `go.mod` at H) that holds a changed `.go`, `go.mod` or `go.sum`, `deadcode -test ./...` over the whole module (L39: scoping by package gives false positives) and `staticcheck` over the packages of the changed `.go` files; `shellcheck` over the changed `.sh` files; each on a copy of B and a copy of H at the stable paths `<state home>/labdrian/review-sweep/<repo key>/base` and `.../head` (`<repo key>`: the first 16 hex characters of the sha256 of the main worktree's root), emptied and refilled from the commit's tree at each run (how: pinned in L60, below), so staticcheck's path-keyed cache is reused (L39: ~20 s per candidate with fresh copies, ~5 s estimated with stable paths; the 5 s is an estimate, not a measurement). A line is new when its normalized form (the copy's root removed, and the `:<line>` or `:<line>:<col>` after the path removed) occurs more times at H than at B. A tool that is not found, or that exits non-zero without printing any line in its finding format, is a check that did not run: `review sweep` exits 2 naming the tool and stores nothing (S1); the exit-status conventions of each tool are unverified and the builder pins them in tests. A commit that changes no Go module and no `.sh` file runs no tool and T2 is clean. Pinned in L55: the modules are the directories of every `go.mod` in the tree of H (`git ls-tree -r`), a changed path belongs to the innermost one, and a path under none runs no Go tool; the packages are `.` or `./<dir>` of each changed `.go` file relative to its module, so a commit that changes only `go.mod` or `go.sum` runs deadcode alone; a package, file or module that one copy does not have (the commit adds or removes it) is left out of that side's run, and a run with nothing left starts no tool and finds nothing; deadcode runs as `deadcode -test ./...` in the module and shellcheck as `shellcheck -f gcc` in the copy's root. A tool ran when it exited 0, or exited non-zero after printing at least one line `<path>:<line>[:<col>]: <text>` on stdout; not found, stopped at its deadline (10 minutes for each start unless the caller gives another), or non-zero with no such line, it did not run. A copy is filled into the emptied directory, refusing an entry whose name leads out of it or whose directory is a link (L55 pinned `git archive --format=tar` unpacked by the engine; T6b3 built that, and L60 replaces it until T6b4b lands). Pinned in L60 (replacing the `git archive` fill above and the attribute switches pinned in L59, which git 2.43.0 does not honor for `git archive`): a copy is the exact bytes of the commit's tree, with no attribute, filter or end-of-line conversion of any kind, so `export-ignore`, `export-subst`, `text`/`eol`, filter drivers and `core.autocrlf` change nothing. It is filled from `git ls-tree -r -z -l --full-tree <commit>` (mode, type, object name, size and path of every entry) and one `git cat-file --batch` whose standard input lists the object name of every blob entry, one per line, in the order of the listing; each answer `<name> blob <size>` LF `<bytes>` LF must name the object asked, in that order, with the size the listing gives, and `<name> missing` or any other shape is an error. An entry of mode `100644` is written as a regular file 0644, `100755` as 0755, `120000` as a link whose target is the blob's bytes, `160000` (a submodule) is skipped, and any other mode is an error. Before writing, an entry whose path is not local to the directory (`filepath.IsLocal` after `path.Clean`) or whose parent directory below the copy is a link is refused, and nothing is written through it. The git adapter's `Runner` gains a method that also hands the program its standard input (`execrunner.Runner` implements it, the same bounds and environment as `Output`); git's standard output is bounded as every other git answer of the review verbs (`reviewGitMaxOutput`, 256 MiB), so a tree whose blobs exceed it refuses the sweep (exit 2). A copy that cannot be made exactly stores nothing (exit 2). The analyzers run under exactly the environment they are given: a nil or empty one stays empty and is never the environment of the process. shellcheck runs as `shellcheck -f gcc -- <files>`, so a changed file whose name starts with `-` is a file, not an option. T3: `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` of the working tree when the verb runs, ignoring paths under `odd/review-results/` (assumption, open decision D6); T3 is a property of the tree at that moment, not of the commit, so it is stored as observed and never recomputed by `review check`. The stable directory holds a lock for the run (pinned in L55: the file `<repo key>/lock`, mode 0600, `flock` without waiting, taken once the binding and `review.CheckOneCommit` hold and before T1, given back when the run ends whatever happens; the `base` and `head` directories are emptied, never removed, so their paths stay); a second `review sweep` of the same repository while one runs exits 2 with `another review sweep of this repository is running` and does not wait. Output: the outcome, the stored path and its sha256, and when the sweep fired, the candidates of each trigger; exit 0 when stored (clean or fired), 2 when the binding or a check refused, 1 for a command line it does not understand. Pinned in L59: stdout is one JSON object `{"outcome":"clean"|"fired","path":"<stored path>","sha256":"<sum>","candidates":[...]}`, the candidates in the order of (c) and `[]` for a clean sweep, and stderr says `review sweep: stored <path> (sha256 <sum>)`; once the command line is read, every failure is exit 2 (a refused binding, an unsafe feature or revision, a state home that cannot be found, another sweep running, a check that did not run, git failing, an answer that could not be written after the record was stored, the error then naming the stored path), and only a command line it does not understand is exit 1. `review` with no verb and with an unknown verb names `sweep` among its verbs, exactly `error: review requires a verb: bind, record, verify, check, exempt, sweep` and `error: review: unknown verb "<verb>" (expected bind, record, verify, check, exempt or sweep)`; `stop-hook`, the verb of the installed hook, stays out of both, as T6a left it. Pinned in L63 (the points L61 and L62 left to the builder, confirmed as built in `fcf0b5d7` and `287d506b`): the Git port's `Mentions(repo, commit, names)` refuses, before git starts, a name that is empty or holds a line break, and refuses an answer that names a name not asked, a record without its NUL or LF, or a path without the `<commit>:` prefix; its map has a key only for a name something mentions, each path once per name, and no names is an empty map with no git start; with several patterns `-o` does not report a name that is a whole-word part of a longer asked name matched at the same place, which no two names of `review.RemovedNames` can be (each is `[A-Za-z_]\w*`), so it is a limit of the port, never reached by the sweep. The runner method is `OutputStdin(ctx, env, stdin []byte, bin, args...)` on `execrunner.Runner` and `gitexec.Runner`; `Output` hands no standard input, as before. The copy checks the path and the mode of every entry of the listing before `cat-file` starts, so such a tree writes nothing; only the refusal of a link below the copy runs while writing, since it depends on what was written; a tree with no blob (empty, or submodules only) starts no `cat-file`; bytes after the last answer are an error. The refusal texts are `the tree holds "<path>", a name that leads out of the directory`, `the tree holds "<path>" with the mode <mode>, which is neither a file, a link nor a submodule` and `<path>: <dir> is a link, and an entry of the tree would be written through it`. A file is created with 0644 or 0755, as the listing says, masked by the umask of the process and never chmod-ed afterwards. The bound of the batch stands (L60, L63): the blobs of a tree, each with its header line, are one answer of git held under `reviewGitMaxOutput` (256 MiB); a tree over it fails `Archive` with an error that wraps `execrunner.ErrOutputTooLarge` before any file is written, so `review sweep` exits 2 and stores nothing; the doc comment of `reviewGitMaxOutput` names that answer beside the diff.
  - (c) **The records.** Three new kinds, stored in `odd/review-results/<feature>/` atomically and never overwritten, numbered with the results, verdicts and exemptions of the head (`review.NextNumber`), each holding its binding (`base`, `head`, `diff_sha256`, and no `paths`: they follow from the digest, so `review.CheckRecordBinding` hands `review.CheckBinding` the candidate's own paths; L52) and held to git again by every reader (`review.CheckBinding`), as a result is: **sweep** `<head12>-<n>.sweep.json`, written only by `review sweep`: `{"kind":"sweep","base":"…","head":"…","diff_sha256":"…","outcome":"clean"|"fired","t1":{"deleted_or_renamed":["<path>"],"removed_names":[{"name":"…","hits":["<path>"]}]},"t2":[{"tool":"…","module":"<dir>","new":["<normalized line>"]}],"t3":["<porcelain line>"]}`, `fired` exactly when one of the lists is non-empty; `module` is the module's directory relative to the root (`.` for the root module), and `.` for `shellcheck`, which runs over the changed `.sh` files from the root (pinned in L55, so its candidate id is `T2 shellcheck .: <line>`); **sweeper** `<head12>-<n>.sweeper.json`, written by `labdrian review record --role sweeper --feature F --base B --head H` from the sweeper's report on stdin, `{"commit":"<full sha>"|null,"candidates":[{"candidate":"…","disposition":"removed"|"corrected"|"live"|"finding","evidence":"…"}]}`, stored with its binding and `"sweep":"<name of the fired sweep record>"`; the candidate ids of a sweep are exactly `T1 path: <path>`, `T1 name: <name>`, `T2 <tool> <module>: <line>` and `T3: <line>`, one per finding, once each, in that order (`review.StoredSweep.Candidates`, pinned in L52; `review sweep` prints them and the sweeper answers with them); it refuses when no fired sweep record binds the head, when a candidate of that record is missing, repeated or unknown, a disposition is outside the enum, an evidence is empty, `commit` is not null and is not a commit whose first parent is H and whose subject starts `chore(sweep)`, or `commit` is null while a disposition is `removed` or `corrected` (open decision D3); pinned in L59: when several fired sweep records bind the head, the sweeper record answers the latest by number and names it in `sweep`; a `commit` that is not null is first asked of git as an object (`git cat-file -e <commit>`, whose exit 1 with nothing printed means git does not have it, then `git cat-file -t <commit>`), and one that git does not have, or that is not a commit, is a refused report (exit 2, nothing stored), as one that fails `review.CheckSweepCommit` is; only git failing to answer is a plain error (exit 1); **polisher** `<head12>-<n>.polisher.json`, written by `labdrian review record --role polisher --feature F --base B --head H` from one judge's findings object on stdin (`roles/polisher/body.md`), stored as `{"kind":"polisher","base":"…","head":"…","diff_sha256":"…","findings":<the object, verbatim>}`; it refuses input that is not a JSON object, whose `findings` is missing or not an array of objects, whose `evidence` is missing, empty or not an array of non-empty strings, or that is over `review.MaxReplyBytes`; that bound holds the input only (L52): a reader holds the stored findings to the shape above and not to `review.MaxReplyBytes` (the stored file is indented like every stored file, so it can be larger than the input), and `review.EncodePolisher` refuses, with an error that wraps `review.ErrReplyTooLarge` and nothing stored, findings whose stored file would be over `review.MaxStoredBytes`, so a polisher record that is stored always reads back. Assumption: the polisher's finding schema is pinned nowhere beyond that line, so severities are not checked and no polisher finding blocks delivery in T6b (open decision D7). For both `--role` values B must be the first parent of H; a refused input exits 2 and stores nothing; the output is the stored path and its sha256, with no role-handoff skeleton (assumption: `roles append` gains no payload kind in T6b); pinned in L59: stdout is one JSON object `{"path":"<stored path>","sha256":"<sum>"}` and stderr says `review record: stored <path> (sha256 <sum>)`; the input is stdin or `--result <file>`, as for the reviewer; a refused input is exit 2 and any other failure after the command line is read is exit 1, the convention `review record` already has for the reviewer. `--role reviewer` is the default and is today's `review record` byte for byte; `--rows` with another role is a usage error (exit 1). Pinned in L63 (confirmed as built in `fcf0b5d7`): `IsCommit` answers an error for a name that is not a full object name (the report's parser already requires one, so `RecordSweeper` never meets it); a `tree`, `blob` or `tag` object is false (an annotated tag that points at a commit is not the commit), and any other answer of `git cat-file -t` is an error; the refusal of a `commit` that `IsCommit` says false is one text for missing and not a commit, `the commit <sha> is not a commit that git has`, wrapping `review.ErrInvalidSweeperReport`. Pinned in L66 (confirmed as built in `ec268723`): every reader holds a role record to one commit as well (`review.CheckOneCommit`: its base is the first parent of its head), as the writers do, besides its binding; a record that fails it does not hold.
  - (d) **What a commit needs.** One pure domain function (`review.Needs`) that the hook and `review check` both call, over the facts the app reads for a commit. A **passive** commit, whose trailer is `Risk: none`, whose every changed path is under `odd/tasks/`, `odd/review-results/` or `odd/review-receipts/` (open decision D4), and whose diff deletes or renames nothing, needs nothing else and covers its link of the chain without any record. A **sweep commit**, whose subject starts `chore(sweep)` and which a sweeper record of its first parent names, needs no sweep record of its own (open decision D5). Every other commit needs, in this order: a valid trailer (a); a sweep record (c), of which the latest by number counts (pinned in L59); when that record fired, a sweeper record whose `sweep` names it; when the trailer names an item, at least one polisher record (judgment-day's two judges make two; open decision D7); and a stored result or exemption that covers it in the chain (S5), where an exemption also needs the trailer `Risk: none`. Pinned in L66 (confirmed as built in `64e595bd`): a sweep commit that has a sweep record of its own is held to it as any commit is, so when its latest sweep record fired it needs a sweeper record that answers it; a sweep commit whose trailer fails needs its trailer and nothing else; any commit whose trailer fails is listed with its trailer and, when it has no sweep record and is not a sweep commit, its sweep, and nothing more. `review.RiskValue(items []int) string` writes the items of a trailer as the trailer writes them (joined by `,` with no space, for example `3,5`, in the order given; `none` for no item); since `review.ParseRisk` keeps the order written and the grammar of (a) admits one spelling of each list, `review.RiskValue` of what `review.ParseRisk` reads from `v` is `v` for every valid `v`, and it is the one writer of `<items>` in the texts of (e) and (f).
  - (e) **The hook (changes to T6a).** The walk is the same first-parent commits; the commit asked is now the first that misses anything of (d), so a reviewed commit that misses a role record is asked again (T6a never asked a reviewed commit again), and a passive commit is never asked (closing the known limit of L35). The base is the commit before it in the chain, as in T6a. Once per session, candidate and need (pinned in L70, replacing the single key `<base12>-<head12>` of L63 and L66): the hook keeps one marker for each need it asks, keyed `<base12>-<head12>-<need>` with `<need>` one of `risk`, `sweep`, `sweeper`, `polisher`, `review`; a stop is blocked when at least one need of the commit asked has no marker in the session, and its block lists everything the commit misses, the needs already asked included; a commit amended to add its trailer is a new candidate. Silent cases, exit 0 always, the input bound and the panic recovery stay as in T6a. The block reason is exactly `Commit <head> of branch <branch> has unfinished review steps. Before you stop, for that commit alone (base <base>):` (its first sentence changed by S17 (d), L83) followed by a blank line and, in this order, the lines that apply: `- Risk: <cause of (a)>. End the commit message with a final trailer block that holds the one line "Risk: none" or "Risk: <items>" (for example "Risk: 3,5"); when the commit is HEAD: git commit --amend --trailer "Risk: <none|items>". The rest of what the commit needs is asked once it declares its risk.`; `- Sweep: labdrian review sweep --feature <feature> --base <base> --head <head>`; `- Sweeper: the sweep record <path> fired; launch the sweeper with its candidates, then: labdrian review record --role sweeper --feature <feature> --base <base> --head <head>`; `- Polisher: the commit declares Risk: <items>; launch the polisher judges on the commit and record each: labdrian review record --role polisher --feature <feature> --base <base> --head <head>`; `- Review: labdrian review bind --base <base> --head <head>, then 'labdrian review record' the reply of the reviewer and 'labdrian review verify' it` with, when the trailer is `Risk: none`, `; or, since the commit declares Risk: none, 'labdrian review exempt --risk none' it`; then a blank line and `<feature> is the feature whose odd/tasks/<feature>.md holds the task of the commit. A record counts once it is stored and binds the commit, whatever its outcome; 'labdrian review check' judges the outcome. The stop hook asks for each thing a commit misses once for each session and commit.` (that last sentence pinned in L70) When the trailer fails, only the Risk and Sweep lines are listed. The no-default-branch question is unchanged. Pinned in L63 (for T6b6): the hook's output stays T6a's (the same decision object, exit 0 always) and only its reason text changes, to the one above; in it `<cause of (a)>` is the cause of the failing trailer exactly as (a) words it (the text after `commit <sha>: `), `<path>` the repository-relative path of the latest sweep record of the commit, `<items>` the trailer's value as written (for example `3,5`), `<feature>` the literal text `<feature>` (the hook reads the records of every feature of the tree, as T6a reads results), and `<base>` and `<head>` as T6a prints them. A role record that does not read back or does not hold is not counted, so the hook asks for it again, as T6a skips such a result; `review check` refuses it (f). Pinned in L66 (for T6b6a-T6b6c): the facts of a commit are its message, trailers, first parent and, for a passive candidate, its paths, read through the Git port once, and its role records, read from every feature of the tree in the order `Results.Features` gives: its sweep is the latest sweep record that holds of the first feature that has one, and only a sweeper record of that feature can answer it; it is a sweep commit when a sweeper record of its first parent, of any feature, names it; it is polished when a polisher record of any feature binds it; a role record that does not read back or does not hold, whatever the reason, counts as absent and is no error. The commits of the branch are read oldest first; a passive commit is a link from its first parent beside the links of the stored results and exemptions (T6a's `links`, unchanged); `review.FirstUnreviewed` over those links gives the first commit no chain reaches; walking oldest first up to that commit, the commit asked is the first whose `review.Needs` is not empty, where only that first unreached commit asks for its review (`AskCoverage` true, `Coverage` `review.ErrNoResult`); commits after it are not read for the question. `<base>` is the first parent of the commit asked. With no such commit nothing is asked. A failure of git or of the store listing is an error and no block, as in T6a. The reason is exactly the header line, a blank line, the lines of (e) joined by one line feed in the order `review.Needs` lists them (one line per need: Risk, Sweep, Sweeper, Polisher, Review), a blank line and the closing paragraph; `<cause of (a)>` is the text of the need's error after the first `commit <head>: ` (the whole text when it holds none), and the `; or, since the commit declares Risk: none, ...` clause is added exactly when the trailer holds and `review.RiskValue` of it is `none`. Pinned in L70 (for T6b6d): `Stop` keys each need of `Question.Needs` as `<base12>-<head12>-<need>` in the order `review.Needs` lists them, asks the store of the questions `Has` for every key in that order, and when every key is there does not block; otherwise it calls `Add` for every key that was not there, in the same order, and blocks with the whole question; a failure of `Has` or `Add` is an error and no block, as in T6a (a key added before a failing `Add` stays, so the next stop asks for the rest); a `review.Missing` other than those five (none today) is keyed `need<n>` with `<n>` its number, so the naming never fails; the no-default-branch question keeps its one key `no-default-branch`. A need can appear after an earlier block of the same commit (a sweep that fires asks for its sweeper), and that new need blocks the stop once in the same session; a need already asked is not asked again while the commit misses it.
  - (f) **`review check` refuses.** With `--base`, for every first-parent commit of `base..head` (per commit, because one result can cover several commits), and without it for the head alone, whatever (d) says the commit misses, naming the commit and the missing item: a trailer that fails (open decision D2: refuse rather than read as high risk), no sweep record, a fired sweep record with no sweeper record, a declared item with no polisher record, a record that does not read back or does not hold. A passive commit is a link of the unbroken chain from its first parent with no record. Every refusal of today stays. `review exempt` refuses, before anything is stored, a head whose trailer fails or names an item (open decision D8); its other refusals and outputs stay. Pinned in L63 (outputs and error classes for T6b5): `review check` keeps its convention: cleared is exit 0 with today's JSON on stdout, and every refusal, the new ones included, is exit 1 with `error: review check: <reason>` on stderr and nothing on stdout; no refusal of `review check` is exit 2. Its stdout gains no member: a passive commit is the range `{"base":"<its first parent>","head":"<the commit>","results":[]}` with no `exemptions`, and a passive head checked without `--base` prints `{"head":"<the head>","outcome":"clear","results":[]}`; the role records of a commit are judged and not printed. `review.Needs` lists what a commit misses in the order of (d) (the hook prints the list); `review check` refuses with the first item, so a commit that misses only its review is refused with today's text byte for byte. The walk is today's, from the head back: at a commit that ends a range, Needs with the coverage of that range (today's `cleared`); at each commit inside a range, from the range's head back to its base, Needs without the coverage. A passive commit needs no record, but a stored result or exemption that binds it is still read and held as today, so one that blocks, is not clear or does not hold still refuses it. The reasons, each after `the chain from <base> to <head>: ` under `--base`, as today: a trailer that fails, the error of (a) unchanged (it wraps `review.ErrRiskTrailer` and names the commit); `commit <sha>: review: no sweep record binds the commit` (`review.ErrNoSweep`); `commit <sha>: review: no sweeper record answers the fired sweep record: <path>` (`review.ErrNoSweeper`, `<path>` the latest sweep record, repository-relative); `commit <sha>: review: no polisher record binds a commit that declares a high-risk item: Risk: <items>` (`review.ErrNoPolisher`); an exemption of a commit whose trailer names an item, `<exemption path>: commit <sha>: ` then the error `review.CheckRisk` gives for the trailer's value (it wraps `review.ErrHighRisk`), which refuses the commit even when a result also binds it, as an exemption that does not hold does today; a role record of the commit that does not read back or does not hold, `<path>: <reason>`, or `read <path>: <reason>` when it cannot be read, as a stored result today; and the coverage with today's texts (`head <sha>: ` then the error that wraps `review.ErrNoResult`, `ErrBlocked` or `ErrNotClear`, and the gap and loop refusals of the chain). The three new sentinels sit in the domain package beside `ErrNoResult`, and each reason wraps exactly one sentinel. A sweep, sweeper or polisher file whose name is of the commit but whose stored head is another commit is not the commit's, as a result's is today. `review exempt` (D8): after the binding and `oneCommit` and before anything is stored, the head's message and trailers are read through the Git port; a trailer that fails is refused with the error of (a), one that names an item with `commit <sha>: ` then `review.CheckRisk`'s error for its value (it wraps `review.ErrHighRisk`); both are plain errors, exit 1 with `error: review exempt: <reason>`, as a `--risk` that names an item is today, and nothing is stored; every other message, exit code and the stdout skeleton stay byte for byte. Pinned in L66: a commit whose trailer fails is refused with the error of (a) even when a role record of it, or a sweeper record of its first parent, does not read back or does not hold: while the trailer fails such a record counts as absent (its error is dropped, never turned into a pass, since `review.Needs` lists the failing trailer first), and the record's own refusal (`<path>: <reason>` or `read <path>: <reason>`) is met once the trailer holds. A commit with no parent whose trailer is `Risk: none` is refused with `the commit <sha> has no parent, so what it changes cannot be told` (exit 1); with any other trailer a parentless commit is refused by the first thing it misses (as built in `ec268723`). The help of `review check` and `review exempt` (`engine review` usage) states these rules with the sentences of L66, in T6b5a.
  - (g) **The role files learn the trailer.** `roles/builder/body.md` gains one line: end every commit message with a final trailer block holding exactly one line `Risk: none` or `Risk: <items>` in the grammar of (a), the items of the high-risk list the commit touches. `roles/sweeper/body.md`: commit the sweep as `chore(sweep)` with its own `Risk:` trailer, and return the report of (c) as the sweeper record's JSON. `roles/polisher/body.md` is unchanged (the parent records each judge). `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md`, Triggers: the sweeper's line names `labdrian review sweep` as the check of T1-T3 and the two `review record --role` records; the human re-approves the skill. The role files of the three runtimes are regenerated (`gadu-generate`).
  - (h) **Limits.** The risk is declared by the agent, not computed (S5). T3 is what the tree held when the sweep ran. The hook is Claude Code only; `review check` is the gate for every runtime. `review sweep` needs `deadcode`, `staticcheck` and `shellcheck` on `PATH` and a toolchain they run under (L35: both Go tools failed under Go 1.27.3 and ran under go1.26.1); until then it refuses, and delivery with it. Pinned in L63: a tree whose blobs exceed `reviewGitMaxOutput` (256 MiB) in one `git cat-file --batch` answer cannot be swept (b), so delivery of such a repository stops until the copy streams; accepted in L60 and L63.
- **S11 — Delivery chooses the branch base.** Owner (L18): "puede ser main o puede ser una tracker o una rama dev esto deberia de dise;arlo el delivery deacuerdo a la naturaleza del proyecto". The delivery role chooses the integration base (main, a tracker branch or a dev branch) to suit the project and passes it to `review check`. Built in T4 (L20): `roles/delivery/body.md` passes `--base` and tells the role to pass the merge base itself when the base moved on after the branch was made. Design of the rest (shaper, L109; open decision D18 decides whether the engine computes the start of the chain at all, D20 whether `--onto` must name a branch); acceptance criteria:
  - (a) **`review check --onto <rev>`.** `review check` takes `--onto <rev>`, the integration branch the delivery chose, as a revision, and the engine computes where the chain starts: the merge bases of `<rev>` and the head through the Git port's `MergeBases` (`engine/review/app/app.go:90`), held to exactly one by `review.OneMergeBase` (`engine/review/landing.go:49`, reused, not re-implemented), then the chain from that commit exactly as `--base <that commit>` checks it (`Service.CheckChain`, `engine/review/app/chain.go:37`, not re-implemented). So `--onto main` is `--base main` while `main` has not moved, and `--base <the commit the branch was made from>` once it has, and the role no longer computes it. stdout is byte for byte what `--base <merge base>` prints (`reviewChainJSON`, no new member: `base` is the merge base). Refusals, each exit 1 with `error: review check: <reason>` and nothing on stdout, as every refusal of `review check` (S6 f): `--onto` with `--base`, `--base and --onto cannot be given together`, before git is asked anything; `--onto` twice, `--onto given twice` (the parser's text); a revision that is not one, the error of `review.CheckRev`; none or several merge bases, `<onto> and <head> do not have exactly one merge base (they have <n>), so the commits the pull request adds cannot be told` (the text of `review.OneMergeBase`, wrapping `review.ErrMergeBase`, both full object names); git failing, `find the merge base of <onto> and <head>: <cause>`; and every refusal of the chain (S5, S6 f) unchanged. Nothing is stored. Without `--onto` every output, message and exit code of `review check` stays byte for byte, and no other review verb takes `--onto` (it stays `unknown flag "--onto"` there).
  - (b) **Docs and the role.** The usage line becomes `engine review check --feature <name> [--head <rev>] [--base <rev> | --onto <rev>] [--cwd <repo>]` with one sentence for `--onto`; `bin/lib/usage.sh` `check --feature NAME [--head REV] [--base REV | --onto REV] [--cwd REPO]` with one clause; README the same. `roles/delivery/body.md` tells the role to run `review check` with `--onto <rev>`, the integration branch of the delivery (main, a tracker branch or a dev branch: the prompt names it, and when it names none choose the one that suits the project), so that the engine starts the chain where the branch was made; the parenthetical on computing the merge base is removed. The three agent files are regenerated from it.
  - (c) **Limit.** `--onto` is still a revision the agent names: a commit of the branch itself given as `--onto` shortens the chain as a wrong `--base` does (D20). What `--onto` removes is the step the role did by hand.
- **S12 — Reviewer optional for exempted work.** Accepted in L19: a head closed by an exemption hands off without a `reviewer` record that never ran; today the exemption record goes `reviewer → delivery` because `engine/roles` has no `builder → delivery` transition. Design (shaper, L109; open decisions D19, D21 and D22 marked where they apply); acceptance criteria:
  - (a) **The transition.** `RoleHandoff.Validate` (`checkTransition`, `engine/roles/handoff_validate.go:57`) also accepts a record from `builder`, `sweeper` or `polisher` to `delivery` exactly when its `payload_kind` is `review-exemption` and its `status` is `completed` (assumption, D22: an interrupted exemption skips nothing). The rule is one pure function of package `roles`, `SkipsReviewer(h RoleHandoff) bool` (the name is an assumption): true exactly when `h.ToRole` is `delivery`, `IsValidTransition(h.FromRole, RoleReviewer)` holds (which is true for `builder`, `sweeper` and `polisher` and for no other role, `engine/roles/roles.go:132-152`), `h.PayloadKind` is `roles.PayloadExemption` and `h.Status` is `completed`. `IsValidTransition` and `NextRoles` are unchanged, so every caller of them answers as today (known limit: `NextRoles` knows no payload and does not list `delivery` after `builder`). `roles.PayloadExemption = "review-exemption"` is the one spelling of the kind, and `review.PayloadExemption` (`engine/review/review.go:106`) is defined as it (`review` imports `roles`, never the reverse). Every other skip of the reviewer is refused with today's text `transition from "<from>" to "<to>" is not allowed`; `reviewer → delivery` stays valid for every kind, so records already stored still parse.
  - (b) **The skeleton.** `review.ExemptionHandoff` (`engine/review/handoff.go:71`) leaves `from_role` empty (assumption, D19): the parent fills the role the chain is at (`builder`, `sweeper` or `polisher`), which `roles.VerifyChain` holds to the previous record's `to_role` (`engine/roles/chain.go:59`); a record appended with it left empty is refused with `from_role "" is not a known role` (`handoff_validate.go:58-60`). `review exempt` names it first among what the parent fills, its last stderr line becoming exactly `review exempt: stdout is the skeleton of the record for 'roles append --stdin'; the parent of the chain fills from_role, project_id, goal_id, chain_id, seq, prev_sha256, context.summary`; the lines of `review record` and `review verify` stay byte for byte. Every other member of the skeleton, the stored file, the exit codes and the evidence check of `roles append` (`engine/review/app/evidence.go:116`) stay. The help of `review exempt` (`engine/cmd/usage.go:207`) and README (`:711`) say `from_role left to the parent (the role the chain is at: builder, sweeper or polisher)` where they say `from_role reviewer`. `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` is unchanged (assumption, D21).
- **S13 — The lens count is checked, not trusted.** Owner (L29), to closing advisory A1 of L25 with option B, the actuals validator comparing against the stored rows: "B ahora". `tools/actuals-record-validator` refuses an actuals record whose `review_lens_count` differs from the number of rows recorded in the stored review results of the change (one per row of each launch, as S8 defines it), and names both numbers. The count is computed by one engine function that both the validator and any other reader call; the validator does not re-implement it. Assumption: the change's results are `odd/review-results/<change_name>/` of the repository; a record of a change reviewed before S8 (gentle receipts, no stored results) needs an explicit outcome the builder proposes, never a silent pass (S1).
- **S14 — The builder never designs.** Owner (L37): "pero es medio choto que el builder este dise;ando", "esto hay que ajustarlo definitivamoente", and to "¿Cómo lo dejamos fijo para que el builder nunca diseñe?" the answer "Guard mecánico (Recomendado)": a PreToolUse hook on the Agent tool denies launching `builder` when its prompt does not cite a task (`odd/tasks/<f>.md`, `T#`) that the shaper already marked as designed in the feature document; the deny message says to launch the shaper first. Installed, reported and removed like the other hook families. The form of the shaper's mark is for the shaper to propose. Design (shaper, L38); acceptance criteria:
  - (a) **The mark.** A task line designed by the shaper carries exactly one segment `shaped: L<n>` (the line's segments are split by ` — `; by convention the segment sits just before `commit:`), where `L<n>` (n a positive integer, no leading zero) names the Log entry in which the shaper recorded that design. The mark holds only when the `## Log` section of the same document has exactly one entry whose line starts `- **L<n>** (<YYYY-MM-DD>, shaper)`. Only the shaper writes the mark (its role file and `skills/odd-feature-doc/SKILL.md` say so); the builder and delivery never write it. Example: `- [ ] T11a — S14 (b-d) — delegated — ... — shaped: L38 — commit: -`.
  - (b) **The citation.** The hook reads `subagent_type` and `prompt` of the Agent tool input (through `hookwire.DecodeAgentCall`) and the session `cwd`. The prompt cites tasks with exactly one line that, after leading white space and a trailing `\r` are dropped, starts `Spec: ` and reads `Spec: <path>, <item>[, <item>...]` (the form the ODD protocol already prescribes, "Spec: odd/tasks/<feature>.md, T2, S3-S4"). `<path>` is absolute, or relative to `cwd`, and after cleaning ends in `odd/tasks/<f>.md` with `<f>` matching `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*`. Each `<item>` is a task id or a spec reference `S<digits>` / `S<digits>-S<digits>`; at least one task id is required; any other item makes the line not a citation. Every cited task must pass (c); the first that fails is named. **Task id grammar** (L46, owner, to "¿Cómo resolvemos que el guard rechace los IDs T6b1..T6b7?": "Ampliar el guard (Recomendado)"): a task id is `T<n>` followed by lowercase letters and digits, `^T[0-9]+[a-z0-9]*$`, one grammar for the citation items and for the id of a task line in (c) (it replaces `T<digits>[a-z]?`, `engine/oddtask/oddtask.go:36` and `:105`; designed in L47). Acceptance: `T6b1`, `T11a`, `T11` and `T1ab` are task ids; `T6B1`, `T6-b`, `t6`, `T`, `Tb1` are not, and a citation holding one is not a citation; a task is matched by its whole id, never by a prefix: citing `T6b` does not find the line `- [ ] T6b1 — `, and citing `T6b1` does not find the line `- [ ] T6b — `.
  - (c) **The lookup.** The document is read as a regular file whose last path component is not a symbolic link, at most 8 MiB. A cited task is the one line of the `## Tasks` section (from the line `## Tasks` to the next line starting `## `) that starts `- [ ] <id> — ` or `- [x] <id> — `; none or more than one is a failure. The task passes when its line carries the mark of (a) and the mark holds against `## Log`.
  - (d) **The deny.** Stdout is exactly one JSON object `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"deny","permissionDecisionReason":"<message>"}}` (`hookwire.PreToolUseReply`), exit 0; an allow prints nothing and never `permissionDecision` "allow". The message is exactly `labdrian builder guard: <reason>. The builder never designs: launch the shaper first to design the task and mark its line "shaped: L<n>", then launch the builder with the line "Spec: odd/tasks/<feature>.md, T<n>" in its prompt. This guard reads text only, so it is a speed bump, not a security boundary.`, where `<reason>` is one of: `the prompt cites no task (it has no line "Spec: odd/tasks/<feature>.md, T<n>")`; `the prompt has more than one "Spec:" line`; `the "Spec:" line does not name a feature document odd/tasks/<feature>.md and at least one task T<n>`; `the feature document <path> cannot be read (<cause>)` (not found, not a regular file, a link, over 8 MiB, or a relative path with no `cwd`); `task <T> is not in the ## Tasks section of <path>`; `task <T> appears more than once in the ## Tasks section of <path>`; `task <T> of <path> is not marked as designed by the shaper (its line has no "shaped: L<n>")`; `task <T> of <path> is marked "shaped: L<n>", but the ## Log of the document has no entry L<n> written by the shaper`; `the Agent call is larger than 4194304 bytes and could not be checked`; `the guard hit an internal error and could not check this launch`. `<path>` is the resolved absolute path.
  - (e) **Fail-closed for the builder, open otherwise.** Once the call is decoded and `subagent_type` is `builder`, every failure denies (S1: a check that cannot run refuses, never passes), a recovered panic included; this costs at most a builder launch with a message that says what to do. Input it cannot decode, or a failing stdin, cannot be told to be a builder launch and is allowed, with one line on stderr: Claude Code sends valid JSON, and denying it would block every Agent call. Input over `hookwire.MaxAgentCallBytes` (4 MiB) is denied whatever the agent, because the agent controls the length of its own prompt (the approve guard's rule; open decision D3 of L38). A missing engine binary does nothing and `status-hooks` reports it (open decision D2 of L38).
  - (f) **Other agents.** A call whose `subagent_type` is not exactly `builder` is allowed untouched: no output, no file read, exit 0.
  - (g) **Install, report, remove.** A settings family `builderGuardFamily`, identity and verb `roles builder-guard`, one entry `PreToolUse` with matcher `Agent`, command `command -v <bin> >/dev/null 2>&1 && <bin> roles builder-guard || true`, appended last to `installedFamilies`; `uninstall-hooks` removes exactly that entry and leaves the minimalism and design entries on the same matcher, and foreign entries, byte for byte. `HasSupportedClaudeLifecycleState` requires it, so `runtime status --target claude` reads `partial` until `install-hooks` is re-run. `status-hooks` gains the check `hook: PreToolUse matcher="Agent" (builder guard)` after `hook: Stop (review stop-hook)`: OK when the entry is exactly what install writes, WARN naming the missing or drifted parts with the `uninstall-hooks` then `install-hooks` remedy and the restart note, FAIL when `settings.json` cannot be read.
  - (h) **The shaper marks.** `roles/shaper/body.md` gains one line: mark each task line you designed with the segment `shaped: L<n>` before `commit:`, where `L<n>` is the Log entry `(<date>, shaper)` you append with that design, and never mark a task you did not design; the three role files are regenerated (`gadu-generate`). `skills/odd-feature-doc/SKILL.md` adds the segment to the task-line format, gives it to the shaper in the Decision Gates table and forbids it to builder and delivery, and states that reopening a task removes its mark (open decision D4 of L38); the human re-approves the skill.
  - (i) **Limits.** Claude Code only (Codex and Pi launches are not guarded; open decision D5 of L38). The mark can be forged by any writer of the document together with a false shaper Log entry; the guard stops a forgotten shaper, not a deliberate bypass (open decision D6 of L38).
- **S15 — The sweeper keeps the project clean, not only the code.** Owner (L44), to the parent's list of what else the sweeper could do while staying removal-only (1 leftovers of work: `.orig`, `.bak`, scratch scripts, stale review and spike worktrees; 2 dead dependencies, what `go mod tidy` removes; 3 orphaned `testdata/` fixtures and goldens no test reads; 4 docs that name things that no longer exist: removed flags, verbs or functions still in README, help or CHANGELOG; 5 merged branches, proposed only, the owner approves): "bien me gusta ese punto de vista para mantener no soolo el codigo limpio sino tambien el proyecto simpre legible siempre limpio". The sweeper stays removal-only; it runs no tests (verifier) and judges no style (polisher). Design is the shaper's. Design (shaper, L127; open decisions D31-D37 of L127 marked where they apply; every assumption marked); acceptance criteria:
  - (a) **Where each duty runs.** Duties 1 (the files), 2, 3 and 4 are checks of `labdrian review sweep` (S6 b), run once per commit like T1-T3, so a commit whose sweep finds one fires and needs its sweeper record (S6 d), and nothing in `review check`, the stop hook or `review anchor` changes. Each finds only what the commit itself adds, never what its base already had: debt that is older than the commit is a builder unit of its own, never a sweep (`docs/architecture/role-design/per-role-sweeper.md:41`). Duty 1 (the worktrees) and duty 5 are not properties of a commit: they are the proposals of (g), which store nothing, fire nothing and delete nothing. The sweeper stays removal-only: it deletes or corrects what a candidate names, or answers `live` or `finding` (S6 c).
  - (b) **Duty 1, leftover files (T4).** A path the commit changes (the paths of the binding) whose base name ends `.orig` or `.bak` and that the commit does not delete is a leftover (assumption, D32: the two suffixes the owner named; the scratch scripts of duty 1 have no mark a check can read, and an untracked one is already a T3 line). Domain: `review.Leftovers(paths, deleted []string) []string`, sorted, each once.
  - (c) **Duty 3, orphaned fixtures (T4).** The packages of a commit are the directories of its changed `.go` paths and, for a changed path with a `testdata` element, the directory above the first `testdata` (`.` at the root). A fixture of a package `p` is a file of the tree under `p/testdata/`; its names are its base name and the name of each directory between `testdata` and it. A fixture is orphaned at a commit when no `.go` file directly in `p` at that commit mentions any of its names as a whole word (`Mentions`, the T1 search). T4 lists the fixtures orphaned at the head that are not fixtures orphaned at the base, sorted (assumption, D36: a fixture read through a glob or from another package is a false positive the sweeper answers `live` with the test that reads it).
  - (d) **Duty 4, docs that name what no longer exists (T1).** T1 already finds a removed Go or shell name wherever the head still mentions it outside the diff, README, help and CHANGELOG included. It also finds removed flags: a flag is `--` followed by a lowercase letter and then lowercase letters, digits and single hyphens, not ending with a hyphen, with no letter, digit, `_` or `-` right before or after it; a removed flag is one held by a removed line of a `.go` or `.sh` file that no added line of a `.go` or `.sh` file holds (`review.RemovedFlags`). Its hits are searched as the names' are, in a second `Mentions` call, and it is listed among `removed_names` with its `--`, so its candidate id is `T1 name: --<flag>`. Removed verbs and other strings are not searched (D31). A CHANGELOG line that records the removal is a hit the sweeper answers `live`.
  - (e) **Duty 2, dead dependencies (T2).** For each Go module of T2 (S6 b) whose `go.mod` or `go.sum` the commit changes, or one of whose `.go` files loses a line that is an import spec of a path whose first element holds a dot (`review.RemovesImport`; assumption, D33), `go mod tidy -diff` runs in the module on the copy of the base and on the copy of the head, under the environment the analyzers are given; its finding lines are `go.mod: require <module path> <version>`, one for each line of the `go.mod` part of its diff that removes a required module (a `require` line, or an entry of a `require (` block, its `// indirect` comment dropped); the new ones (`review.NewFindings`) are the T2 entry `{"tool":"go-mod-tidy","module":"<dir>","new":[...]}`, candidate `T2 go-mod-tidy <dir>: go.mod: require <path> <version>`. Lines that add a requirement, and the `go.sum` part, are not findings (removal-only). It ran when it exited 0, or exited non-zero having printed a unified diff (a line starting `--- `); not found, stopped at its deadline, or any other exit is a check that did not run (S1): the sweep exits 2 naming `go mod tidy` and stores nothing (D33). The exit status of `go mod tidy -diff` is unverified (L30 saw only that it exists and that it fails offline when the module cache lacks a module); the builder pins it in a test and records one real run.
  - (f) **The record.** A sweep record gains one member after `t3`: `"t4":{"leftovers":["<path>"],"orphaned_fixtures":["<path>"]}`, both lists always written; the outcome is `fired` exactly when any list of t1-t4 is not empty. A stored sweep without `t4` (every record stored before T12b) reads as one with both lists empty; a `t4` that is present must hold both lists, arrays and never null, of non-empty paths (D37). The candidate ids gain, after those of T3, `T4 leftover: <path>` then `T4 fixture: <path>` (`review.StoredSweep.Candidates`), so every id of a sweep stored before keeps its text and its place. The stdout of `review sweep` is unchanged in shape.
  - (g) **Duties 1 (worktrees) and 5: proposals.** `labdrian review proposals [--cwd <repo>]` (engine `review proposals`) prints, and never runs, what the owner may remove: the worktrees of the repository other than the main one and the one of `--cwd`, not locked, that git marks prunable or whose HEAD is an ancestor of the tip of the default branch (the review worktrees `review bind` made and spike worktrees whose work merged), then the local branches other than the default one and the one checked out at `--cwd` that are merged into the default branch. stdout is one JSON object `{"default":"<branch>","worktrees":[{"path":"<abs path>","head":"<sha>","reason":"merged"|"prunable","command":["git","worktree","remove","<path>"]}],"branches":[{"name":"<branch>","command":["git","branch","-d","<branch>"]}]}`, a prunable worktree's command being `["git","worktree","prune"]`; the commands are git's own refusing forms (`git worktree remove` refuses a dirty or locked worktree, `git branch -d` an unmerged branch), never `--force` or `-D`; exit 0. A repository with no default branch is refused, exit 1: `the repository has no default branch (origin/HEAD, main or master), so nothing can be told merged`; a command line it does not understand, or git failing, is exit 1. Nothing is stored and nothing is deleted; the parent relays each proposal to the owner and runs a proposed command only after the owner confirms it (D35). Remote branches are not proposed. `review` with no verb and with an unknown verb become exactly `error: review requires a verb: bind, record, verify, check, exempt, sweep, anchor, launch, proposals` and `error: review: unknown verb "<verb>" (expected bind, record, verify, check, exempt, sweep, anchor, launch or proposals)`; `stop-hook` stays out of both.
  - (h) **Roles, skill and docs.** `roles/sweeper/body.md` line 8 names the new candidates and lets the sweeper delete a fixture no test reads (its delta stays four lines, `per-role-sweeper.md:3`); `skills/sweeper/SKILL.md` names T1-T4 and the proposals step (the human re-approves it); the three sweeper agent files are regenerated (`gadu-generate`). Each subtask updates the engine usage, `bin/lib/usage.sh`, `README.md` and one clause of one Unreleased `CHANGELOG.md` entry for what it builds.
  - (i) **Limits.** The checks find what a commit adds, never older debt. T4 reads the suffixes of (b) only; T3 still lists no ignored file (`.gitignore` ignores `*.swp` and `*~`). A fixture mentioned by its name in any `.go` file of its package is not orphaned, even when no test reads it. A tidy that cannot run (an offline machine whose module cache lacks a module, L30) stops the sweep of a commit that touches a module's dependencies, and its delivery, until the cache is filled (D33). A branch merged by squash or rebase is not proposed. A binary older than T12b refuses a sweep record that holds `t4` (an unknown member), so the installed `labdrian` is upgraded with the merge (D37).
- **S7 — A feature's pull request lands a reviewed tree.** Owner (L85), relayed by the parent after it explained S7 and reported that no openspec archive report has been written since 2026-09-21 and that ODD features write none: "claro es por ahi, y tenemos que adaptar s7 a odd como documentacion o spec de la campaña  en lugar de usar openscpec". The approved-tree check works for ODD: the feature document `odd/tasks/<feature>.md`, the campaign's spec, names the feature, and the stored reviews under `odd/review-results/<feature>/` are the source of the approved tree, in place of an openspec archive report (the chain adapter that `docs/architecture/adr-c13-inception-role-chain.md:50` left as a follow-up). Design (shaper, L85, replacing the design of L83, which read our results inside the openspec branch of `tools/archive-anchor-gate`); the open decisions D1-D5 of L85 are marked where they apply, and the assumptions are marked; acceptance:
  - (a) **When it runs.** A new CI job runs the engine verb of (b) as `review anchor --merge HEAD` on every `pull_request` event, where the checkout's HEAD is the merge GitHub prepares of the pull request into its base (assumption, unverified in this repository: `actions/checkout` checks out `refs/pull/<n>/merge`, whose first parent is the base branch's tip and whose second is the pull request's head), and on every `push` to `main`, where HEAD is the merge that landed (the repository merges with merge commits, for example `e0cfc085`, PR #695). Pushes to other branches do not run it. It holds the one merge HEAD names and never walks the history.
  - (b) **The verb, the merge, the head and the base.** `engine review anchor --merge <rev> [--cwd <repo>]` decides whether the merge `<rev>` lands a tree that stored reviews cleared. M is the full name of `<rev>`. M must have exactly two parents, the first P (the base) and the second H (the head of the pull request); otherwise it refuses with `the commit <M> is not the merge of a pull request (the number of its parents is <n>, not 2), so what it lands cannot be told to be reviewed` (D2: a squash or rebase landing, or a direct push to `main`, is refused, never passed, per S1). B is the one merge base of P and H (`git merge-base --all P H`); none or several refuse with `<P> and <H> do not have exactly one merge base (they have <n>), so the commits the pull request adds cannot be told`.
  - (c) **The features.** The features of the merge are the names `<f>` of the paths `odd/tasks/<f>.md` (directly under `odd/tasks/`, ending `.md`) among the paths of `B..H` (the Git port's `Paths`, the paths of a binding), in that order, each once; a name `review.CheckFeature` refuses is refused with `odd/tasks/<f>.md: ` then the error of `review.CheckFeature`. A merge whose `B..H` changes no feature document has no feature: its outcome is `no-feature` and it passes (assumption, D3: the pull request of small ODD work, which tracks no feature document and stores no review).
  - (d) **What clears it.** For each feature, in order, `reviewapp.Service.CheckChain(<repo>, <f>, B, H)` (`engine/review/app/chain.go:37`), the use case `review check --feature <f> --base B --head H` runs; nothing of it is re-implemented. The commits of the merge are reviewed when at least one feature's chain clears: a chain that clears covers every commit of `B..H`, whatever feature the other documents name. Every feature is checked, also after one clears, so the output names each. When none clears it refuses with `no feature document the merge changes clears <B>..<H>: <f>: <reason>` and, for each further feature, `; <g>: <reason>`, `<reason>` the text of the error `CheckChain` returned. A chain cleared only by passive commits or exemptions clears (assumption: unlike L83 (c), the question is whether anything unreviewed lands, not whether a reviewer saw a tree).
  - (e) **The tree.** Once a chain clears, the tree of M (`<M>^{tree}`) must equal the tree of H: equal is the outcome `verified`; different refuses with `the merge <M> lands the tree <tree of M>, not the tree <tree of H> of the reviewed head <H>: the branch did not hold <P> when it was merged` (D1: refused, not disclosed, since an ODD feature has no report to disclose it in).
  - (f) **Output.** Cleared (`verified` or `no-feature`): exit 0, nothing on stderr, and on stdout one JSON object encoded as `review check` encodes its own, its members in this order: `merge`, `base`, `head`, `tree` (only for `verified`: the tree of H), `outcome`, `features` (for each feature in order `{"feature":"<f>","outcome":"clear"}` or `{"feature":"<f>","outcome":"refused","reason":"<reason>"}`; `[]` for `no-feature`). Every refusal, and any failure of git or of the store, is exit 1 with `error: review anchor: <reason>` on stderr and nothing on stdout, the convention of `review check`; a command line it does not understand is exit 1 with a usage error. Nothing is stored.
  - (g) **The shape.** The pure decisions sit in the domain package `engine/review`, each refusal wrapping one new sentinel: `review.MergeHead(merge string, parents []string) (first, head string, err error)` (`review.ErrNotAMerge`), `review.OneMergeBase(first, head string, bases []string) (string, error)` (`review.ErrMergeBase`), `review.FeaturesOf(paths []string) ([]string, error)` (a refused name wraps the error of `review.CheckFeature`), `review.NoFeatureClears(base, head string, features []string, reasons []error) error` (`review.ErrNoFeatureClears`) and `review.CheckLandedTree(merge, mergeTree, head, headTree, first string) error` (`review.ErrTreeMoved`). The use case `reviewapp.Service.CheckLanding(dir, merge string) (Landing, error)` reads the facts through the Git port, which gains `Parents(repo, commit) ([]string, error)`, `MergeBases(repo, a, b string) ([]string, error)` (none is an empty list and no error) and `Tree(repo, commit) (string, error)` (`gitexec` runs `git rev-list --parents -n 1 <commit>`, `git merge-base --all <a> <b>`, whose exit 1 with nothing printed is none, and `git rev-parse --verify <commit>^{tree}`), and calls `CheckChain`; the CLI adapter composes it as `review check` does (`newReviewService`, `engine/cmd/review.go`). No other review verb's output changes but the two lists of verbs, which become exactly `error: review requires a verb: bind, record, verify, check, exempt, sweep, anchor` and `error: review: unknown verb "<verb>" (expected bind, record, verify, check, exempt, sweep or anchor)`.
  - (h) **openspec archives.** `tools/archive-anchor-gate`, its CI job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:263-305`) and `known-gaps.txt` are not changed: the gate keeps scanning `openspec/changes/archive/`, where seven changes are still live under `openspec/changes/` (L83), as owner decision 5a left it. Retiring it is not part of S7 (D5).
  - (i) **Help and docs.** The engine usage (`engine/cmd/usage.go`), the wrapper's `review` help (`bin/lib/usage.sh`) and `README.md` (section `overlay review <verb>`) gain the `anchor` entry, saying what (b)-(f) say; `odd/review-results/README.md` says that CI holds the merge of a pull request to the stored reviews of the feature documents it changes; `CHANGELOG.md`, Unreleased, one entry. `skills/inception-pipeline/SKILL.md:147` is not changed (it speaks of the openspec gate, which stays).
  - (j) **Limits.** The job refuses a merge only once the owner marks it a required check in the repository's branch protection, a setting and not code (D4). A pull request of a feature whose commits were reviewed only by gentle-ai receipts is refused (D4). A merge of a branch that was behind its base is refused, and bringing the branch up to date adds commits that need their own records (D1). A merge made before S7 lands is not checked again.
- **S8 — Lens rows as data.** The reviewer's lens row sentences (default, readability, risk-line second launch) live once in a canonical file the parent copies from; the rows used are recorded in the result, so `review_lens_count` in `skills/_shared/actuals-record.schema.json:62` derives from our data, not gentle's `selected_lenses`.
- **S9 — Codex launcher with enforced read-only.** Decision D11 of `per-role-design-v3.md`: `labdrian review launch --runtime codex` runs the reviewer through `codex exec --sandbox read-only -m <model>` and captures its output for `record`; the v3 contradictions on Codex (`:72`, `:105`) are reconciled. Design (shaper, L115; open decisions D23-D30 of L115 marked where they apply); acceptance criteria:
  - (a) **The verb.** `engine review launch --runtime codex --feature <name> --base <rev> --head <rev> --rows <id,id> --model <model> [--cwd <repo>]`, the brief on stdin (read as `review exempt` reads its report, at most `review.MaxReplyBytes` plus one byte). `--runtime` takes only `codex`; any other value is refused with `--runtime must be codex: on Claude Code and Pi the parent launches the reviewer agent natively` (exit 1). `--model` is required and passed through (D27: no default; the Codex agent files omit the model, D14 of v3). In order, before codex starts: `review.CheckFeature`; `review.ParseRows` of `--rows` (as `review record`); `review.CheckModel` (new: refuses a model that is empty, starts with `-` or holds a control character, wrapping `review.ErrInvalidModel`); `Service.Bind` exactly as `review bind` (it makes or reuses the detached worktree); a candidate over `review.DiffLimitBytes` is refused (wrapping `review.ErrOverLimit`: `the diff is <n> bytes, over the 150000-byte limit of one launch; split the candidate into one launch per commit (skills/role-chain/SKILL.md)`); `review.CheckBrief` (new, wrapping `review.ErrInvalidBrief`) refuses a brief that is empty, not valid UTF-8, over `review.MaxReplyBytes`, or that does not hold, as text, the full `base`, the full `head`, the `diff_sha256` and the worktree path of the binding, with `the brief does not name the <base|head|diff_sha256|worktree> <value> of the binding, so it is not the brief of this candidate`. Every refusal of this list is exit 1 and starts nothing (D28).
  - (b) **The run.** The adapter `engine/review/codexexec` reads the reviewer's instructions from the deployed Codex agent file `<codex root>/agents/reviewer.toml` (`core.Config.DefaultCodexRoot`: `$CODEX_HOME` when absolute, else `~/.codex`; D23), its `developer_instructions` read by `agentgen.CodexInstructions`, the exact inverse of the generator's `codexFile`; a missing or unreadable file, or one the generator did not write, is an error naming the path and `run: labdrian-overlay apply --target codex` (exit 1). It starts, through a port and never a shell, exactly `codex exec --sandbox read-only --model <model> --cd <worktree> --skip-git-repo-check --ephemeral --ignore-user-config --output-last-message <scratch>/last-message.txt -` (D30 for `--ignore-user-config`), its standard input the instructions, a blank line and the brief, its environment the process's, bounded by a deadline (assumption: 30 minutes) and by 64 MiB on each stream, the `<scratch>` directory made under the system temporary directory and removed when the run ends whatever happens. The reply is the bytes of the last-message file. Codex not found, exiting non-zero, stopped at its deadline, or leaving the file missing or empty, is an error wrapping `reviewapp.ErrReviewerDidNotAnswer` that names `codex` and repeats what it printed on stderr (bounded), exit 1, nothing stored.
  - (c) **The record.** The reply is recorded by `Service.Record` over the full names of the bound candidate and the `--rows` given, so every rule of `review record` holds unchanged: a reply refused by it is exit 2 with its text and nothing stored; stored, stdout and stderr are exactly what `review record` prints for that reply (`reportRecorded`), exit 0.
  - (d) **The verb lists and docs.** `review` with no verb and with an unknown verb become exactly `error: review requires a verb: bind, record, verify, check, exempt, sweep, anchor, launch` and `error: review: unknown verb "<verb>" (expected bind, record, verify, check, exempt, sweep, anchor or launch)`; `stop-hook` stays out of both. The engine usage, the wrapper's `review` help and README (`overlay review <verb>`) gain the `launch` entry saying what (a)-(c) say, and the sentence "No verb launches an agent or dispatches work" of the review sections becomes one that names `launch` as the one verb that starts a reviewer, on Codex, read-only; the header comment of `engine/cmd/review.go` says the same. `CHANGELOG.md`, Unreleased, one entry. `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md`, Binding, gains one line after the `review record` line: on Codex the parent pipes the same brief to `labdrian review launch --runtime codex` with `--model`, never a native reviewer sub-agent (D29; the human re-approves the skill).
  - (e) **v3 reconciled.** `docs/architecture/role-design/per-role-design-v3.md:72`: the read-only reviewer on Codex is enforced by the sandbox of the separate process `labdrian review launch --runtime codex` starts (D11), not by prose; `:105`: the cut `codex exec`-driven reviewer runner is marked as adopted by D11 (owner 2026-10-22) and built by own-rdd S9. No other line of v3 changes.
  - (f) **Limits.** The polisher's judges through the launcher (v3 `:67`) are not part of S9 (D26). The brief check is textual: a brief that names the binding and asks something else passes it (accepted limit: the reply is still held to the binding by `review record`). No test starts the real `codex`; that the installed Codex (0.164.0, `@openai/codex` package.json; D11 verified 0.148.0) enforces read-only is proved only by the live check of D25.
- **S10 — Pi verifier.** A verifier role file for Pi, generated like the other roles. Met before T8 (shaper, L115): `engine/agentgen/roles.go:139-145` generates `pi/agents/verifier.md` with the other roles, `linkPackageAgents` (`engine/runtime/pi_agents.go:152`) links it at `~/.pi/agent/agents/verifier.md`, where gentle-pi 4.3.0 discovers agents (`lib/agents-config.ts:208-217`), and `engine/agentgen/roles_test.go:62` pins its row; T8 changes nothing for S10. No live Pi verifier run is recorded (D24).
- **S16 — The tests of the base pass on the toolchains in use.** Outside S1-S15 (recorded as T13 in L75). Owner (L79), approving the order T13, then the carried docs items (S17), then T7, T9, T8, T12: "vamos con ese orden che a ver si algun dia terminamos". Diagnosis in L80 (the prototyper's spikes as the parent reports them, and what the shaper checked). Acceptance:
  - (a) **JSON error texts that do not follow the Go toolchain.** `engine/go.mod` says `go 1.21` and CI builds the engine with it (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:86`, `go-version-file`), while the local toolchain is go1.27.3, whose `encoding/json` words two errors differently; the tests and goldens pin the go1.21 words and stay unchanged, so the fix is in product code. `jsonstrict.DecodeObject(data []byte) (map[string]json.RawMessage, error)` (new, `engine/jsonstrict/jsonstrict.go`) unmarshals data into a `map[string]json.RawMessage`; when that fails with a `*json.UnmarshalTypeError` (decoding into that map, the only one possible is a top-level value that is not an object), the error's text is exactly `json: cannot unmarshal <Value> into Go value of type map[string]json.RawMessage`, `<Value>` the error's `Value` field, and `errors.As` still finds the original `*json.UnmarshalTypeError` (the error unwraps to it); any other error is returned as it is; `null` gives a nil map and no error, as today. `jsonstrict.CheckKnownFields` and `memoryscope.ParseDirectiveMember` call it in place of their own `json.Unmarshal` into the map; `jsonstrict.WireVersion`, which drops the error, is not touched. `settings.Parse` (`engine/settings/document.go:37`), for data whose first three bytes are `EF BB BF` (a UTF-8 byte order mark), returns the error `invalid character 'ï' looking for beginning of value` without calling `json.Unmarshal`: the text go1.21 gives. Assumption (the prototyper's spikes, unverified beyond them): the `Value` words (`array`, `string`, `number`, `bool`) are the same under both toolchains. Checks: unchanged, `TestVersionThreeRefusesWithNamedReason` (`engine/goal`), `TestParseDirectiveMemberReadsAnOptionalNestedDirective` (`engine/memoryscope`) and `TestSettingsJSONIsWhatTheProgramWroteBeforeH27` (`engine/settings/settingsfile`, its utf8-bom case) pass under `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.21.13` and under go1.27.3, and so does `cd engine && go test -count=1 ./...` under both. Limits: only the texts a test pins are held; a first character other than a byte order mark that is not ASCII, and a type error into any other destination, are still worded as the toolchain words them.
  - (b) **A test that needs the history says so.** `TestD2AnchorIsVerifiableAndUnambiguous` (`engine/skills/actuals_instrumentation_d2_anchor_test.go`) reads the history of the repository (the commit `be2c3ca46ea5f4f740ecab6b51dc2db3f167f37c` and `origin/main`). Before any of its checks it runs, in the repository, `git cat-file -e be2c3ca46ea5f4f740ecab6b51dc2db3f167f37c^{commit}` and `git rev-parse --verify --quiet origin/main`; when either fails, the test is skipped with exactly `needs the repository's git history (commit be2c3ca46ea5f4f740ecab6b51dc2db3f167f37c and origin/main), which this checkout does not have: a git archive or a shallow clone`, except when the environment variable `CI` is `true`, where the same text fails the test, so a CI checkout without the history is never a silent pass (S1). Checks: in an extraction of `git archive HEAD` the test is skipped with that text (today it fails); there with `CI=true` it fails with that text; in the checkout it runs and passes.
  - (c) **`TestOverlayStatusAndSyncCheck_PiStubNoEmptyMkdir`** (`engine/shelltest/overlay_pi_target_test.go:77`; T13 named `engine/cmd` by mistake): not reproduced, cause unknown (L80); no change. It stays listed as an unproven flaky failure in builder and verifier prompts until it fails again with its output kept.
- **S17 — The stop hook's docs say what it asks for.** Carried from L77 and L78: SUGGESTION F1 of `odd/review-results/own-rdd/dba4a474a59e-2.json` (owner rule: every finding is resolved) and the README sweep entry's "until the copy streams". Acceptance:
  - (a) Every text that says the hook asks for what "the first commit not ready for delivery" misses says "the first commit with unfinished review steps" instead: a commit whose stored review blocks has its review and is not asked again (`engine/review/app/unreviewed.go:49-53`), yet it is not ready for delivery. The texts: `README.md:750-751` (the `stop-hook` entry of `overlay review <verb>`), `README.md:1217` (section `gentle-ai-overlay review stop-hook`), `bin/lib/hooks.sh:63`, `bin/lib/usage.sh:69-71`, `engine/capability/declarations.go:36`. None of them says `not ready for delivery` afterwards.
  - (b) `README.md:1221-1222`: the remedy of the trailer, `` (`git commit --amend --trailer`) ``, says when it applies, as the hook's block does (`engine/cmd/review_stop_hook.go:129-130`): `` (`git commit --amend --trailer` when the commit is HEAD) ``.
