  - (c) `README.md:729-730`: `the delivery of such a repository stops until the copy streams.` becomes `the delivery of such a repository stops.`: the 256 MiB bound was kept in L63 (the streaming copy rejected, the owner free to overturn it), and no task makes the copy stream. S6 (h) is not changed.
  - (d) Carried from L82 (SUGGESTION F1 of `odd/review-results/own-rdd/dafd5b75044a-2.json`, `causal_disposition` pre-existing; owner rule: every finding is resolved): the hook's own block reason says what the docs say. Its first sentence (`engine/cmd/review_stop_hook.go:119`) becomes exactly `Commit <head> of branch <branch> has unfinished review steps.`; the rest of the reason, every need line and the closing paragraph stay byte for byte; S6 (e) is rewritten to match. No text the hook prints says `not ready for delivery` afterwards.
  - (e) Carried from L82: `odd/review-results/README.md:316`, the sentence `Reviews are per commit, so when the agent is about to stop with its branch at a commit that is not ready for delivery, the hook blocks the stop and asks for what the first such commit misses (` becomes exactly `Reviews are per commit, so when the agent is about to stop on a branch that has commits with unfinished review steps, the hook blocks the stop and asks for what the first of them misses (`; the rest of the paragraph stays.
  - (f) Carried from L82 (SUGGESTION F1 of `odd/review-results/own-rdd/bd9c076ecd6c-3.json`, introduced, cosmetic): the doc comment of `runGitReadOnly` (`engine/skills/actuals_instrumentation_d2_anchor_test.go:463-467`) is rewrapped at the width of its other lines (at most 79 columns), its words unchanged.
- **S18 — own-rdd's remaining work runs inside the workflow machinery.** Owner (L85), to the parent's report that own-rdd runs outside the goal contract and the workflow machinery (`labdrian workflow binding` prints `{"classification": "absent"}`, nothing is under `~/.local/state/labdrian/role-chains/`, and no Log entry decides not to use a workflow) and to its proposal to bind own-rdd to a workflow with its goal and role chain, so each hop is recorded from now on without redoing finished work: "claro es por ahi, y tenemos que adaptar s7 a odd como documentacion o spec de la campaña  en lugar de usar openscpec"; earlier: "explicame cual es el tema de S7 ?? tambien explicame poruqe noe stamos usando el contrato de goal y la definicion del workfloww y todo eso _". It is the first use of S19. Design (shaper, L85; D6 and D7 of L85 open); no engine change; acceptance:
  - (a) **The goal.** One Goal v3 for the remaining work, committed as `odd/goals/own-rdd.json` (assumption, D7: versioned beside the feature document, so the goal digest `workflow create` records can be held to a committed file; `workflow create` and `workflow verify` re-read it from the path they are given, `engine/cmd/usage.go:285`). Its members, in the contract's order (`engine/goal/testdata/v3.json`), with two-space indentation: `"version": 3`, `"project_id": "<P>"`, `"goal_id": "own-rdd"`, `"objective": "Finish own receipt-driven development: the tasks of odd/tasks/own-rdd.md that are not checked off, so that our review lifecycle stands alone before gentle-ai's review is removed."`, `"scope": "The specs of odd/tasks/own-rdd.md that those tasks name; one workflow and one role chain for each task."`, `"constraints": ["Finished tasks of odd/tasks/own-rdd.md are not redone.", "The builder builds only a task the shaper designed (S14).", "Every commit is reviewed or exempted, and labdrian review check clears it (S5, S6)."]`, `"non_goals": ["Removing gentle-ai review, its hooks, lens agents and engine/reviewreceipt."]`, `"acceptance_criteria": ["T17, T7, T9, T8 and T12 of odd/tasks/own-rdd.md are checked off, each with its commit.", "labdrian review check --base clears the head of each task's pull request."]`, `"memory_directive": {"version": 1, "scope": "project", "sources": ["engram", "longterm-mem", "procedural-skills"], "write": "none"}`, `"runtime_scope": "Claude Code, Codex and Pi, as the specs of odd/tasks/own-rdd.md name them."`, `"delivery_boundary": "Work-unit commits on the branches of odd/tasks/own-rdd.md; push, pull requests and merges are the owner's decisions."`. `<P>` is the project of `mem_current_project` (assumption: `labdrian-sdd-overlay`, unverified; `workflow create` refuses a goal of another project). The text is the shaper's draft from L1 and the parent's report; the owner approves it (D7).
  - (b) **One workflow and one role chain for each task.** A role chain ends at its first `delivery` record (`roles.VerifyChain`, `engine/roles/chain.go:62-64`), a pull request is one delivery (one PR per task, the header), and a workflow follows the one chain named at its creation (`workflow create --role-chain`; `Narrow` and `Verify` read only that chain, `engine/workflow/lifecycle.go:641-682`, `:770-830`). So each remaining task T, in the order T17, T7, T9, T8, T12, runs under the workflow `own-rdd-<t>` (`<t>` the task id in lower case, for example `own-rdd-t17`), created with the goal of (a), the profile `odd` and the role chain `own-rdd-<t>` of goal `own-rdd` (assumption, D6: one workflow per task with a shared goal; the chain id differs from the goal id, unlike inception's goal id = chain id, `skills/inception-pipeline/SKILL.md:64`, because own-rdd delivers once per task). A finished task gets no workflow and no record afterwards.
  - (c) **Opening the workflow of T17 by hand** (the bootstrap: S19's verb does not exist yet), run by the parent from the repository root, `<doc>` the sha256 of `odd/tasks/own-rdd.md` at the commit that holds L85 (`git show <commit>:odd/tasks/own-rdd.md | sha256sum`): (1) `labdrian roles append --project <P> --goal own-rdd --chain own-rdd-t17 --stdin` with the record `version` 1, `seq` 1, `from_role` `shaper`, `to_role` `estimator`, `prev_sha256` 64 zeros, `payload_kind` `feature-doc`, `payload_sha256` `<doc>`, `evidence` `[]`, `status` `completed` and a context summary naming T17 and L85; then `seq` 2, `estimator` to `builder`, the same `payload_sha256` and `prev_sha256` the sha256 of the first record's bytes (`skills/role-chain/SKILL.md:16`; no estimator agent exists, so the parent writes it); (2) `labdrian workflow create --project <P> --workflow own-rdd-t17 --goal "$PWD/odd/goals/own-rdd.json" --profile odd --role-chain own-rdd-t17`, then `labdrian workflow start --project <P> --workflow own-rdd-t17`; (3) `labdrian workflow stage --project <P> --workflow own-rdd-t17 --stage <s>` for `authorize`, `explore`, `resolve-uncertainty`, `classify` and `track-if-substantial`, in that order (the profile's, `engine/workflowprofile/workflowprofile.go:91`; the shaping did them); (4) `labdrian workflow bind --project <P> --workflow own-rdd-t17`. Checks: `labdrian workflow binding` names `own-rdd-t17`, owned, status `running`; `labdrian roles resume --project <P> --goal own-rdd --chain own-rdd-t17` names the role `builder`; `labdrian memory plan --instructions` prints the plan of the workflow. From T7 on, S19 (b) runs these steps in one command.
  - (d) **The hops while T is built.** When the builder starts, `workflow stage ... --stage implement-task-by-task`. Each hop is one `roles append` record of a transition `roles.IsValidTransition` allows: `builder` to `reviewer` (or to `sweeper` or `polisher` when they ran) with `payload_kind` `progress` once T's last work unit is committed; `reviewer` to `builder` when a review blocks; `reviewer` to `delivery` with the `review-report` record `review record` and `review verify` print for the review of T's last reviewed commit, or the `review-exemption` record of `review exempt` when that commit was exempted. Reviews are per commit (S5) while a review hop's evidence carries one `review-result` (`engine/cmd/usage.go:239-245`), so the reviews of T's earlier commits stay under `odd/review-results/own-rdd/` and are not hops (limit). A record that carries a `memory_directive` is followed by `labdrian workflow narrow`.
  - (e) **Closing T.** After T's pull request merges: `workflow stage ... --stage close`, `labdrian workflow verify --project <P> --workflow own-rdd-<t> --goal "$PWD/odd/goals/own-rdd.json"`, `labdrian workflow close --project <P> --workflow own-rdd-<t> --outcome completed`, `labdrian workflow unbind`. A goal file that changed after the workflow was created fails `verify` on its digest, so a change of (a) needs the owner's words in the Log and a new workflow.
  - (f) **What a binding does to sessions.** The binding is per repository and shared by every worktree (`engine/cmd/usage.go:301-303`): while it holds, every Claude Code session in this repository gets the workflow in its context and the memory gate of its plan, and Write and Edit are denied while the workflow is paused (`engine/cmd/usage.go:312-321`). The directive of (a) is the profile's ceiling, so it narrows no read (assumption, D7).
  - (g) **Limits.** The profile `odd` still declares the gentle-ai review dependency (`ReliesOnGentleReview`, `engine/workflowprofile/workflowprofile.go:95-100`); S18 does not change it. Owner decision 1 (a) of `docs/architecture/adr-c13-inception-role-chain.md:19` (inception opens no workflow) is not changed by S18; S19 is where the harness requires one.
- **S19 — The harness keeps every feature inside its workflow.** Owner (L85), relayed by the parent (2026-10-29): "tenemos que hacer que el harness haga esto de forma automatica que no lo pierda porque es el diferenciador que tenemos de los harneses que dicen que todo esta probado y tal que pal". The parent's reading: not a one-off bind of own-rdd; the harness itself makes sure every large ODD feature runs inside a workflow with its goal contract and role-chain records, enforced mechanically, so a feature can never again run outside it silently, as own-rdd did. Design (shaper, L85; D8-D10 of L85 open); acceptance:
  - (a) **The gate is the builder guard.** Every large ODD feature reaches its code through a builder launch that cites `Spec: odd/tasks/<f>.md, T<n>` and passes S14's checks (`engine/cmd/roles_builder_guard.go:89`); that is the one point the harness already sees for every feature and that nobody can forget, so the guard gains one more check there and no new hook is installed. After every check of S14 passes for a builder launch, the guard reads the workflow binding of the git repository of the session's `cwd` (the store and classification `labdrian workflow binding` reads, `engine/cmd/workflow_bind.go:231`, `engine/projection/bindingstore.go:140`) and the bound workflow's state, and denies the launch, in this order, when: the repository is bound to no workflow, or its binding is not owned (`the repository of <cwd> is bound to no workflow (labdrian workflow binding: <classification>)`); the bound workflow is not owned or its status is not `running` (`the workflow <id> bound to the repository is <status>, not running`); its goal is not the cited feature, the goal id being the feature's name `<f>` (`the workflow <id> bound to the repository is of goal <goal>, not of the feature <f> the prompt cites`); it follows no role chain (`the workflow <id> bound to the repository follows no role chain`); the chain has no records or fails `roles.VerifyChain` (`the role chain <chain> of the workflow <id> does not verify (<cause>)`); or the chain's current role (`roles.Resume`) is not `builder` (`the role chain <chain> of the workflow <id> hands the work to <role>, not to the builder`). Any failure to read the binding, the log or the chain denies, as every failure of a builder launch does (S14 e). Other agents and every reason of S14 stay byte for byte.
  - (b) **One command opens a task's workflow.** `labdrian workflow open --feature <f> --task <T> [--goal <path>]` (engine `workflow open`), from the repository, does S18 (c) (1)-(4) for any feature: the goal is `odd/goals/<f>.json` under the repository's root unless `--goal` names another; the project is the goal's `project_id` and its `goal_id` must be `<f>`; the task line of `T` in `odd/tasks/<f>.md` must hold the mark of S14 (a), read with `engine/oddtask` as the guard reads it; the chain and the workflow are `<f>-<t>`, `<t>` the task id in lower case; the two records carry `payload_sha256` the sha256 of the bytes of `odd/tasks/<f>.md` as they are on disk and a summary naming `T` and its `shaped: L<n>`; then create with profile `odd` and that chain, start, the five stages of S18 (c) (3), and bind. It reuses the role-chain store's append, `workflow.Lifecycle` (`Create`, `Start`, `RecordStage`) and the binding store's bind, and writes none of their rules again. It refuses, before anything is written, a goal that is missing, invalid or of another goal id, a task that is not marked, a chain that already has records and a workflow that already exists; a step that fails after an earlier one wrote leaves what was written, and the error names the steps done and the step that failed (assumption: the stores have no transaction across them). stdout is one JSON object `{"workflow":"<f>-<t>","chain":"<f>-<t>","goal":"<f>","project":"<P>","status":"running","bound":true}`; exit 0 opened, 2 refused, 1 usage error or a failure after a write.
  - (c) **The deny message.** For the reasons of (a) the message is exactly `labdrian builder guard: <reason>. Every feature runs inside its workflow: run labdrian workflow open --feature <f> --task <T> (it records the shaper and estimator hops, then creates, starts and binds the workflow of the task), then launch the builder again. This guard reads text and local state only, so it is a speed bump, not a security boundary.`, `<T>` the first task the prompt cites.
  - (d) **Docs.** The engine usage of `roles builder-guard` and `workflow open`, the wrapper help, `README.md`, `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` (one line: a feature's builder runs only inside the workflow `workflow open` opens; the human re-approves the skill) and `CHANGELOG.md` say what (a)-(c) say.
  - (e) **Limits.** Claude Code only, as S14 (i). Work the parent writes inline, with no builder, is not gated (D9). The gate holds in every repository where `install-hooks` wired the builder guard, not only this one (D8). A large ODD feature without a goal file cannot get past the guard until one is written, so the shaper's tasks for a new feature start with its goal (D10).
- **S20 — The next subtask of a task stays in the task's workflow.** Owner (L93), to the parent's question after the builder guard refused T7b inside `own-rdd-t7` with the remedy `labdrian workflow open --feature own-rdd --task T7b`: "lo recomendado" (the parent's recommendation: append a `reviewer → builder` hop inside `own-rdd-t7`, carrying the clean T7a review as evidence and saying it returns no finding); then (L96): "resolvamos lo que queda pendiente que anotaste ene  l log". Scope: the gap L93 logged for the shaper, nothing else; S18 (b) (one workflow per task, L86) and S19 (a) (every reason and its order) stay. Design (shaper, L97; D11-D14 of L97 open); acceptance:
  - (a) **The hop.** After a clear review of a subtask's last commit, the next subtask of the same task is handed to the builder inside the task's workflow by one `roles append` record `reviewer` to `builder`, `payload_kind` `review-report`, its evidence the `review-report` evidence of that review (`review-diff`, `review-result`, `verifier-report` when the verifier ran), held by `roles append` as today (`engine/cmd/roles_evidence.go:32-48`), and a summary naming the subtask and saying the review has no finding (as hop `000005` of `own-rdd-t7` does). The transition is already valid (`roles.IsValidTransition`, `engine/roles/roles.go:138-140`) and the guard allows a builder once the chain's last `to_role` is `builder` (`engine/oddtask/workflow.go:77`), so no engine rule changes. A subtask never gets a workflow of its own (assumption: a subtask is a task line whose parent row says it is done when its subtasks are, as T7 says of T7a and T7b).
  - (b) **The guard's remedy for a chain that waits for a hop.** When every reason of S19 (a) but the last passes (the binding is owned, the workflow owned and running, its goal the cited feature, it follows a chain that has records and verifies) and the chain's current role is neither `builder` nor `delivery`, the denial is exactly `labdrian builder guard: <reason>. The repository is already inside the workflow <workflow> of this feature, so do not open another: append the hop its role chain <chain> waits for, then launch the builder again; after a clear review, the next subtask of the same task goes back to the builder with a reviewer to builder hop that carries the review (skills/role-chain). This guard reads text and local state only, so it is a speed bump, not a security boundary.`, `<reason>` the text S19 (a) gives today (`the role chain <chain> of the workflow <workflow> hands the work to <role>, not to the builder`, unchanged). Every other reason, and a chain whose current role is `delivery`, keeps the message of S19 (c) byte for byte.
  - (c) **Docs.** `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` line 35: `(the only backward edge)` becomes `(rework)`, and one line follows it, exactly ``- Clear review and a next subtask of the workflow's task: `reviewer → builder`, `payload_kind` `review-report` with that review's evidence, the subtask in the summary; never a second `workflow open`.``; no other line of the skill changes, and its body stays within `BodyRecommendedTokens` (700, `engine/skills/lint.go:44`; estimated at about 640 before and 690 after by the shaper's byte count, unverified until the budget test runs). The engine usage of `roles builder-guard` (`engine/cmd/usage.go:262-264`) and the README's builder guard section (`README.md:1309-1317`) gain, after the sentence that ends `launch the builder again`, exactly `When the bound workflow is the running one of the feature and its chain verifies but waits for a hop other than to the builder or to delivery, the denial says instead not to open another workflow and to append that hop: after a clear review, the next subtask of the same task is handed back with a reviewer to builder hop that carries the review.`, and the README also quotes the denial of (b); `bin/lib/usage.sh:257` `(run workflow open)` becomes `(run workflow open, or append the hop the chain waits for)`, rewrapped at its column; the doc comment of `roles.IsValidTransition` says the backward transition is rework or the next subtask of the same task after a clear review (comment only); `CHANGELOG.md`, one clause in the Unreleased S19 entry.
  - (d) **Limits.** The guard tells neither rework from a next subtask nor a subtask of the workflow's task from any other shaped task of the feature: under a running workflow of the feature whose chain is at `builder`, any shaped task of the feature passes, as today (D12). The `reviewer` role's catalog text (`engine/roles/roles.go:94`, "approved for delivery, or sent back to the builder with findings") is not changed (D14). A chain at `delivery` keeps the `workflow open` remedy, which still writes before its bind refuses while the repository is bound (L89) until the delivered workflow is closed and unbound (S18 e).
- **S21 — Two loose items of T18.** Owner (L102), relayed by the parent (2026-10-29): "y cerremos las cosas sueltas". Scope: the two loose items of L101 the parent names in L102, nothing else: the polisher's findings shape, and SUGGESTION F1 of `odd/review-results/own-rdd/7df8dca6a617-2.json`. The stale approval of `role-chain`, T18's delivery and the close of `own-rdd-t18` stay as L101 leaves them. Design (shaper, L103; D15-D17 of L103 open); acceptance:
  - (a) **The polisher's agent file spells out the object it returns.** The defect (L101): a judge returned each finding as `{severity,title,detail,introduced_by_target,proof}` and `labdrian review record --role polisher --result FILE` refused it: `review: invalid polisher findings: finding 1 has no "evidence" that is a non-empty array of non-empty strings`. The recorder (`review.ParsePolisherFindings`, `engine/review/rolerecords.go:420-451`) is the one rule and does not change. The third line of the delta of `roles/polisher/body.md` (line 10) becomes exactly `This replaces the return envelope of the line above: your whole return is one JSON object and no prose, {"findings":[{"location":"path:line|path:start-end","severity":"BLOCKER|CRITICAL|WARNING|SUGGESTION","claim":"...","evidence_class":"deterministic|inferential","causal_disposition":"introduced|behavior-activated|worsened|pre-existing|base-only|unknown","evidence":["the concrete causal proof"]}],"evidence":["what was inspected"]}; {"findings":[],"evidence":["what was inspected"]} when clean; every finding has at least one non-empty evidence entry, or labdrian review record refuses the whole reply.`; lines 8 and 9 do not change. `agents/polisher.md`, `pi/agents/polisher.md` and `codex/agents/polisher.toml` are regenerated from it (`overlay gadu-generate`, `README.md:429-435`), never edited by hand. The members are those of judgment-day's judge shape with `proof_refs` named `evidence`, as the recorder names it, and the enumerations those of `roles/reviewer/body.md:10` (assumption, D15).
  - (b) **The judge prompt of judgment-day points to that object.** In `skills/judgment-day/references/prompts-and-formats.md` the judge prompt's lines 14-18 (an inline shape whose findings carry `proof_refs` and no `evidence`, and which allows no other finding member, so the recorder refuses every finding a judge returns by it) become the one line `Return one JSON object and no prose: the object your agent file (polisher) spells out, which labdrian review record --role polisher stores. Return {"findings":[],"evidence":["what was inspected"]} when clean, then terminate.`; in line 21, `This shape governs judgment-day judges only.` becomes `The polisher's object governs judgment-day judges only.`; no other line of the skill changes.
  - (c) **Pins.** A test requires the object the polisher's delta spells out, filled with a legal value for each member and with each alternative of each enumeration, to be accepted by `review.ParsePolisherFindings`, the clean object too, and the same object without its finding's `evidence` to be refused; the reply of L101 is refused with the exact text of (a); the judge prompt of judgment-day holds `the object your agent file (polisher) spells out` and neither `proof_refs` nor `"findings":[{`. `skills/sweeper/SKILL.md:13` does not change: it names the verb, and the agent file is what every polisher launch loads.
  - (d) **`WaitsForHop` only for a known role.** `oddtask.WaitsForHop` (`engine/oddtask/workflow.go:89-96`) is false when `v.Role` is not a role of `roles.Vocabulary` (`roles.IsKnownRole`, `engine/roles/roles.go:120`), so an empty or unknown role with records gets the denial of S19 (c), not the hop remedy of S20 (b); every known role gives what it gives today. This resolves F1 of `7df8dca6a617-2.json` (owner rule: every finding is resolved, S17). No input of `roles builder-guard` reaches the case (the role is `roles.Resume` of a chain the store verified, `engine/cmd/roles_builder_guard.go:153-165`), so the verb's output does not change.
  - (e) **Docs.** `CHANGELOG.md`, one Unreleased clause for (a) and (b). None for (d), and none in `README.md` or the engine usage, which already state the recorder's rule (`README.md:742-744`, `engine/cmd/usage.go:224`).
  - (f) **Limits.** The recorder still holds only each finding's `evidence`; the members and enumerations of (a) are the agent's instructions, not checked (D7 of L40 stays: no polisher finding blocks), and the top-level `evidence` stays unchecked (D16).
- Assumption (from the owner's "todo"): the S6 hook blocks once per candidate, like gentle's, rather than only reminding. Out of scope: removing gentle-ai review, its hooks, lens agents and `engine/reviewreceipt` (step 3 of the owner's order).

- **L5** (2026-10-26, builder): T1 is done and checked off. The commit stays `-` because the parent commits; I made no commit, no push and no branch change.

  - Covered: S2 in full. S1 for the rules of the review contract that concern the reviewer's result (the echo of the binding, the inspection status, the inspected paths, the enumerations, the finding `id`, the proof of a severe finding); the other rules of S1 belong to T2-T8.
  - Delivered: the domain package `engine/reviewresult` (`reviewresult.go`, `errors.go`, `rules.go`, `wire.go` and three test files), registered as a domain package in `engine/architecture_test.go`; `id` in the finding schema of `roles/reviewer/body.md`, plus the three rules the parser enforces, in the same line (the delta is still 4 lines); `agents/reviewer.md`, `pi/agents/reviewer.md` and `codex/agents/reviewer.toml` regenerated (one line each); the pin `TestReviewerDeltaSpellsOutTheJSONObjectItReturns` updated and `TestReviewerDeltaObjectIsTheOneTheEngineParses` added, which requires `reviewresult.Parse` to accept the object the delta spells out, filled with every value the delta lists; `odd/review-results/README.md` and `CHANGELOG.md`. No CLI verb was added.
  - Errors: ten named values, told apart with `errors.Is`: `ErrInvalidBinding`, `ErrUnparseable`, `ErrUnknownMember`, `ErrCandidateMismatch`, `ErrInspectionNotCompleted`, `ErrPathsMismatch`, `ErrMissingID`, `ErrDuplicateID`, `ErrOutsideEnum`, `ErrMissingProof`. A refused reply returns the zero `Result`. A reply that breaks several rules is refused for the first one, in the order of the package comment, so the same reply is always refused for the same reason.
  - Decisions the prompt asked me to state:
    - Malformed JSON (an empty reply, prose or a fence around the object, an array, `null`, two objects or trailing bytes, bytes that are not UTF-8, a key written twice, a member of the wrong type) is refused as `ErrUnparseable`. White space around the object is accepted.
    - An unknown member, at the top level or inside `candidate`, `inspection` or a finding, is refused as `ErrUnknownMember`. Names are matched exactly: the JSON decoder matches names without regard to case and keeps the last one, so `"severity":"BLOCKER","Severity":"SUGGESTION"` would have downgraded a BLOCKER in silence.
    - Every member of every object is required and none may be null. A missing or null `findings` is `ErrUnparseable`, never a clean review; a clean review is `"findings":[]`.
    - The inspected paths are compared with the paths of the diff as sets: order and repeats do not matter, and the error names the paths on each side.
    - Beyond the S2 list but inside S1: `evidence_class` is checked against its enumeration too, the binding itself is validated (with an empty binding, an empty echo would be "the same candidate"), and a blank string is neither an `id` nor a proof reference.
    - The enumerations of `roles/reviewer/body.md` and of `docs/architecture/role-design/per-role-reviewer.md` section 4 agree; there was nothing to reconcile, and the parser uses the body's.
  - Commands run (from `engine/`) and their exact results:
    - `go test -count=1 ./reviewresult/... ./skills/... ./agentgen/... .`: all `ok` (reviewresult 0.012s, skills 8.307s, skills/app, skills/engramexport, skills/projectidentity, skills/registryyaml, skills/skillsfs, agentgen 0.057s, engine 0.075s), exit 0.
    - `OVERLAY_DIR=$PWD/.. go run ./cmd/ gadu-generate --check`: `gadu-generate --check: OK (committed artifacts match generator output)`, exit 0.
    - `go test -count=1 ./...` filtered with `rg -- '--- FAIL|^FAIL'`: 70 packages `ok`; the only failures are the three tests the prompt lists as known environmental failures (I did not re-run them on the base): `TestVersionThreeRefusesWithNamedReason/memory_directive_not_an_object` (engine/goal), `TestParseDirectiveMemberReadsAnOptionalNestedDirective/not_an_object` (engine/memoryscope), `TestSettingsJSONIsWhatTheProgramWroteBeforeH27/utf8-bom.merge` and `.../utf8-bom.uninstall-first` (engine/settings/settingsfile).
    - `go vet ./...`: no output, exit 0. `gofmt -l reviewresult architecture_test.go skills/role_agents_artifact_test.go`: no output.
    - `staticcheck ./reviewresult/...`: not run. The installed staticcheck 2026.2.1 was built with an older Go than go1.27.3 and fails on every package with `internal error in importing "internal/byteorder" (cannot decode "internal/byteorder", export data version 5 is greater than maximum supported version 4)`, exit 1; an offline rebuild, `GOPROXY=off go run honnef.co/go/tools/cmd/staticcheck@v0.8.1 ./reviewresult/...`, failed with `module lookup disabled by GOPROXY=off`. There is no staticcheck verdict, neither a pass nor a finding.
    - `go test -count=1 -v ./reviewresult/`: 16 tests and 101 first-level subtests PASS, none FAIL.

  - Covered: S3 in full. S1 for the rules of the review contract that concern the binding and where a result is stored: the digest and the changed paths are computed by engine code from git and never taken from a reply, every reply is held to the binding recomputed on that call (`reviewresult.Parse`, S2), and what cannot be checked refuses instead of passing (a git without `--attr-source`, a worktree path that is taken or at another commit, a reply above 1 MiB, a feature or a name that would leave `odd/review-results/`). S4-S10 are not touched.
  - Delivered, by ring (the four packages are registered in `engine/architecture_test.go`):
    - `engine/review` (domain): `Candidate` and `NewCandidate` (full names of one length, the SHA-256 and the size of the diff, the paths once each and valid UTF-8), the names (`CheckFeature`, `CheckRev`, `ResultName`, `ResultPath`, `NextNumber`, `WorktreePath`), `EncodeResult` (the stored file) and `Handoff` with `ParentFields` (the skeleton of the record of the chain).
    - `engine/review/app` (application): the ports `Git` and `Results`, and `Service.Bind` and `Service.Record` with their typed refusals (`ReplyRefusedError`, `ErrExists`, `ErrWorktreeElsewhere`, `ErrWorktreeForeign`, `ErrNoFreeNumber`).
    - `engine/review/gitexec` (adapter): git through a `Runner`, the canonical diff (`DiffArgs`, `PathsArgs`, `EmptyTreeSHA1`, `EmptyTreeSHA256`), `Locate` through `gitprov`, the worktree. `engine/review/fsstore` (adapter): the results directory, written atomically, never over a file, walked without following links.
    - `engine/cmd/review.go` (composition root, wired in `dispatch.go`), the usage block of `engine/cmd/usage.go` and `bin/lib/usage.sh`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`; `odd/review-results/README.md` rewritten (the two commands, the digest and the paths, what makes a result valid, the binding to the chain); the Worktrees line and Binding rules 1-2 of `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` (body 2,796 bytes, inside the 700-token lint budget); the comment of `Binding` in `engine/reviewresult/reviewresult.go`, which now describes the canonical diff.
    - Pins: `engine/skills/role_chain_artifact_test.go` (skill and README fragments), `engine/review/gitexec/readme_test.go` (the README must state the commands, the environment and the empty-tree constants the code uses), the `review` row of `engine/cmd/dispatch_routes_test.go` and of `engine/shelltest/overlay_dispatch_test.go`.
  - Decisions the prompt asked me to state:
    - The digest is the SHA-256 of one fixed `git diff-tree -p -r --full-index --binary --no-renames ...` run in the root of the working tree (the exact vector is `gitexec.DiffArgs`, written out in `odd/review-results/README.md` and pinned against it) under the environment of the process without any `GIT_*` variable and without `LC_ALL`, plus `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1` and `LC_ALL=C`. `--binary` is kept and the zlib level of a binary patch is pinned (`core.compression=0`, `core.looseCompression=0`); the zlib of the machine stays a residual. Attributes come from the empty tree (`--attr-source`), so no `.gitattributes` of the candidate or of the checkout decides what is binary; `$GIT_DIR/info/attributes` stays a residual. It needs git 2.40 or later: an older git fails and no digest is made. A digest that does not agree is refused by whoever recomputes it, never accepted.
    - The paths are `git diff-tree -r --name-only -z --no-renames --ignore-submodules=none`: NUL-terminated, byte for byte, a rename counted as a removal plus a creation (the L5 notes: `--no-renames` decided, `-z` because of `core.quotePath`). A `base` of 40 and a `head` of 64 characters are refused as `ErrInvalidCandidate`.
    - 150 KB is 150,000 bytes. A diff above it is bound and the worktree is made: the JSON says `over_limit: true` with a `note` and the exit is 0. A diff that changes no path is accepted: `paths: []` and the digest of no bytes.
    - The worktree is `<main-parent>/<main-name>-worktrees/review-<head12>`, beside the MAIN checkout whichever worktree the command runs in, made last (a candidate that cannot be bound leaves no directory) with the hooks of the repository off (`core.hooksPath=/dev/null`). One that is there at the head is reused unchanged (`worktree_created: false`); one at another commit (`ErrWorktreeElsewhere`) or anything else at the path (`ErrWorktreeForeign`) is refused and left as it is.
    - Exit codes: 0 bound or stored; 2 a reply that is refused (it does not hold to the binding, is not the object of the contract, or is above 1 MiB), with nothing written; 1 a usage error or any other failure. stdout is the binding JSON (`bind`) or the skeleton (`record`); stderr says where the result was stored and which fields the parent fills.
    - The stored file is `{"version":1,"binding":{base,head,diff_sha256,paths},"reply":<the reviewer's reply, verbatim>}`, indented with two spaces, mode 0644 in directories of mode 0755 whatever the umask says. The reviewed head is `binding.head` (equal to `reply.candidate.head`). The number is one more than the highest `<n>` among the names of the head (a verifier report `<head12>-<n>.verifier.md` holds its number too) and is retried up to 16 times when another writer takes it, so a number is not given again while a later one is there.
    - A revision is resolved with `rev-parse --verify --end-of-options <rev>^{commit}` (a tag is peeled); one that starts with `-` or holds a control character is refused before git is started. `--feature` is one segment of `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, 1 to 100 bytes, starting with a letter or a digit and without `..`. A relative `--result` is relative to the working directory of the process, not to `--cwd` (as `--file` of `roles validate`).
    - The skeleton: `from_role reviewer`, `payload_kind review-report`, `payload_sha256` the sha256 of the stored bytes, evidence `review-diff` (ref `<base>..<head>`, sha256 the `diff_sha256`) and `review-result` (ref the stored path, sha256 the stored bytes), `status completed`. `roles validate` refuses it until the parent fills `project_id`, `goal_id`, `chain_id`, `seq`, `prev_sha256`, `to_role` and `context.summary`.
  - Shell forwarder, for the parent: `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-labdrian-labdrian-sdd-overlay/0ab75d10-56e5-4782-b19c-76b1a65f1c29/scratchpad/t2-shell-forwarder.patch` (`git apply --check` from the repository root: exit 0). It adds `    review)          cmd_review "$@" ;;` after the `roles)` case of `bin/labdrian-overlay`, names `review` in the header comment of `bin/lib/engine_verbs.sh`, and adds `cmd_review` after `cmd_roles` (the same body: `die "engine binary not found at $ENGINE_BINARY; run 'labdrian install-hooks' first"` when the binary is not executable, else `exec "$ENGINE_BINARY" review "$@"`). With it applied in a scratch copy the three `TestOverlayDispatch/review/*` subtests pass and `shellcheck` finds nothing in the two files.

  - Covered: S3 in full. S1 for the rules of the review contract that concern the binding and where a result is stored (a result never leaves `odd/review-results/` and never replaces another, a number is not given twice, a worktree is reused only when it is the head, and what cannot be checked refuses instead of passing). S2 and S4-S10 are not touched.
  - Delivered, by file: `engine/review/app/service.go` (A1-A3; tests in `bind_test.go`, `fakes_test.go`, `record_test.go`); `engine/review/fsstore/fsstore.go` (the package comment, the errors, the hook, `Names` and `Create` as the common front of two stores), `held_linux.go` (linux: the store that holds the directories, with the raw `linkat`), `bypath.go` (the store that works by path), `portable.go` (`!linux`: by path), and the tests `fsstore_test.go`, `changes_internal_test.go` (an `impls()` table runs one property over every store of the platform), `held_linux_test.go`, `impls_linux_test.go`, `impls_other_test.go`; `engine/review/gitexec/gitexec.go` (G1-G3; tests in `gitexec_test.go` and `realgit_test.go`); `engine/architecture_test.go` (G4); `engine/cmd/review.go`, `usage.go`, `review_test.go` (the clock of A2, C1, C2); `README.md`, `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md`, `engine/skills/role_chain_artifact_test.go` (D1, D2).
  - Outcome per finding: all 16 fixed, none declined; F2 is closed on linux only (below). The ledger ids of the request are in parentheses; the tests that pin each fix are in the table at the end.
    - A1 (WARNING, `R4-002`, `R3-bind-worktree-toctou`): when `AddWorktree` fails, `Service.Bind` inspects the path once more; a worktree of this repository at the head that is there now (a concurrent bind made it between the first inspection and the add) is reused, with `worktree_created: false`, and anything else returns the error of the add unchanged. This supersedes the sentence of L7 that says the loser of two concurrent binds fails with git's message; L7 is not edited.
    - A2 (WARNING, `R4-001`): the tries to take a free number pause between them, 1 ms doubling up to a 50 ms cap, with none before the first try or after the last (15 pauses, 513 ms in all, for the 16 tries). The wait is injected (`Service.WithSleep`) and `app.New` waits for nothing by default. That differs from a wait built in, on purpose: the application ring may not read the clock (a call of `time.Sleep` in `review/app` fails `TestArchitectureFollowsTheDependencyRule`), so the program that composes the service hands the clock over (`reviewServiceOver` in `cmd/review.go` calls `WithSleep(time.Sleep)`), and a test through the verb pins that wiring.
    - A3 (WARNING, `R2-001`): `Service.store` returns unnamed results, `(int, string, error)`, so no result is shadowed. A refactor; the tests of `Record` are its net.
    - A4 (SUGGESTION, `R2-002`): the limit of 16 tries is pinned exactly: 16 creates, then `ErrNoFreeNumber`.
    - A5 (SUGGESTION, `R3-store-retry-exhaustion-untested`): a test where other writers take each number the store tries: three in a row (the result is number 4), 15 (the 16th and last try lands, as number 16) and 16 (`ErrNoFreeNumber`, only the files of the other writers stored); every try lists the directory again.
    - F1 (WARNING, `R3-names-unfiltered`): `Names` leaves out the temporary files of a write in flight (`.review-*.tmp`), in both stores; `.gitkeep`, `.review-1.tmp.bak` and `review-1.tmp` are still listed.
    - F2 (WARNING, `R1-001`, the window between looking at a directory and using it): closed on linux with the standard library only (`syscall` and `unsafe`; no new dependency, `golang.org/x/sys` is not in `go.mod`), not closed elsewhere. On linux the store (`held_linux.go`) opens `odd`, `odd/review-results` and the directory of the feature one below the other, each with `openat(O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC)` relative to the directory above (a link, a file or a dangling link answers ENOTDIR and is `ErrNotADirectory`), keeps the directory of the feature open for the whole call, and lists, stages and publishes relative to that descriptor: the temporary file is made there with `O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` and linked to its name with `linkat` between two descriptors (the standard library has no `linkat`, so it is the raw `SYS_LINKAT`, which fails with EEXIST and never replaces). No path is resolved after the walk, so a directory of the path that another process moves, or replaces with a link, after the store opened it cannot redirect what the store does. The root is opened as the caller gave it, links in it followed: it is the working tree the caller named. Elsewhere (`portable.go`, build tag `!linux`) the store works by path (`bypath.go`), because `syscall` has no `openat` or `linkat` on darwin and windows, `os.OpenRoot` needs a newer Go than the `go 1.21` of `go.mod`, and `atomicfile`, which the engine shares, works on paths. There the window stays, shorter and loud: the directories are looked at again (`os.Lstat` and `os.SameFile`) after a listing is read, right before the link that publishes a file and after it, and a directory that is no longer the one first seen is `ErrChanged` (after the link the file stays linked and the error says so). The package comment of `fsstore` states which store does what, and the limit. Evidence that the held store closes the window and the one by path does not (scratch, not committed): two goroutines move `odd` aside, put a link to a directory outside the tree in its place and put it back, while 20,000 creates run; in three runs the entries that appeared outside the results tree were 9, 17 and 11 by path, and 0, 0 and 0 held.
    - F3 (SUGGESTION, `R3-mkdir-race-untested`): the race of making a directory is tested at each of the three levels (`odd`, `odd/review-results`, the directory of the feature), over every store of the platform, with the hook of the store (`Store.race`, nil in `New()`) acting at the moment a directory has been found missing: another writer makes a real directory (its mode, 0700 in the test, is kept and the file is stored in it), or a link or a file appears in its place (`ErrNotADirectory`, with "is a symbolic link" or "is not a directory", and nothing written through it).
    - G1 (WARNING, `R3-001`): `Worktree` says that a path is taken (present, not a worktree of this repository) only when git answered, that is, ended with an exit status within its deadline. A git that could not be started, was killed by a signal, ran out of time (even if it ended with an exit status after the deadline) or gave too much output is an error that names the path and the command (`inspect <path>: git rev-parse --show-toplevel: signal: killed`), not a taken path.
    - G2 (WARNING, `R3-002`): `gitexec.DefaultTimeout` is 5 minutes and an `Options.Timeout` of zero or less means it, so every start of git, those of `Locate` and `Worktree` too, has a deadline. The options type says so.
    - G3 (SUGGESTION): the comment of `emptyTree` states its precondition: `head` must be a full object name (`gitobject.IsName`), since only its length tells SHA-1 from SHA-256, and a name of any other length is given the SHA-1 empty tree. Comment only.
    - G4 (SUGGESTION): the rows of `rings` in `engine/architecture_test.go` are in alphabetical order: the three `reviewreceipt` rows now come after `review`, `review/app`, `review/fsstore` and `review/gitexec`.
    - C1 (WARNING, `R3-001` of `cmd/review.go`): `writeReviewJSON` returns the error of the write to standard output instead of dropping it, and the verb ends with exit 1 and says what it had done: `error: review bind: the worktree is at <path>, but the binding could not be written: <cause>` and `error: review record: the result is stored at <path>, but the handoff could not be written: <cause>`. The worktree is made and the result is stored all the same, which is what the message says; before, both verbs exited 0 and said nothing.
    - C2 (SUGGESTION): the usage text derives the byte limit from `review.DiffLimitBytes` (`A diff over %d bytes is bound all the same`) in place of the literal 150000, and its exit clause says `exit 0 bound, 1 usage error, refusal or a binding that could not be written`. `bin/lib/usage.sh` says `exit 0 bound, 1 error`, which is still true, and was not touched.
    - D1 (WARNING, `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md:33`): the exit 2 clause reads "Exit 2 (`candidate` differs, `inspection.status` is not `completed`, `paths` differ or any other rule of the reviewer's contract breaks)", as the README does. The clause took the body to 713 tokens, 13 above the budget of the skill lint, so I trimmed eight phrases elsewhere in the body, none of them a rule: "Per hop, pipe a record" became "Pipe each record", the label "Estimator hop:" went, "with a `resume_reason`" became "with `resume_reason`", "and a second blind launch" became ", a second blind launch", "the one path" became "one path", "and the rows" became "and rows", "by `verifier` or by you" became "by `verifier` or you", and "the others keep saving" became "others keep saving". The body is now 2,797 bytes, 700 tokens by the estimate of the lint (the bytes divided by four, rounded up), which is the budget, with 3 bytes of slack.
    - D2 (SUGGESTION, `README.md:603-605`): the help paragraph of `record` is rewrapped to the width of the rest of the section, with the same words; the README sentence of `bind` names the binding that could not be written, like the usage text (`error, refusal or a binding that could not be written`).

The shell forwarder of T2 passes `review verify` through unchanged.
    - Prompt and pins: `roles/verifier/body.md` gains one line (the third of its delta: it replaces the return envelope with the verdict object, spelled out) and the three agent files are regenerated; `TestVerifierDeltaSpellsOutTheVerdictObjectItReturns` and `TestVerifierDeltaObjectIsTheOneTheEngineParses` in `engine/skills/role_chain_artifact_test.go` require `reviewverdict.Parse` to accept the object the prompt spells out, filled with every value the prompt lists, so the prompt and the parser cannot drift apart; the fragments of the README pin; the `review` row of `engine/cmd/dispatch_routes_test.go`.
  - Decisions the prompt asked me to state:
    - Where the round is recorded: by the stored verdict file itself, in `Service.Verify` (application ring), with no counter file. A candidate is the feature and the `base`; `review.IsCorrection` (domain) says two bindings are a try and its correction when the `base` is equal and the `head` differs. When a verdict blocks, `Verify` lists the directory of the feature, reads every verdict stored there with its result, decides each again, and if one that blocks is for another `head` of the same `base` it returns `ErrCorrectionSpent` (exit 1, nothing stored, the message names the verdict that spent the round, and the owner decides). A verdict that does not block never spends the round and is stored; an unreadable result or verdict is a refusal, never an unspent round. Documented in `odd/review-results/README.md` ("One correction round") and in the comment of `Verify`. Limits, stated there: a fix reviewed alone, as the range from the reviewed head to the fix, has another `base` and is another candidate that the rule does not see; the owner reopens the round by removing the verdict that spent it.
    - The stored verdict is `<head12>-<n>.verifier.json`, beside its result and under the same number: the reply of the verifier, indented with two spaces, characters as written, mode 0644, created atomically and never over a file (the `Create` of T2). The README of T2 and `skills/inception-pipeline/SKILL.md` named a prose report `<head12>-<n>.verifier.md` that no code ever wrote; the contract is JSON, so the name says so. `NextNumber` is unchanged and still counts any name that starts `<head12>-<n>.`, a verdict among them (a test pins it).
    - The decision (`reviewverdict.Decide`, one pure function, no I/O): a BLOCKER or CRITICAL the diff caused (`introduced`, `behavior-activated`, `worsened`) blocks only when `result` is `reproduced`; `not-reproduced` is nothing; `not-run` is unverified. `pre-existing` and `base-only` never block: carried. `unknown` is read on `base` first: `result` `not-reproduced` is nothing, `base_result` `reproduced` is carried, `base_result` `not-run` is unverified, `base_result` `not-reproduced` is decided like a finding the diff caused. So an `unknown` finding reproduced on head with its base check not run is unverified, not blocking: "not on base" was not shown, and a check that cannot run never passes. WARNING is fixed before delivery and SUGGESTION carried, whatever the verdict. A finding with no verdict is read as a check that did not run. The outcome is `blocks` when anything blocks, else `unverified`, else `clear`, and `Decision` lists the ids of each kind in the order of the result.
    - `reviewverdict.Parse` applies its rules in one fixed order and reports the first broken, so the same reply is always refused for the same reason: one JSON value in valid UTF-8 with no key written twice, the shape (no member outside the contract, matched by exact name; every member present and none null; each of its type), then per verdict an id the result has (`ErrUnknownID`) that no earlier verdict answered (`ErrDuplicateID`), a `result` and a `base_result` in the enumeration (`ErrOutsideEnum`), an `output` that is not blank and a `command` whenever a check ran on head or on base (`ErrMissingEvidence`), and finally every finding answered (`ErrUnanswered`).
    - Exit codes of `verify`: 0 stored (a directory that could not be flushed is a warning on stderr and the verdict is not stored again, as `record`); 2 a reply that is refused (it is not the object of the contract, does not answer the stored result, or is above 1 MiB), with nothing written; 1 a usage error or any other failure, among them a result that already has a verdict and a spent round. stdout is the skeleton of `record` for the same result plus the evidence `verifier-report` (the stored verdict and its sha256) and `to_role` filled: `builder` when the review blocks, `delivery` when it is clear, none when a check did not run (the parent fills it once the check has run, and stderr names `to_role` among the members the parent fills only then).
    - `Verify` reads the stored result as `record` wrote it (`DecodeResult`) and holds its reply to its stored binding again with `reviewresult.Parse`; it does not recompute `diff_sha256` from git (git is asked only where the repository is), which `review check` of T4 does. `--stored` is one file name of the feature: `CheckResultName` refuses a path, a verdict name or a number below 1 before anything is read.
    - `Store.Read` works by path on every platform: the walk that follows no link, `Lstat` of the file (a link or a directory is `ErrNotAFile`), the open, the check that what was opened is what was looked at, a read of at most `MaxStoredBytes` (8 MiB, `ErrTooLarge`), and a second look at the directories after the read (`ErrChanged`, the bytes dropped). It does not hold the directories as `Create` does on linux: a read cannot write outside the tree.
  - Simplified, because of the owner's correction ("me gustaria que entregue unidades hasta 400 no que se 900 da mucha chance de mandar locuras recuerda que el contrato de simplicidad debe mantener simple los desarrollos", relayed by the parent): the smallest thing that satisfies S4, reusing the packages and guards of T2. No `Verdicts` type (`Parse` returns `[]Verdict`); no wrapper around the stored verdict and no stored outcome (the stored file is the verifier's reply and the decision is made again from the result and the reply whenever it is needed); no counter file for the round; no new port (`Results` gained the one method `Read`) and no held-directory read; one flag parser for the three verbs (`parseReviewFlags` takes the flags each verb accepts, so a flag of another verb is still refused); no new error type for the exit code (the `ReplyRefusedError` of T2 carries exit 2 for `verify` too); `engine/roles` and `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` untouched. `recordFailed` of `record` became `reviewFailed(p, verb, err)`, one failure path for `record` and `verify`, and the comments of the new packages were trimmed to what the code does not say.

V2 (a stored result edited in place is trusted) goes to T4/S5; V3 (a verdict without its result blocks every later blocking verdict of the feature, failing closed) and V4 (a second launch on the original head can block after its correction cleared, as `IsCorrection` says) are advisories. Done inline: `Results.Lock(root, feature)` (application port); `Verify` takes it only when the verdict blocks, before the stored verdicts are listed, and gives it back after the verdict is stored; `fsstore` on linux opens the directory of the feature as `Names` does (no link followed, nothing made) and takes an exclusive `flock(2)`, retried on EINTR; elsewhere it holds none and says so. RED: `go test ./review/app/`: `the store was asked ["Names" "Create"], want ["Lock" "Names" "Create" "Unlock"]`; `go test ./review/fsstore/`: `Store.Lock undefined`, build failed. GREEN: `go test -count=1 ./review/...` ok; `GOOS=darwin go vet ./review/...` and `GOOS=windows go build ./review/...` ok. Load-bearing: with the `flock` call replaced by `nil`, `TestLockHoldsTheDirectoryOfTheFeatureUntilItIsGivenBack` FAILs. End to end (`race.sh`, the verifier's probe helpers, a fresh `mktemp -d` per run): the `14ba5163` binary stored both verdicts in 16 of 20 runs; the new binary in 0 of 60, exactly one stored in each.

  - Decisions the prompt asked me to state, with their evidence:
    - What `roles append` holds: only a completed `review-report`. One that is `interrupted` stored nothing, and a record of another kind is not about a review; both are stored as before and the working directory is not asked for them (`TestRolesAppend*`; differential 10, 11). Exactly one `review-diff` and one `review-result`, at most one `verifier-report`; a missing or repeated one is refused. The ref of the `review-result` must be exactly one `odd/review-results/<feature>/<head12>-<n>.json` and its sha256 the sha256 of the file stored there now, read as `verify` reads it (a link, a file that is not a regular one and a file above 8 MiB are refused) and still a result that holds to its binding. The `review-diff` must be `<base>..<head>` of the binding stored in that result, with that binding's `diff_sha256`. The `verifier-report`, when given, must be the verdict stored beside that result with its sha256. The shape of the record and of every ref is checked before the repository is asked, so a record wrong by its shape reads nothing. The message names the kind and the ref: `error: roles append: evidence review-result "odd/review-results/own-rdd/<head12>-1.json": differs from the stored file: its sha256 is ... and the stored file's is ...`. Exit 1, nothing stored.
    - The repository of that check is the one that holds the working directory of the process, and `roles append` does not recompute the diff from git: it compares the record with the stored files, which `review check` then compares with git. `payload_sha256` of the record is not compared with the stored file, because S5 asks for the evidence only (see Open questions).
    - What `review check` clears (rule R1, fail closed): the head is cleared when at least one stored result of the feature binds it and every one of them is clear. A result binds the head by its `binding.head`, not by its file name; the stored binding (`base`, `head`, `diff_sha256`, `paths`) must equal the candidate that git gives now for the base the result names and the head being delivered, recomputed by the same `Service.bind` as `bind` and `record`; its reply is held to the binding again with `reviewresult.Parse`, as `verify` holds it; the verdict stored beside it, when there is one, is read and held to the result with `reviewverdict.Parse`, and `reviewverdict.Decide` decides (with no verdict, `Decide(result, nil)`: a severe finding the diff may have caused counts as a check that did not run, so the result is not clear). A result that blocks keeps the head blocked whatever was recorded after it: a verdict that reproduced a severe finding is not undone by another launch on the same head, and a blocked head goes back to the builder and comes back as another head. A result that is not clear keeps the head from delivery too, before or after a clear one. A file named for the head that cannot be read or is not a result any more, a verdict that is stored and cannot be read, and any failure of git or of the store refuse the check. The refusal wraps `ErrNoResult`, `ErrBlocked`, `ErrNotClear` or `ErrBindingDiffers` and names the result, the verdict and the finding ids.
    - Exit codes of `check`: 0 cleared; 1 for a usage error, a refusal and any other failure. The prompt said usage errors exit 2; the code and the tests of the three review verbs give 2 only to a refused reply (`ReplyRefusedError`), and `check` has no reply, so I kept 1 for every refusal and said it in the help (`exit 0 cleared, 1 usage error, refusal or any other failure`). stdout of exit 0 is one JSON object: `head` (full name), `outcome` (`clear`) and `results`, one for each result used, with `result`, `verdict` (when the verifier ran), `base`, `diff_sha256` and, when there are any, `fix` and `carry`. Nothing is stored.
    - `check` takes no `--base`: it is by head, and the base of each result is the one that result names. A candidate that was split into one launch per commit has a result for each head and the check clears the head it is given by the results of that head alone.
    - The delivery role: line 9 of `roles/delivery/body.md` no longer says `verification is optional, and a missing or failed report never blocks the close`, which says the opposite of S5. It now says: never rewrite checkboxes, earlier Log entries or history; before appending the entry run `labdrian review check --feature <feature>` in the repository, with `--head <rev>` when the prompt names the head being delivered; on exit 1 close nothing and return status blocked with its stderr line; on exit 0 name the head and the results it prints in the entry. The role already has `Bash` (the tools test requires it). `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` is not edited: its Binding section is at the 700-token lint limit, its text already says `Delivery recomputes diff_sha256 and trusts the record only on equality` (pinned), and editing it would make its approval stale. The design note `per-role-reviewer.md` (section 4) says how delivery finds out.

  - Open questions (not decided by me):
    - A feature delivered with no stored result for its head is now blocked by `delivery` (`no stored result binds the head`), which S5 asks for. The small and passive paths of ODD that skip review will need a recorded clean result, or the parent closes the document itself. Whether those paths are exempt is the owner's decision.
    - Rule R1 refuses a head when any stored result of it is not clear, even if a later launch is clear. The alternatives are R2 (any clear result clears) and R3 (a block is sticky, otherwise the latest launch decides). R1 is the one that never lets a check that did not run or a reproduced finding be overtaken by a later launch; the cost is that a head with an unverified launch needs its `verify` or another head.
    - `roles append` does not compare `payload_sha256` with the stored result (S5 names the evidence), and holds the `verifier-report` to the digest of the stored verdict only; `review check` is the one that parses the verdict. Holding `payload_sha256` too is one more rule in `review.CheckFile` terms.
    - `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` has no sentence for `review check` (as L10 said for `verify`): its Binding section is at the 700-token lint limit, so adding one means trimming another and makes the approval of `role-chain` stale until the owner runs `labdrian skills approve --id role-chain --approver <name>`.

- **L15** (2026-10-27, owner), to "should every feature need a stored review result to close, or do we add an explicit exception for work that takes no review?": "y buenos hay labros que no llevan review que ya cone l verifier basta por validacion del riesgo tambien". Open: how the exemption is evidenced; T4 reopened until it is decided.

- **L16** (2026-10-27, owner), to the proposed `review exempt` design (verifier report on stdin, bound to `diff_sha256`, high risk never exempt, a blocking review wins, risk declared not computed): "si vamos con ese". S5 rewritten with it; T4 reopened.

The commit field of T4 now also lists the 15 commits below, on `feat/own-rdd-t4` after `8e49d921`, and the commit that records this entry. I made no push, no pull request and no branch change, put no attribution in any message, left `odd/review-receipts/own-rdd/` alone and did not run `skills approve`. No `SKILL.md` changed (nothing under `skills/` changed), so no approval record is stale. All 55 files changed are inside the edit surface I was given.

  - Covered: S5 in full, as L16 rewrote it. (1) `labdrian review exempt --feature F --base B --head H --risk <none|items> [--cwd <repo>]` reads the report of the verifier on stdin, and from nowhere else, and stores `odd/review-results/<feature>/<head12>-<n>.exempt.json` bound to the `diff_sha256` of the head; it refuses a declared risk that names any high-risk item (1-6), a missing report and a report that is not clear. (2) `labdrian review check` clears a head with a non-blocking review result or with a valid exemption bound to it, and a result that stops the head wins over any exemption. (3) `labdrian roles append` holds a completed `review-exemption` record to the stored exemption, the way it holds a `review-report` to its result. (4) The verifier role says how to return the report of an exemption, and the delivery role names the exemptions `review check` prints. S1 for these rules: a check that cannot run refuses and never passes (see "Fail closed at check time"). S2-S4 are used as they are; S6-S10 are not touched.
  - Units, in commit order (changed lines = additions plus deletions; the 15 generated lines of `cf42ca7a` and this document are not counted). Each commit is one behavior with its tests, help and docs, and was built, vetted and tested on its own (see "Commits standing alone"):

  - Delivered, by ring:
    - Domain, `engine/reviewverdict`: `report.go` (`ParseReport`, `Report`, `Check`, `CheckResult` with `CheckPassed`, `CheckFailed` and `CheckNotRun`, `Report.Clear`, `ErrNotClear`). `engine/reviewresult`: `Binding.Check`, which was unexported, is exported.
    - Domain, `engine/review`: `exempt.go` (`NoRisk`, `CheckRisk`, `CheckReport`, `CheckExemption`, `ErrInvalidRisk`, `ErrHighRisk`); `stored.go` (`EncodeExemption`, `DecodeExemption`, `StoredExemption`); `handoff.go` (`ExemptionHandoff`, `PayloadExemption`, `EvidenceExemption`); `names.go` (`ExemptionName`, `CheckExemptionName`, `ErrInvalidExemptionName`, and one `stem` rule for the three kinds of file of a head); `check.go` (`ExemptionsOfHead`, `Exempted`, `Cleared`); `evidence.go` (`ExemptionRef`, the kind of the record in `RequiredEvidence`, `NeedsEvidence` true for a completed `review-exemption`).
    - Application, `engine/review/app`: `Service.Exempt` and `Exemption`; `Service.Check` reads the exemptions of the head (`exemptedHead`) and `Checked.Exemptions`; `CheckEvidence` holds a `review-exemption` record (`checkExemptionEvidence`); `store` takes the name from its caller. No new port: `Git` and `Results` as T3 left them (`Create`, `Read`, `Names`).
    - Root, `engine/cmd`: the verb `review exempt` (`runReviewExempt`, `reportExempted`) and `reviewCheckJSON.Exemptions`; `roles append` names the kind of record it could not check; `usage.go` and `bin/lib/usage.sh`; the words of a missing or unknown `review` verb now list `exempt`.
    - Prompt: `roles/verifier/body.md` (the delta is 5 lines now) and `roles/delivery/body.md` line 9, `engine/agentgen/roles.go` (the description of the verifier), and the six generated files.
  - Decisions the prompt asked me to state, with their evidence (M-numbers are the mutants below):
    - What is stored. `<head12>-<n>.exempt.json` is `{"version": 1, "binding": {"base", "head", "diff_sha256", "paths"}, "risk": "none", "report": {...the report as the verifier gave it...}}`, written like a result (two-space indent, one final newline). The binding is the candidate git gives now, computed by the one function `bind`, `record` and `check` use (a test compares the questions `Exempt` and `Record` ask git, in order), and never read from the report. The number is the next free one of the head among results, verdicts and exemptions (`NextNumber` counts every `<head12>-<n>.` name; `store` takes the name from the caller, `Record` passes `ResultName`, `Exempt` passes `ExemptionName`), created with `Results.Create`, so never over an existing file, and retried when another writer took the number. Evidence: e2e `a second exemption of the same head takes the next number` (`b4ce11fca46a-1.exempt.json`, then `-2`); `TestRecordStillStoresAResultUnderItsOwnNameBesideAnExemption`; M20, M21.
    - The risk. The agent declares it and the engine never computes it (L16). The grammar is `none`, or the numbers 1 to 6 separated by commas with no space and none twice. A declaration that names an item is refused as `review.ErrHighRisk` and names every item in the order written (`the declared risk names 2 (security), 4 (concurrency)`); anything else (`None`, `2, 4`, an empty value, a number outside 1-6, a repeat) is `review.ErrInvalidRisk`, so a declaration the engine cannot read is never taken for the absence of risk. Both exit 1 and store nothing, and the risk is held before the report is looked at, so a high-risk declaration is refused even with a clear report. Evidence: e2e (below); M01-M04, M17.
    - The report. Read from stdin only: `--report`, `--result` and `--stored` are unknown flags of this verb and a positional argument is refused. At most `review.MaxReplyBytes` (1 MiB, as a reply). Parsed by `reviewverdict.ParseReport`, strict as the verdicts are: one JSON value in valid UTF-8, no key twice, no member the contract does not name (matched by exact name), every member present and none null, `result` one of `pass`, `fail` and `not-run`, `command` and `output` never blank. It is clear when it names at least one check and every check is `pass`: `not-run` never passes and a report that names no check is not clear. A report that is refused is a `*ReplyRefusedError`, exit 2 and nothing stored, as a refused reply of `record` and `verify` is; any other failure, a refused risk included, is exit 1. Evidence: e2e (five refusals, exit 2); M05-M08, M18, M19, M28.
    - What clears a head (`review.Cleared`). A head with no stored result and at least one exemption that holds is cleared. A head with a stored result is decided by its results alone, as before: a result that blocks wins over any exemption (L16), and so does a result that does not block and is not clear either (a severe finding no verifier checked), because an exemption is for work that takes no review and is not a way around a review that left something unchecked (open question 1). For a head with no exemption the answer is word for word what `Clearance` gave (a test compares them). `exempt` does not look for a result of the head; `check` decides, whatever was recorded first. Evidence: e2e `check: the blocking result wins over the exemption` (exit 1, names the result, the verdict and `F1`); M09, M10.
    - Fail closed at check time (S1). `Service.Check` reads every `<head12>-<n>.exempt.json` of the head again: it decodes the file strictly (`DecodeExemption`: one object, no key twice, no member the file does not have, version 1, a risk and a report), holds it to the rules it was made under (`CheckExemption`: the binding names a candidate first, so a base such as `--output=x` never reaches git; then the risk; then the report) and recomputes the candidate from git for the base the file names and the head being delivered (`CheckBinding`: base, head, `diff_sha256` and paths). An exemption that does not hold refuses the head even when another file would clear it, as a result that does not hold does; one stored under the name of this head with another head's binding, or the reverse, never clears it; a failure of git or of the store refuses. Evidence: e2e: the file edited to `"risk": "2"` is refused with `a change that names a high-risk item is never exempt from review: the declared risk names 2 (security)`, and edited to a failed check with `the report is not clear: checks[0] ("go test ./...") is fail`; `TestCheckDoesNotPassTheForgedBaseOfAnExemptionToGit`; `TestCheckNeverClearsAHeadByTheExemptionOfAnotherHead`; M12-M16.
    - The output of `check`. `{"head", "outcome": "clear", "results": [...], "exemptions": [{"exemption", "base", "diff_sha256"}]}`: `results` is `[]` (it printed `null` for a head cleared by an exemption alone, the first RED of U5) and `exemptions` is left out when there is none, so a head cleared by results prints byte for byte what it printed before (differential: `check-clear`, `check-default`, `check-after-verify`, `check-blocked` identical). Evidence: M29, M30.
    - The record that closes the exemption. `exempt` prints on stdout the skeleton for `roles append --stdin`: `from_role` `reviewer`, `to_role` `delivery`, `payload_kind` `review-exemption`, `payload_sha256` the sha256 of the stored file, evidence `review-diff` (`<base>..<head>` and the sha256 of the diff) and `review-exemption` (the path and the sha256 of the stored file), status `completed`; stderr names what the parent still fills (`project_id, goal_id, chain_id, seq, prev_sha256, context.summary`; `to_role` is not among them because the skeleton names it). The record is the reviewer's because the reviewer is not an optional role: `roles.IsValidTransition` has no builder-to-delivery transition, so the record that closes an exemption takes the reviewer's slot and its `payload_kind` tells it apart (open question 4). Evidence: e2e: after builder-to-reviewer at seq 3, `roles append` of the filled skeleton at seq 4 exits 0 and `roles resume` says role `delivery`, terminal; M25-M27.
    - `roles append`. A completed `review-exemption` is held to the stored exemption as a completed `review-report` is held to its result: exactly one `review-diff` and one `review-exemption`, each given once, their shape checked before the repository is located; the ref of the second exactly one `odd/review-results/<feature>/<head12>-<n>.exempt.json` (`ExemptionRef`); its sha256 the digest of the bytes stored now; the stored file still an exemption that holds to its rules, so an exemption edited after the record was made is refused for what it now says even when the record carries the digest of the edited bytes; and the `review-diff` equal to the range and the diff digest of the stored binding. An interrupted record, and a record of any other kind, are stored as before without the working directory being asked. The diff is not computed again there: that the head being delivered is the one exempted is `check`'s. The message names the kind and the ref, and when the check could not run it names the kind of the record (it said `review-report` for a `review-exemption` before `099abe1f`). Evidence: e2e (below); M31-M38.
    - The roles. `roles/verifier/body.md` has a fourth line in its delta: when the prompt lists no finding id and names the checks, the whole reply is the object `{"checks":[{"command","result","output"}]}` with one entry for each check named, in the order run, `command` and `output` never empty and `not-run` never a pass. A test fills the object the line spells out with every value of `result` and requires `reviewverdict.ParseReport` to accept it (and an empty `command` or `output` to be refused), so the prompt and the parser cannot drift apart. `roles/delivery/body.md` line 9 now ends `on exit 0 name the head and the results it prints, and the exemptions when it prints any, in the entry`. The three agent files of each role are the output of `gadu-generate`. `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` is not edited.
    - The documents. The help of the engine and the help of the wrapper (`bin/lib/usage.sh`) for `review exempt`, `review check` and `roles append` say the same (pair rows in `TestWrapperUsageOfReviewVerifySaysWhatTheEngineUsageSays`); the README, `odd/review-results/README.md` and CHANGELOG say what the exemption is, how `check` clears by it, which result wins, and that an exemption grants nothing by itself; two tests pin the documents.
  - Simplified: the smallest thing that satisfies L16, reusing T2-T4. No new port and no new adapter. No stored outcome and no counter: whether a head is cleared is decided again from the stored files at every `check` and every `roles append`. No `--report` flag, no reason or scope field, no list of required checks, no time or order between an exemption and a result. One flag parser and one `parentFills` for the verbs; one name rule (`stem`) and one ref rule (`storedRef`) for results, verdicts and exemptions; one `encodeFile` and one `decodeFile` for the files of results and of exemptions.
