  - Commits standing alone: each of the 15 commits was checked out in a scratch clone that has the whole history (`git clone` of the repository, then `git checkout --detach <hash>`), and `go build ./... && go vet ./...` and `go test -count=1` of `./review/... ./reviewresult/... ./reviewverdict/... ./roles/... .` ran on it, plus `./cmd/ ./shelltest/` for the commits that change `engine/cmd` or `bin/lib/usage.sh`, and `./skills/ ./agentgen/` for the commits that change the prompts, the agent files, the documents or the tests of `skills`: all 15 `OK`, `fail=0` (`final17/percommit17.out`). My first idea was to export the parent trees with `git archive` of `engine`, `archguard` and `identity` only; that is enough for the packages of `review` and `reviewverdict`, but `go test ./cmd/` fails in such an export (29 to 31 failing lines, a reason I did not investigate) and passes in the clone, so the safety nets and the REDs of the commits that change `cmd` or `skills` below were run in the clone.
  - End to end with the program built from `7f0bbb61` (`go build -buildvcs=false -o <scratch>/engine-head ./cmd`; the script `u-e2e.sh` in a `mktemp -d` repository of two commits with `GIT_CONFIG_*` isolated and `HOME` and `XDG_STATE_HOME` in the scratch directory). The run was made twice, with the build I made earlier in the session (`engine-new`) and with this build, and the two outputs are identical apart from the scratch path (`diff` of the two outputs, scratch lines removed: no difference). `exempt` with `--risk 2`: exit 1, `error: review exempt: review: a change that names a high-risk item is never exempt from review: the declared risk names 2 (security)`; with `--risk 2,4`: exit 1, `... names 2 (security), 4 (concurrency)`; with `--risk "2, 4"` and with `--risk None`: exit 1, `error: review exempt: review: invalid risk declaration: "2, 4" is not "none" or a list of the numbers 1 to 6 of the high-risk items, separated by commas with no space and none written twice (for example "2,4")`. With `--risk none` and a report with a failed check, a check that did not run, no check, text that is not JSON, and an empty stdin: five times exit 2, `error: review exempt: the reply is refused and nothing was stored: reviewverdict: the report is not clear: checks[0] ("go test ./...") is fail` (and `is not-run`, `it names no check, so nothing was checked`, `unparseable reply: invalid character 'o' in literal null (expecting 'u')`, `unparseable reply: empty JSON document`); nothing was stored after the nine refusals (the listing of the results directory was empty). `review check` of the head before any exemption: exit 1, `error: review check: head b4ce11fca46a988ce6a3f2cbf3e3c4d4c93b6cc9: review: no stored result binds the head`. `exempt --risk none` with a report of two passing checks: exit 0, stored `odd/review-results/f1/b4ce11fca46a-1.exempt.json` (sha256 `a7ef401279aa0ddf314fa5bb4f8aeb55960f4cde2f106c78a0b4735b1019eb9a`), stdout the skeleton (`from_role` `reviewer`, `to_role` `delivery`, `payload_kind` `review-exemption`, the evidence `review-diff` and `review-exemption`), stderr the three lines (`stored ...`, `the head is exempted from review: the declared risk is none and the report of the verifier is clear; 'review check' clears it unless a stored result blocks it`, `stdout is the skeleton of the record for 'roles append --stdin'; the parent of the chain fills project_id, goal_id, chain_id, seq, prev_sha256, context.summary`). `check`: exit 0, stdout one JSON object with `"outcome": "clear"`, `"results": []` and `"exemptions"` holding the file, the base `aaf71967643a325371bb7d0a48b66332aeeb161b` and the `diff_sha256` `32d17579c2b3ea753f4611f378fc46c835ccf5c2e3cd7b28a50899edb8253fa8`. A second `exempt` of the same head: exit 0, stored `b4ce11fca46a-2.exempt.json`. `roles append` of the filled skeleton on a chain of three records: with the sha256 of the `review-exemption` replaced by `bbbb...`, exit 1, `error: roles append: evidence review-exemption "odd/review-results/f1/b4ce11fca46a-1.exempt.json": differs from the stored file: its sha256 is bbbb... and the stored file's is a7ef4012...`; with the `review-exemption` left out, exit 1, `error: roles append: evidence review-exemption: missing: a completed review-exemption record carries one`; the record as `exempt` printed it, exit 0, stored as `000004.json`, and `roles resume`: role `delivery`, `terminal: true`. Then `record` of a result with a BLOCKER finding for the same head (exit 0, `b4ce11fca46a-3.json`) and `verify` with the finding reproduced (exit 0, `b4ce11fca46a-3.verifier.json`, `the review blocks`, `blocking: F1`): `check` exit 1, `error: review check: head b4ce11fca46a988ce6a3f2cbf3e3c4d4c93b6cc9: review: a stored result blocks the head: odd/review-results/f1/b4ce11fca46a-3.json (verdict odd/review-results/f1/b4ce11fca46a-3.verifier.json): blocking findings F1`. In another feature, an exemption stored and then edited: `"risk": "2"` gives `check` exit 1, `error: review check: odd/review-results/f2/b4ce11fca46a-1.exempt.json: review: a change that names a high-risk item is never exempt from review: the declared risk names 2 (security)`; with the risk put back and the first check turned to `fail`, exit 1, `error: review check: odd/review-results/f2/b4ce11fca46a-1.exempt.json: reviewverdict: the report is not clear: checks[0] ("go test ./...") is fail`.
  - Differential check (a build of `8e49d921` and a build of `7f0bbb61`, both built in scratch with `-buildvcs=false`; the same 34 invocations over two identical repositories with `GIT_CONFIG_*`, `HOME` and `XDG_STATE_HOME` isolated; stdout, stderr, exit code, the stored `odd/` tree and the role-chain store compared after the scratch path was replaced by `<ROOT>`). The invocations: the usage with no arguments and with an unknown command; `review` with no verb and with an unknown verb; `bind`, and `bind --risk none`; `record` (clean, with `--risk none`, a reply that is refused); `check` (cleared, default head, with `--risk none`, a feature with nothing, a head with no result); `verify` (clean, with `--risk none`) and `check` after it; a result that blocks (`record`, `verify` reproduced, `check`); the setup of three role chains (nine `roles append`) and, over them, `roles append` of a `review-report` with the files `record` printed, the same with another file's digest, and a record of another kind; `roles resume` twice. 30 identical, including the stored files and the exit codes, and `--risk` given to `bind`, `record`, `verify` and `check` is refused as an unknown flag exactly as on the base. 4 different, all on stderr, exit code 1 in both: the usage with no arguments and with an unknown command (23 lines added and 7 removed: the block of `review exempt` is new, and the paragraphs of `review check` and of `roles append` now say what an exemption does) and the two words of a `review` with no verb and with an unknown verb, which now list `exempt` (`review requires a verb: bind, record, verify, check, exempt` and `review: unknown verb "bogus" (expected bind, record, verify, check or exempt)`). Those four are the change that was asked for.
  - Mutation pass, to prove the new tests load-bearing: 38 one-fragment mutants of the production files (`review/exempt.go`, `check.go`, `names.go`, `stored.go`, `handoff.go`, `evidence.go`; `review/app/exempt.go`, `check.go`, `evidence.go`; `reviewverdict/report.go`; `cmd/review.go`, `cmd/roles_evidence.go`), each applied alone to a scratch export, run against `./review/... ./reviewverdict/...` and then, if those passed, `./cmd/ -run 'Review|Roles|Exempt|Evidence|Check'`, and restored after: 38 killed, 0 survived, 0 that did not apply or did not compile. The export equals the tree of `7f0bbb61` for `review`, `reviewverdict`, `reviewresult`, `roles`, `cmd/review.go`, `cmd/roles_evidence.go` and `cmd/usage.go` (`diff -rq` and `cmp`: no difference). They cover the risk grammar (M01-M04), the report (M05-M08), what clears a head and the re-check of a stored exemption (M09-M16), the verb and the use case (M17-M20), the numbering and the names (M21-M22), the stored file (M23-M24), the skeleton and the output (M25-M30) and `roles append` (M31-M38). The runner and the list are in `mutrunner/` of the scratch directory.
  - Risk: items 2 and 3. Item 2: `review check` is the guard of delivery and `exempt` adds a way through it for work that took no review, on a risk the engine cannot compute. The engine refuses what it can read (every declared high-risk item, a declaration it cannot read, a report that is missing, malformed or not clear), re-checks every stored exemption from the file and from git at `check` and at `roles append`, and lets a result that stops the head win over any exemption; a revision or a stored base that git could read as an option never reaches git (M15, `TestCheckDoesNotPassTheForgedBaseOfAnExemptionToGit`). What it cannot refuse is a `--risk none` declared for risky work with a clear report whose checks prove little: that is the design of L16 (open questions 2 and 3). Item 3: `review check` prints a new member (`exemptions`, left out when there is none) and `results: []` instead of `null` for a head cleared by an exemption alone; `roles append` now holds a completed `review-exemption`, a kind of record it used to store without a check; `reviewresult.Binding.check` became the exported `Check` (a rename of the one definition `Parse` applies first); the usage of the engine and of the wrapper and the words of a missing or unknown `review` verb changed; the prompts of the verifier and of delivery are mirrored into six files. The differential shows 30 of 34 invocations identical, and the 4 that differ are the intended usage and verb words.
  - Open questions (not decided by me):
    1. A result that does not block but is not clear (a severe finding no verifier checked) keeps the head from delivery even when an exemption exists (`Cleared`). L16 says only that a blocking review wins; I read an exemption as "this work takes no review", not as a way around a review that was started and left something unchecked. The alternative is that an exemption clears such a head. Which does the owner want?
    2. The risk is the declaration of the agent (L16), so `--risk none` on a risky diff is accepted when the report is clear: the engine has nothing to compare it with. Whether the declaration should be tied to something the engine can read (the Risk line in the feature document, a second signature of the parent) is the owner's decision.
    3. A report is clear with one passing check of any command, so `{"checks":[{"command":"true","result":"pass","output":"ok"}]}` exempts a head. The prompt of the parent names the checks and the engine cannot know which suffice; whether `exempt` should require a minimum (the test command of the feature, say) is a product decision. The report is evidence the agent pipes in, not proof that the verifier ran it, as the replies of `record` and `verify` are.
    4. The record that closes an exemption is the reviewer's (`reviewer` to `delivery`) because the reviewer is not an optional role and the chain has no builder-to-delivery transition, so the chain says `reviewer` for a head nobody reviewed; `payload_kind` `review-exemption` is what tells them apart. Making the reviewer optional for exempted work changes the role state machine (`engine/roles`) and is not part of this task.
    5. `roles append` holds the evidence of a `review-exemption` record and not its `payload_sha256` (the skeleton fills it with the digest of the stored file, as for a result); as in L14, S5 names the evidence only.
    6. `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` has no sentence for `review exempt` (skill id `role-chain`): its Binding section is at the 700-token limit, so adding one means trimming another and makes its approval stale until the owner runs `labdrian skills approve --id role-chain --approver <name>`. I did not touch it.
    7. Five of the 15 commits are above the budget of about 400 lines that the owner set for a native review (by 8 to 59 lines, with 262 to 357 of their lines tests). I did not split or cut them; if the native review of one of them reports its context budget as exceeded, the parent decides how to review it.
    8. The Engram mirror `odd/own-rdd/tasks` is not updated by me; the parent mirrors this document.

  - Decisions the prompt asked me to state:
    - `--base` is optional. Without it `check` is the single-head check and answers byte for byte as before (the pin, and the differential below); with it the head is cleared only by the chain. It is the smallest change that keeps every existing invocation as it was.
    - The walk goes back from the head. At a commit it holds what `Check` holds for a head (`cleared`: the files read again, the binding recomputed from git, `review.Cleared`), takes the base of the first result that binds it (the first exemption when there is none) as the next commit, and is done when that base is the resolved delivery base. It always holds the head's own range first, so a `--base` equal to the head cannot clear with no range at all (probe: refused at the oldest commit).
    - It refuses a gap (no result or exemption ends at a commit), a range that is not clear or does not hold, git or the store failing, and a loop. The bound is the set of commits already passed (`review.ErrChainLoops`), not a count from `git rev-list`: the `Git` port has no such call and a new one costs an adapter and its tests. Each step takes a commit not passed before, so the walk ends. A range that begins before the base walks past it and ends in a gap at an older commit. No ancestry is asked of git, and soundness does not need it: each hop is a diff between two trees that was reviewed and recomputed from git, and hops that meet end to end compose to the diff from the base to the head.
    - Several results at one commit must all be clear (`review.Cleared`, as for a single head) and the first launch's base chooses the way. That is incomplete and never unsound: where another result's base would have led to the delivery base the chain is refused. No test pins the tie-break between a result and an exemption of one commit (the result's base wins).
    - Output with `--base`: `{head, base, outcome, ranges}`, `ranges` from the one that starts at the base, each `{base, head, results, exemptions}` with the entries `check` prints (`results` is `[]` for a range that an exemption alone clears, `exemptions` is left out when there is none). A refusal is `error: review check: the chain from <base> to <head>: <the refusal of the commit where it stopped>`, exit 1, nothing on stdout, nothing stored.
    - Delivery body: `--base <rev>` with the integration base the prompt names, or the one that suits the project, or the merge base when it moved on after the branch was made; the entry names the results and exemptions of each range. The pins of the delivery role (`engine/skills/role_agents_artifact_test.go`, outside my surface) pass unchanged; the new clause has no pin of its own.
    - Four existing tests changed on purpose, no new test function beyond the six asked: `TestReviewCheckRefusesACommandLineItCannotRead` had a row that pinned `unknown flag "--base"` for `check`, now `the base twice` (`--base given twice`); `TestCheckRefusesWhatIsNotACommitAndWhatGitWouldReadAsAnOption` has a third row, `a base that is an option`, so the value of `--base` reaches git only through `review.CheckRev` as `--head` does (killed by M10); `TestUsageDocumentsReviewCheck` and `TestWrapperUsageOfReviewVerifySaysWhatTheEngineUsageSays` pin the new signature and the chain sentence (the help row of the table below).

A `--base main` is refused when `main` moved on after the branch was made (a gap at the commit the branch was made from); the delivery body tells the role to pass the merge base then. Whether the engine should compute it (for example `--base` defaulting to the merge base with a named branch) is the decision of T9, S11.
  - Risk: items 2 and 3. Item 2: `review check` is the guard of delivery and `--base` adds a way through it; the chain re-reads every file and recomputes every range from git as the single-head check does, refuses at a gap, a range that is not clear or does not hold, and a loop, and its `--base` reaches git only through `review.CheckRev` (10 mutants killed). What it cannot refuse is a chain whose hops are all clear but whose first result's base was chosen over another that would not have led to the base (incomplete, not unsound), and it does not ask git whether the hops are commits of `base..head` (not needed: the hops compose). Item 3: `Check` is split and `reportChecked` replaced in shared code, the flag list of `check` grew, the usage and the delivery prompt (mirrored in three files) changed; the differential shows 23 of 27 invocations identical and the 4 that differ are the asked change.

  - Delivered, one fix commit, `3352d520`: 561 changed lines (520 added, 41 removed): 407 of tests, 116 of Go (8 of them the help in `usage.go`) and 38 of help and docs (`usage.sh`, README, results README, CHANGELOG). That is 161 over the target of about 400. The tests are almost three quarters of it, because each new seam has its own (the domain rule, the port method in the fake-runner and the real-git styles of `gitexec`, the fail-closed path of the use case, and the public interface), and I did not cut them. Domain `engine/review`: `CheckOneCommit` and `ErrNotOneCommit` (`exempt.go`); `NewCandidate` refuses a base that is its head (`review.go`); `CheckRange` takes the noun of the stored file (`evidence.go`). Port `engine/review/app`: `Git.Parent(repo, commit) (string, error)`. Use case: `Service.oneCommit`, asked by `Exempt` and by `exemptedHead` of `Check`. Adapter `engine/review/gitexec`: `Parent`. Help in `usage.go`, `bin/lib/usage.sh`, README, `odd/review-results/README.md` and CHANGELOG.
  - Decisions the prompt asked me to state:
    - B1. An exemption binds exactly one commit. `exempt` refuses unless `--base` resolves to the first parent of `--head`, as git gives it (`review.ErrNotOneCommit`, exit 1, one line that names both commits, nothing stored, not even the results directory). `check` refuses a stored exemption whose base is not that parent, for a head alone and for a chain, naming the file. The check runs after the binding of the exemption has been compared with git (`exemptedHead`), because `TestCheckRefusesAnExemptionThatNoLongerHoldsAndStoresNothing` expects `resolve base` first when git does not know the stored base. Results are not held to it: a clean result over C2..C4 after a fix commit is the correction round of T3 and still clears (the pin `TestACleanResultOverTwoCommitsAfterACorrectionRoundStillClears` passes on `4f60385a` and after; mutant M14, which holds results to one commit too, fails it).
    - The port. A new method `Parent`, not a revision-syntax trick such as `head^` (the owner's preference for the most decoupled design). `git rev-list --parents -n 1 --end-of-options <commit>` must print exactly one line, the commit and then its parents, each a full object name; any other shape is an error, so a rule that cannot be checked refuses. A commit with no parent answers empty and the exemption of it is refused (`<head> has no parent`).
    - Merges and shallow clones. For a merge the base is its first parent (the diff the merge brought in), as the chain follows first parents only. A shallow clone or a graft that hides the parent makes the exemption of that commit refuse (fail closed).
    - A1 is fixed in the rule `bind` shares: `review.NewCandidate` refuses a base that is its head (`base and head are the same commit <sha>, so the range covers no commit and clears nothing`, wrapping `ErrInvalidCandidate`). So `bind`, `record`, `exempt` and `check` (a head and a chain) all refuse it. I refused `record` too, because it is the one line in the shared rule and not a second change; it refuses before the reply is read (exit 1, where an invalid reply gave exit 2). The one behavior change nobody named: `bind --base X --head X` exits 1, where it exited 0 and made the worktree. A stored result or exemption over such a range, which no verb makes now, refuses the head with the same reason.
    - A4. `review.CheckRange(e, b, stored)` takes the noun of the stored file, `"result"` or `"exemption"`; the words for a result are byte for byte what they were (the new unit test pins them for both nouns).
    - A2. One sentence in the section on the check before delivery of `odd/review-results/README.md`, as an accepted limit; no code. Closing it would mean `check` reading the role-chain records, which it does not read today.
    - `roles append` of a `review-exemption` is not given the one-commit rule: `check` is the guard of delivery and the record carries only the digest of the stored file.
    - No role body and no generated agent file changed: `rg` finds no sentence about the range of an exemption in `roles/*/body.md` or `skills/role-chain/SKILL.md` (the latter has no sentence about exemptions at all), and `gadu-generate --check` is OK.
    - Existing tests changed on purpose: `TestExemptComputesTheBindingFromGitAsRecordDoes` (the calls of `Record`, then `Parent <head>`), the call list `wantGit` in `check_exempt_test.go` (it ends with `Parent <head>`), the three `CheckRange` calls of `evidence_test.go` (the third argument), the row `a base that is the head` added to the refusals of `NewCandidate`, and the pair rows and README fragments of `TestUsageDocumentsReviewExempt` and `TestReadmeDocumentsReviewExemptWhereItDocumentsTheOtherReviewVerbs`. `fakeGit` of `review/app` gained `Parent`.

With the build of `4f60385a` every exit of the script was 0 (`exempt C2..C4`, both `check` of the head C4, both `check` over the forged file, and `record`, `check`, `check --base` and `exempt` of C2..C2).   - Risk: items 2 and 3. Item 2: `review check` is the guard of delivery and the change makes it stricter and adds no way through: an exemption clears one commit only, an empty range clears nothing, and what cannot be told (git failing to name the parent, an answer of a shape it does not expect, a commit with no parent) refuses. What it still cannot refuse is the verdict edited in place (A2, documented), and a result of a wide range over a commit whose review blocked, by design (the correction round of T3). Item 3: `bind --base X --head X` exits 1 where it exited 0; `exempt` refuses wide ranges it used to store and `check` refuses a stored wide exemption (the repository holds no stored result or exemption: `odd/review-results/` has only its README); the `Git` port gained a method, so any other implementation must have it; `CheckRange` has a third parameter; the words of `roles append` for a `review-exemption` and the help in three places changed. The differential shows 30 of 34 invocations identical and the 4 that differ are the asked refusals.

  - Covered: S5 (typed evidence in `roles append`) and S1 (what cannot be told refuses) for the one behavior change; the other findings are tests, comments and documents of S5. S6, S11 and S12 are not touched. The owner's rule for the round: every finding is resolved, either fixed or shown already fixed at HEAD with evidence; warnings are fixed before delivery; suggestions are fixed when cheap and otherwise carried with a reason.
  - Delivered, one fix commit, `4606a49f`: 347 changed lines (235 added, 112 removed): 298 of tests, 30 of Go and 19 of help and docs. That is under the target of about 400. The only production behavior change is the verdict parse in `CheckEvidence` (`engine/review/app/evidence.go`, 14 lines with its doc comment); the rest of the Go is doc comments and the two help lines of `usage.go`. Three tests were rewritten to say less and to say it better, and they account for most of the removed lines.
  - Decisions the prompt asked me to state:
    - The stored verdict is held to `reviewverdict.Parse` and not to `reviewverdict.Decide`: `roles append` asks only whether the file is what `verify` stored, a verdict of that result, and what the verdict makes of the review stays `check`'s, which reads the file again. The parse runs after the digest, so a wrong digest still says `differs from the stored file` (row r5 of the differential is identical in both builds). The error is an `EvidenceError` of the verifier-report that wraps the rule it broke.
    - `Cleared` and `Exempted`: the contract is now explicit, and it was already kept. At HEAD `Service.exemptedHead` decodes each exemption and holds it to `CheckExemption`, `CheckBinding` (against the candidate git gives now) and `oneCommit` before it builds an `Exempted`, and `Check` and `CheckChain` take exemptions only from it; `Cleared` counts what it is given. The smallest change was the doc comments of `Exempted` and `Cleared`. Making `Exempted` unforgeable with a constructor in `review` would move the sequence of the use case into the domain, for a type that has one builder, and I did not.
    - Tests that pinned phrases were reduced or clarified and none was added: the help-parity test names four facts and says what it does not compare; the README and convention tests are grouped by rule and their failures state the rule. The new help words of `roles append` (`which must still be a result and a verdict of that result`) have no pin of their own, because the round asked for fewer phrase pins; the behavior is pinned through the verb.

  - Decisions the prompt asked me to state:
    - The canonical file is `roles/reviewer/rows.json`, beside the reviewer's `body.md`: one object `{"rows": [{"id", "sentence"}]}` with the rows `default`, `readability` and `risk-line`, in that order. `default` is the scope the reviewer reads when nothing else is added (behavior and tests, then failure handling and load, then the risk line: secrets, permissions, path escapes, exec or net imports), `readability` is the readability scope sentence of the review contract verbatim, and `risk-line` is the second blind launch that a Risk line naming an item asks for. The parent copies the sentences from it into the prompt of the reviewer.
    - The program does not read the file. The domain package reads no file and `roles/` is not deployed (no row of `overlay.manifest` names it), so `engine/review/rows.go` holds the ids and the grammar, and `TestTheRowsTheProgramAcceptsAreTheRowsOfTheCanonicalFile` reads the file strictly (`jsonstrict`) and holds its ids to the program's: a row added to one and not to the other fails. `agentgen` reads only `roles/<name>/body.md`, so no generated agent file changed (`gadu-generate --check` below).
    - The parent declares the rows with a required `--rows <id,id>` on `review record`, in the grammar of `--risk`: commas, no space, none written twice, in the order written. It is checked right after the feature and before git or the reply, as `Service.Exempt` does with `--risk`: a missing flag is `error: review record: --rows is required`, a bad declaration names the rows (`error: review record: review: invalid rows: "nope" is refused, "nope" is not a row; the rows are default, readability, risk-line, separated by commas with no space and none written twice (for example "default,readability")`), both exit 1 (the reply is not at fault, so not 2) and nothing is stored. The other verbs keep refusing `--rows` as an unknown flag.
    - The rows are recorded in a `rows` member between `binding` and `reply` of the stored result, `{version, binding, rows, reply}`. The version stays 1 (it is shared with the exemption files, which have no rows) and the reply contract of `reviewresult` is untouched: the rows are not part of the reply.
    - Validation against the canonical rows: `EncodeResult` refuses rows that are not rows (it never writes a file `DecodeResult` would refuse), and `DecodeResult` refuses a stored file whose rows are missing or are not rows (`review: invalid stored file: its rows are not the rows of a launch: <reason>`). `DecodeResult` has one production caller, `judged()` in `review/app/verify.go`, the reader that `verify`, `check`, the correction round and the evidence of `roles append` share, so the rule holds for each of them.
    - Existing results without rows: refused, not migrated and not defaulted. There is nothing to migrate: `odd/review-results/` holds only its README, and the reviews of T1-T4 are native receipts under `odd/review-receipts/`. A default of `["default"]` for a missing member would invent a lens count, which S1 forbids; a result stored by the previous program is reviewed again and recorded with `--rows` (the differential shows the refusal and its words).
    - The lens count: `review_lens_count` is the number of rows recorded in the stored results of the change, one for each row of each launch (a corrected candidate adds its launches), and is omitted when no stored result records one. The schema description says so and no longer claims "0, 1, or up to 4", the results README says so, and `TestReviewLensCountIsCountedFromTheRowsOfTheStoredResults` pins the description and the role-chain pointer. No code computes the number: the closing agent counts it, as for the other optional fields of the record, and `tools/actuals-record-validator` only requires a count not to be negative, so it was left alone.
    - The role-chain skill: its Binding section says the rows come from `roles/reviewer/rows.json` and that `record` takes `--rows R`. The body was at the limit (2797 of 2800 bytes) and the role-chain tests pin about seventy of its fragments, so the 39 bytes of the pointer are paid for by trimming words nothing pins (`Pipe each record` to `Pipe records`, `keep saving theirs` to `keep saving`, `its parent` to `parent`, `explicitly` after `Pass Engram project`, `when` to `if` in the blocking rule, the exit-2 clause merged into one list, two commas for `and`). Every fragment of `roleChainDuties()` is intact and the body is 2797 bytes again, 700 tokens.

  - Decisions the prompt asked me to state:
    - One engine function, `CountLenses(results, root, feature) (LensCount{Results, Lenses}, error)` in `engine/review/app`, the application layer over the `Results` port, so it needs no git and a test drives it with the fake store. It lists `odd/review-results/<feature>/`, reads each file named like a result (`review.CheckResultName`; a `.verifier.json`, an `.exempt.json` and any other name are skipped unread) the way `verify` and `check` read one (`judgedStored`, the new core of `judged`) and adds `len(Rows)`. It refuses with the zero count a feature that is not one safe path segment (asked before the store), a directory that cannot be listed, a file that cannot be read and a file that is not a result any more. A missing directory, or no result, is a zero count and no error: whether zero is right is for the reader to say.
    - Not a new verb. Option A of L29 (a verb that prints the count) adds a CLI surface, its help and a process boundary, and the owner chose B. The validator imports the engine module, as `tools/archive-anchor-gate` does (`require .../engine v0.0.0` and `replace ... => ../../engine`). The build closure is 12 engine packages and the `jsonschema` and `x/text` it already had: no third-party module is added and `go.sum` is unchanged. This is item 5 of the Risk line.
    - Where the validator finds the results: a required `--repo PATH`, the repository root and an absolute path (`go run -C` changes the working directory); the results are `odd/review-results/<change_name>/` under it, with the `change_name` of the record. The two callers pass `--repo "$root"`, the `root="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"` they already compute for `-C`, and a test requires each documented call to carry `--schema`, `--instance` and `--repo "$root"`. The validator stats `--repo` itself, because `Names` answers "none" for a root that is not there, and that would pass.
    - The outcomes, after the checks of shape and of the other rules, which run first and are unchanged. The record counts N and the stored results record N rows: exit 0, and a second line says what was compared (`review_lens_count: N equals the N rows recorded in K stored review results of "<change>"`). It counts N and they record M rows: exit 6, naming N, M and K. It counts N and the change has no stored result (a change reviewed before S8, with gentle receipts, or one whose results are in another change): exit 6, `nothing backs a count for this change ... omit review_lens_count`. It omits the field and the results record M rows: exit 6, `write review_lens_count M`. It omits the field and no result records a row: exit 0, and a second line says `review_lens_count: omitted, and no stored review result of "<change>" records a row: there was nothing to compare`. The results cannot be counted: exit 7, new, never a pass.
    - The pre-S8 change, the case S13 left to me: its record omits `review_lens_count`. A count claimed for it came from the receipt of another tool, and the validator cannot tell that from a number made up; the schema already defines the count (T5) as the rows of the stored results, so a count nothing backs is refused, and the message says to omit it. The lenient reading, no stored result means skip the comparison and pass, is the silent pass S13 forbids, for exactly the records A1 was about. By the same rule an omitted count is no way around the check while a result records a row. I judged both settled by S1, S13 and the schema wording and did not ask; the lenient alternatives are named in "Open for the parent". An edge: a record that counts 0 for a change with no stored result passes, because 0 does not differ from 0 rows, and its second line says `0 equals the 0 rows recorded in 0 stored review results`.
    - Exit codes: 0, 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6 keep their meanings (2 is now also a missing `--repo`, and a pass prints one more line); 7 is new, the stored review results could not be read. `go run` reports any non-zero exit of the tool as 1 and prints `exit status N` (it did before), which the end-to-end run below shows; the contract the skills state, exit 0 is the only result that authorises the write, holds.

  - Covered: S6 up to and including the L18 sentence. A Claude Code Stop hook that `labdrian install-hooks` installs; it detects a committed head on a non-default branch with no recorded result binding it, and blocks the stop once per session and candidate with the exact `labdrian review bind --base <last reviewed head> --head <next commit>`; `status-hooks` reports it; `uninstall-hooks` removes it (the task line); per L18 it asks only for the next unreviewed commit of the chain, never the whole branch, and a head cleared by a valid exemption (`review exempt`) counts as reviewed. Not covered, because they are T6b: the L28 and L34 sentences (the sweeper and polisher triggers, the `Risk:` trailer, the two records, and `review check` refusing a head whose trigger fired). No other `S#`.
  - Delivered, eleven commits, 3178 changed lines in all (the net diff of the branch is 3127 added and 49 removed; 125 of the added lines are goldens that the repository's own update flags regenerate), 66 new test functions, each commit ending with its `Risk:` trailer:
    - `0fcbc57b` feat(review): find the next commit of a branch that no review covers. 94 lines: `review.Link` and `review.FirstUnreviewed`, the chain of reviews read from the commit the branch was made on. Risk: none.
    - `fe3c2d65` feat(review): ask git where HEAD stands in its branch. 257 lines: the `Standing` and `Parent` questions of the Git port and their `gitexec` adapter (the branch, the default branch, the first-parent commits the default lacks). Risk: none.
    - `ed32824d` feat(review): list the features a working tree keeps reviews for. 198 lines: `Results.Features` and its `fsstore` adapter. Risk: none.
    - `ae607617` feat(review): name the next commit of a branch that no stored review covers. 326 lines: `Service.NextUnreviewed`, `Question`, `NoDefaultBranchError`. Risk: none.
    - `bd147ec3` feat(hookwire): decode the input of a Stop hook and encode its block. 156 lines: `hookwire.DecodeStop`, `StopReply`, `EventStop`; the wire format stays in `engine/hookwire`, as `TestNoHookWireFormatOutsideHookwire` requires. Risk: none.

    - `2a50c0a4` feat(review): block a stop once per session and question. 277 lines: `Service.Stop`, `Ruling`, the `Asked` port, `WithAsked`. Risk: none.

Risk: none.

  - Why twelve commits and not the four of the plan: the plan was four (the use case with the Git port, the Stop codec, the verb with the marker store and the usage, and the settings family with the lifecycle check, the status line and the docs). The native review stops with `lens_context_budget_exceeded` and has no override, and the budget for a unit is about 400 lines, so I cut along the seams of the rings: the rule, the Git port, the features port, the use case, the codec, the marker store, the Stop use case, the verb, the settings family, the status check with the tables, and the docs, which are the eleven commits above and, with this one, twelve. The first seven are 326 lines or fewer. Two go over and are not split on purpose: `d89f0efd` (589; 130 are code and 459 are the tests that drive the whole hook through the public interface, so they cannot be told apart from the verb) and `3c50dc45` (487; 88 are code and 399 are tests; the family, its wiring, the narrowing of the Stop removal and the strip of the golden replay have to land together or the tree is red, and it is the one commit that carries `Risk: 3, 5`). `d6083721` is 369 lines, under the budget, 125 of them generated.
  - Decisions (the design I settled and stated before the relaunch, items 1 to 7, and what I added):
    - Which features, and when the hook is silent (items 1 and 2): every directory `odd/review-results/<feature>/` of the working tree (`Results.Features`); a record is bound by its head and its diff digest, so the directory name carries no authority and nothing is left undetermined. The hook is global, so a tree that keeps neither `odd/tasks` nor `odd/review-results` is outside it. It is silent too on a detached HEAD (review worktrees), on the default branch, behind it, while `stop_hook_active` is true (a hook that keeps blocking keeps the agent from stopping; nothing is asked of git or of the stores then), and when the chain of reviews reaches the last commit.
    - The default branch (item 3): `origin/HEAD`, else `main`, else `master`. When none is found the hook cannot tell which commits are the branch's own, so it blocks once per session with a question of its own (key `no-default-branch`) that names the branch and says to run `git remote set-head origin --auto` or to make the default branch `main`.
    - What "reviewed" means (item 4): a stored result or exemption of any feature that reads back and whose binding equals git (`reviewedResult` and `exemptedHead`, the readers `review check` uses), whatever its outcome. A head whose review blocks, or is still in flight, is not asked for again, and `review check` stays the only judge of clear against blocked. This is my reading of "no recorded result binding it": a strict "cleared" would ask again for a blocked head, with the wrong message. It is written in the usage text, in the README section and in `odd/review-results/README.md`, and a docs test reads the README for it. A record that cannot be read or does not hold is no review of the commit.
    - The walk (item 5, L18): the first-parent commits of HEAD that the default branch lacks, oldest first; a commit is reached when the chain of reviews leads to it from the commit the branch was made on (`review.FirstUnreviewed`), so a review of one commit, of several at once, and the reviews of a branch made one by one all reach the commit they end at, and a review that starts at a commit nothing reached reaches nothing. The question is the first commit after the last one reached, with the commit before it as `--base` (the commit the branch was made on when no review is reached). A first commit with no parent leaves no base to name: that is an error and no block.
    - The block (item 6): `{"decision":"block","reason":"..."}` on stdout and exit 0, which is the documented contract of a Stop hook; never exit 2, which blocks the stop and hands stderr to the agent. From the moment the command line was understood the exit code is 0 whatever happens: input that is unreadable or over `hookwire.MaxEnvelopeBytes` (1 MiB; the verb reads at most the bound plus one byte), the input of another event, a repository or a store that cannot be read, a panic (recovered and reported on stderr). The stop happens in every one of those cases. What the hook could not tell is one line on stderr, which Claude Code shows only in verbose mode; input it cannot use is silent. The command in `settings.json` is `command -v <bin> >/dev/null 2>&1 && <bin> review stop-hook || true`, under the family identity `review stop-hook`, so a binary that is missing does nothing.
    - Once per session and question (item 7): the markers live in `<state home>/labdrian/review-stop/<sha256 of the session id, in hex>/<base12>-<head12>` (or `no-default-branch`), directories 0700 and a marker 0600, written through `statestore` outside the working tree. The question is recorded before the stop is blocked; when it cannot be recorded or read the stop is not blocked, because a block that cannot be remembered would be asked again at every stop. A session that stops again gets nothing for that commit; the next commit, or another session, is asked.
    - Settings: `reviewStopFamily` is the last of `installedFamilies`. `removeHooks` now scans `Stop` but removes only the review stop-hook family there, because legacy entries of other families can sit on Stop and are not ours to remove (pinned by `TestRemoveHooksTakesOnlyTheReviewStopHookOffStop` and by the golden replay of the pre-H27 recordings, whose expectations I did not change: the replay strips the entries added since the recording, `withoutEntriesAddedSinceTheRecording`). `HasSupportedClaudeLifecycleState` requires the hook, so `runtime status --target claude` reads `partial` and `status-hooks` warns until `uninstall-hooks` and `install-hooks` are run again and Claude Code is restarted, as for the approve guard. The Installation claim of the capability table names the Stop hook and its tests.
    - Status: the check `hook: Stop (review stop-hook)` comes after the approve guard and before the contracts. It is OK when the entry is exactly what install writes, WARN naming the missing or drifted parts (with the remedy and the restart note) when it is absent or edited, and FAIL when `settings.json` cannot be read.
    - `stop-hook` is not in the lists of verbs that `review` names to a caller who gets a verb wrong (the precedent is `skills guard-hook`): it is an internal hook command, so no existing message changes.

Those are the four known environmental failures; no other test fails.

First stop of session s1: exit 0 and `{"decision":"block","reason":"Branch feature/e2e has a commit that no review covers. Before you stop, review that commit alone, not the whole branch: ... labdrian review bind --base <c4> --head <c5> ..."}`, so the base is the last reviewed head and the head is the next commit (L18).   - Steelman: the launch is the one step the parent cannot skip, so a deny there makes "the builder never designs" mechanical; the citation is the `Spec:` line the ODD protocol already prescribes, and the mark is cross-checked against an append-only Log entry attributed to the shaper, so a forged mark is a visible false attribution. Everything reuses what exists: `hookwire.DecodeAgentCall` (`engine/hookwire/decode.go:216`) and `hookwire.PreToolUseReply` (`engine/hookwire/encode.go:20`) for the wire, `hookFamily` (`engine/settings/family.go:16`) for install, repair and removal, and the approve guard's deny contract (exit 0, JSON decides; `engine/cmd/skills_guard.go:18-34`).
  - Strongest counter-argument: the guard checks a mark, not a design. The parent has Edit and can write `shaped: L<n>` and a shaper-attributed Log entry itself, and a shaper can mark a thin design. Accepted tradeoff: a speed bump against forgetting the shaper, like the approve guard (`engine/skills/approve_guard.go:96`), not a boundary against intent. Rejected alternatives: binding the mark to the writing agent through the hook input (unverified that Claude Code sends the sub-agent's identity to PreToolUse, and the parent's own writes would carry none); reusing `shaper clearance record` (README:547), which records a human clearance of a handoff and goal JSON, not a task of a feature document.

D2, a missing engine binary: recommend fail-open (no-op, `status-hooks` reports it), because a POSIX text fallback over the raw JSON cannot read a citation and would deny every builder launch. D3, an Agent call over 4 MiB: recommend deny whatever the agent, the one exception to "other agents untouched", as the approve guard denies over its bound.   - Steelman: the trailer makes the risk a fact of the commit that git carries, so the hook, `review check`, `review exempt` and the reviewer's second launch read one declaration; every record reuses what T2-T6a built (the binding and `review.CheckBinding`, the atomic store and `review.NextNumber`, `review.CheckOneCommit`, the T6a walk and once-per-session marker, `statestore`), and one pure `review.Needs` keeps the hook and the gate from drifting apart. - **L52** (2026-10-28, shaper): Design of T6b2a, the answer to the five assumptions of L51 and to the review of `b685f0c5` (`odd/review-results/own-rdd/b685f0c56be2-1.json`, F1 SUGGESTION, F2 WARNING); S6 (b) and (c) amended where this pins them, no new owner text; no code written, nothing committed.
  - Assumptions of L51: (1) confirmed and pinned in S6 (c): S6 (c) already writes the binding as `base`, `head`, `diff_sha256`, and `CheckRecordBinding` (`rolerecords.go:59-61`) compares base, head and digest, the paths following from the digest. (2) confirmed and pinned in S6 (c): the ids (`rolerecords.go:189-214`) are prefixed per trigger, so a path and a name never collide, and T6b3, T6b4 and the skill (T6b7) need one fixed form. (3) confirmed as a known limit, written into S6 (b): a `-U0` diff gives the removed lines without the `const (` opener, so telling a grouped-block entry from a local needs the file around it, which the domain does not get; the cost is a removed grouped name that is not swept. (4) confirmed: S6 (c) already says the sweeper record "refuses when no fired sweep record binds the head" (`rolerecords.go:319-321`). (5) confirmed: a pure `CheckSweepCommit(head, commit, parent, subject)` (`rolerecords.go:346-354`) keeps git in the adapters, as `CheckRecordBinding` does; T6b4 reads the first parent and subject of the named commit and must refuse a commit git does not know before calling it.
  - F1 (fixed, not carried): the Go patterns are `^\s*` (`sweep.go:24-26`), so a removed local `var err error` yields `err` and T1 greps a common word. Decision: read a Go declaration only at column 0. Steelman: gofmt writes every package-level declaration at column 0, and only a package-level name can be mentioned outside its function, which is what T1 looks for; a local, a local `type` and a grouped entry are all indented. Strongest counter-argument: a `.go` file that is not gofmt-formatted (generated code, `testdata/`) can hold an indented package-level declaration that is then missed. Accepted tradeoff: a missed name there costs a stale mention T1 does not flag, against T1 firing on most commits that drop a local (every candidate then needs a sweeper answer); `gofmt -l .` is already a check of every builder unit (L50, L51). The shell patterns stay `^\s*`, since a shell function defined inside another is global once run. Rejected: a minimum name length or exported-only filter (arbitrary; `unusedHelper` is exactly what T1 is for).
  - F2 (fixed before delivery): `EncodePolisher` stores the findings through `encodeFile` (`stored.go:69-78`), whose `SetIndent` re-indents the `json.RawMessage`, and `DecodePolisher` re-runs `ParsePolisherFindings` (`rolerecords.go:484`), which holds the indented bytes to `MaxReplyBytes` (`rolerecords.go:420`). The other stored kinds hold the size bound at the input only (`app/service.go:145`, `app/exempt.go:59`, `app/verify.go:64`) and bound the read by `MaxStoredBytes` (`fsstore.go:223-228`). Decision: the same split for the polisher, shape on read, size on input, plus an encode-time refusal over `MaxStoredBytes`, the invariant `EncodeResult` states ("the function does not write a file DecodeResult would refuse", `stored.go:42`). Why the refusal: unlike a result, the polisher object is stored verbatim with members the engine does not check, and indentation grows with nesting depth, so a nested object under 1 MiB could become a file `fsstore` refuses to read (inferred from `encoding/json` indenting raw values and accepting nesting up to 10000 levels; unverified, the builder's nested test is the proof). Strongest counter-argument: the encoder allocates the whole indented file before it can refuse it, and a small but deep reply is refused as "too large". Accepted tradeoff: one transient allocation for a pathological reply in a CLI, against a stored record that never reads back. Rejected: compacting the findings on decode (fixes the 1 MiB case but not the 8 MiB one) and storing the polisher file unindented (breaks the one format of the stored files).

Not read: `roles/polisher/body.md` (whether real polisher findings are flat is unverified). No command run.

(4) `Modules` is the directories of every `go.mod` of the tree of H (`git ls-tree -r`); a changed file belongs to the innermost one; a changed file under no module runs no tool. (5) Packages for staticcheck are `.` or `./dir` of each changed `.go` file; with only `go.mod`/`go.sum` changed, only deadcode runs. (6) The adapter drops a package, a file or a module the copy does not have (new at the other side) and starts no tool when none is left. (7) Tool run is told from the exit status by what the tool printed: exit 0 ran; non-zero ran only with a finding line (`<path>:<line>[:<col>]: <text>`) on stdout; not found, deadline, or non-zero with none did not run (`app.ErrToolDidNotRun`, naming the tool and repeating its stderr, bounded). (8) `git archive` goes to a temp file and is unpacked in Go (refusing names that leave the directory and links in the path), into a directory that must be empty. (9) The lock is taken after the binding and `CheckOneCommit`, before any read for T1-T3, and given back by `defer`; the busy error is the bare `app.ErrSweepBusy` so the message is exactly `another review sweep of this repository is running`. (10) The lock file is `<repo key>/lock`, 0600, `filelock` with `NoWait`; Reset empties base/head but keeps the directories.

No code written, nothing committed.
  - Evidence (the coordinator's probe, relayed; not run by me): on git 2.43.0, in a scratch repository whose committed `.gitattributes` held `a.go export-ignore` and `b.txt export-subst`, with `core.autocrlf` on, `git archive` under `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null -c core.autocrlf=false --attr-source=<empty tree>`, and with `--attr-source` as a global option, still dropped `a.go` and expanded `b.txt`: archive reads the `.gitattributes` of the archived tree itself, and `$GIT_DIR/info/attributes` still applies. So L59 (1) would refuse every sweep of a repository that uses those attributes. The other two unverified premises of L59 hold: `git cat-file -e <missing 40-hex>` exits 1 with nothing on stderr and `-t` of a tree prints `tree`; `shellcheck -f gcc -- -x.sh` reads `-x.sh` as a file.
  - Decision on (1): the copy is built from the objects, not from an export: `git ls-tree -r -z -l --full-tree <commit>`, then one `git cat-file --batch` fed the blob names on standard input, each blob written with the mode the listing gives (S6 b). `cat-file` prints an object's raw bytes and applies no attribute, filter or conversion (`--filters` and `--textconv` are the opt-ins), so the copy is the tree by construction and needs no after-check; the ls-tree sizes are still held to the batch headers. Steelman: two git starts per copy whatever the tree's size, no temporary file, and no tar code; the path refusals of `unpack` carry over to the listing's entries. Strongest counter-argument: the `Runner` ports have no standard input (`gitexec.go:101-103`; `execrunner.Runner` has `Run` and `Output` only), so the shared process adapter, used by six packages, gains a method, and the whole tree's blobs pass through one bounded buffer (256 MiB, `review.go:50`), so a larger tree refuses the sweep. Accepted tradeoff: an additive method that no existing caller sees, and a fail-closed bound well above this repository, against a copy that differs from the tree. Rejected: `git archive` with a pinned empty index and work tree (`GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `--worktree-attributes`): `info/attributes` still applies and it rests on another unverified reading of git; a detached worktree or `checkout-index` (applies `text`/`eol` and filter drivers, which can run programs); one `git cat-file blob` per file (thousands of starts per copy against the ~5 s aim of L39); keeping L59's after-check with `git archive` (refuses those repositories instead of sweeping them).
  - Split: items (3)-(7) do not touch the copy and stay in T6b4a (~250-340 lines), buildable now. Item (2) moves with (1) into T6b4b: its tests drive the path refusals, and (1) removes `unpack` and the tar input those tests would have crafted, so writing them now means writing them twice. Order: T6b4a, then T6b4b (both edit `gitexec.go`, at different functions), then T6b5. T6b4a keeps every point of L59 items (3)-(7); its mark moves from L59 to L60 because L60 redraws it.

- **L63** (2026-10-28, shaper): Decisions on the points L61 (a)-(g) and L62 (a)-(g) left to the builder, on F1 of `odd/review-results/own-rdd/287d506b0aff-1.json` (SUGGESTION: the copy holds every blob in one `cat-file` answer under the 256 MiB bound, and the doc comment of `reviewGitMaxOutput` still says the only large answer is a diff), and on the outputs, refusal texts, error classes and exit codes of T6b5 and T6b6 before T6b5 starts; S6 (b), (c), (e), (f) and (h) amended where a decision pins something, no new owner text; one new task T6b4c, and T6b5 and T6b6 re-marked, all `shaped: L63`; T6b7 unchanged (it prints nothing new and documents S6 g, h as they stand). No code written, nothing committed.
  - L61, all confirmed as built, pinned in S6 (b) and (c). (a) `IsCommit`: an error for a name that is not a full object name cannot fire from `RecordSweeper` (the parser requires one) and keeps the port honest for other callers; a tag is false because the report must name the `chore(sweep)` commit itself, whose first parent and subject `CheckSweepCommit` reads; an unknown kind is git saying something unexpected, so an error (exit 1), not the report's fault (`gitexec.go:584-609`). (b), (c) `Mentions`: refusing an empty or multi-line name before git starts is S1 (git would read it as several patterns or match everything); a map keyed only by mentioned names with each path once is what `sweepT1` needs to build `hits`. (d) the `-o` overlap: the sweep asks only names `review.RemovedNames` extracts, each `[A-Za-z_]\w*` (`sweep.go:27-31`), and two such names cannot be whole-word matches at the same place, so the limit is the port's and is never reached by the sweep; pinned as such, no code. (e) one refusal text for missing and not a commit: the parent's action is the same (fix the report), so one text is enough (`recordrole.go:125`). (f) a nil `Environ` is empty: already the S6 (b) rule ("a nil or empty one stays empty"). (g) the CHANGELOG clause in the T6b4 entry: where the replaced sentence lived.
  - L62, all confirmed as built, pinned in S6 (b). (a) `OutputStdin(ctx, env, stdin []byte, bin, args...)`: the name says what it adds to `Output` and a byte slice is all one batch needs. (b) path and mode refusals over the whole listing before `cat-file`: a refused tree writes nothing, the cheapest fail-closed order; only the link check needs what was written. (c) no `cat-file` for a tree with no blob: there is nothing to ask. (d) trailing bytes are an error: an answer that is not exactly what was asked is not trusted (S1). (e) the refusal texts as built. (f) the umask: the copy lives under the user's state home and the analyzers only read it; a mode stricter than 0644/0755 under a stricter umask is the user's choice and changes no finding, so no chmod. (g) the CHANGELOG clause as placed.
  - F1 of `287d506b0aff-1.json`: the bound stands (L60) and the finding is resolved by T6b4c: the stale comment fixed, the bound pinned by a test, and the limit written in S6 (b) and (h). Steelman of streaming: a runner method that hands git's stdout as a reader lets `Archive` write each blob as it arrives, memory bounded by one blob, no ceiling on the tree, so no repository is ever refused for its size. Recommended instead: keep one bounded answer. Strongest counter-argument to that: S6 (h) makes a sweep that refuses stop delivery, so a repository whose committed blobs pass 256 MiB (assets, vendored binaries) can never deliver, where `git archive` to a temporary file used to work; the reviewer's claim is right. Accepted tradeoff: a second new method on the shared process adapter that six packages import (L60), with its own pipe, deadline and kill handling, is the larger and riskier change, against a loud, fail-closed refusal (an error wrapping `execrunner.ErrOutputTooLarge`, exit 2, nothing written, `execrunner.go:126`, `gitexec.go:906`) for trees no repository of this project approaches; streaming stays the named way out if one does. Unverified: the size of this repository's blobs (no command run; L62's differential swept it without hitting the bound only on a scratch repository, so that is not evidence either). Also in T6b4c: L62 said the refusal logic of the copy was not mutation-tested (its RED was not discriminating); the T6b4b tests are new, so their mutants are run once.
  - T6b5 (S6 f, pinned): `review check` keeps exit 1 for every refusal and adds nothing to stdout, because `runReviewCheck` sends every error through `reviewFailed` without a `*ReplyRefusedError` (`review.go:489-524`, `:563-571`) and the delivery role reads the JSON members by name (`review.go:431-436`); a passive range prints `"results":[]` with no `exemptions`, which no other range can print, so no new member and no change to `roles/delivery/body.md`. `review check` refuses with the first missing item of (d), so today's coverage texts (`check.go:128-163`, `app/check.go:99-101`, `app/chain.go:62-66`) stay byte for byte and every existing check test keeps its expectation once its fixtures carry a trailer and a sweep record. The three new reasons get sentinels beside `ErrNoResult`, one each, so a caller tells them apart with `errors.Is` as it does today (`check.go:19-35`). A passive commit's stored result or exemption is still held: "needs nothing else" (d) removes a requirement, not a refusal, and a blocking review wins over everything (S5). An exemption on a commit whose trailer names an item refuses with `review.CheckRisk`'s error, the one `review exempt --risk` gives, so the reason reads the same wherever it is met. `review exempt` refuses a failing or high-risk trailer as a plain error, exit 1, because that is how it refuses a `--risk` that names an item (`app/exempt.go:56-58`, `review.go:384-386`): the trailer is a property of the commit, not of the verifier's report, which is the only input exit 2 refuses. Order in `Exempt`: after the binding and `oneCommit`, before `EncodeExemption`, since the message needs the resolved head.
  - T6b6 (S6 e, pinned): the block text was already exact in (e); what was open were its placeholders and what a record that does not hold means to the hook. `<feature>` stays literal because the hook reads every feature (`unreviewed.go:55-110`); a record that does not hold is skipped, as T6a's `links` skips a result whose read fails (`unreviewed.go:104-109`), so the hook asks again and `review check` is the one that refuses.
  - Steelman of the batch: everything pinned here is what the builders already chose or what today's code already does, so T6b5 and T6b6 start with no output left to invent, and the only code change is a comment, a test and mutants. Strongest counter-argument: pinning that `review check` names only the first missing item makes the parent run it several times to see everything a commit lacks. Accepted: the hook lists all of them for the commit it asks, and keeping today's texts byte for byte is what keeps T6b5's Risk 3 to the requested refusals.

Owner rule: every finding is resolved, warnings before delivery, suggestions carried, design gaps to the shaper.
  - (1) F1 of `09ee88a0fe00-2.json` (WARNING): fixed in T6b6d, before T6b6c. The finding is right. `Stop` keys its marker `short(Base)+"-"+short(Head)` (`engine/review/app/stop.go:56`), and `once` does not block when the key is there (`:61-73`). A commit asked for its sweep and its review is never asked in that session for the sweeper its sweep calls for once it fires. The key was my pin (S6 e, L40 and L63), so this is a gap in my design, not in the build.
    - Steelman of keeping one key per commit: the hook is a reminder and not the gate. `review check` refuses a commit with a fired sweep and no sweeper (S6 f, `review.ErrNoSweeper`) on every runtime (S6 h), so nothing is delivered unswept. A hook that asks less nags less.
    - The other fix considered: key the marker by the whole set of needs. It needs one marker and one `Has`, but a set that shrinks is a new key. Once the sweep is stored, `[MissingReview]` would block again for a review the session was already asked for, so "once" would become "after every step forward".
    - Chosen: one marker per need, `<base12>-<head12>-<need>`. A stop blocks when a need of the commit asked has no marker, and the block still lists every need. Grounds: the owner-accepted words of S6 tie the once to the role: "when a sweeper check fires, or the declared risk names a high-risk item (1-6), and no record of that role binds the candidate, it blocks the stop once per session and candidate with the exact launch the parent must run". A per-need marker is that sentence applied to each role. `checkKey` already admits the longer key (`engine/review/fsstore/asked.go:92-101`: lowercase letters, digits and dashes, at most 64 bytes against at most 34 here), so the port and its adapter do not change.
    - Strongest counter-argument: one commit can block one session up to five times (Risk, Sweep, Sweeper, Polisher, Review), and markers under the old key are no longer read, so a session open across the upgrade is asked once more. Accepted: each new block names a need never shown before, there are only five, and a commit amended for its trailer is a new candidate already. `needKey` is total (`need<n>` for an unnamed kind), so naming adds no error path that no test could reach.
  - (2) F1 of `09ee88a0fe00-1.json` (SUGGESTION): carried into T6b6d. `TestNextUnreviewedAsksForTheFirstCommitOfABranchNoReviewCovers` (`engine/review/app/unreviewed_test.go:59`) checks only that the git calls begin with three entries. T6b6d restores the whole-list equality, with the list written out and checked against the walk of S6 (e).

  - What I checked before shaping:
    - Q1: CI builds the engine with Go 1.21 (`engine/go.mod:3` `go 1.21`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:86` `go-version-file: engine/go.mod`), so CI is green and the failures are local to go1.27.3; but `install-hooks` builds the installed engine with the local toolchain, so a user of this machine sees the go1.27 words. `CheckKnownFields` (`engine/jsonstrict/jsonstrict.go:112-137`) is called from 12 product files (search: `reviewverdict`, `reviewresult`, `workflow`, `shaper` x4, `roles`, `memoryscope`, `goal`, `projection`, and `jsonstrict` itself); `ParseDirectiveMember` (`engine/memoryscope/memoryscope.go:101-105`) and `WireVersion` (`:149-160`) decode the same map by hand. Decoding into `map[string]json.RawMessage` can only fail with a type error at the top level, since every member value is taken raw, so `DecodeObject` needs no `Field` test. No product code inspects `*json.SyntaxError` or `*json.UnmarshalTypeError` (`rg 'SyntaxError|UnmarshalTypeError' engine --glob '!*_test.go'`: only `skills/app`'s own `ManifestSyntaxError`). The pinned texts: `engine/memoryscope/member_test.go:41`, `engine/goal/goal_v3_test.go:99`, `engine/settings/settingsfile/testdata/golden-v1/golden-10.json:20`. `longterm-mem/go.mod` says `go 1.26.1` and its goldens hold `invalid character` and `cannot unmarshal` texts (search: 20 golden and test files), so T13a surveys it and the other modules under go1.27.3 and fixes nothing there.

Setting both would change nothing and would read as a fix of a flake nobody reproduced. So S16 (c) has no task. Leads not followed (unverified): the variables `testhome` leaves, `PATH` among them (a real `pi` on it: `bin/lib/pi_package.sh:44` asks `command -v pi`), and the untimed `go build` of `enginetest.Provision` under the load of a wide run.
    - Q3: one test function (`engine/skills/actuals_instrumentation_d2_anchor_test.go:60`); CI fetches the whole history (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:81` `fetch-depth: 0`). That GitHub Actions sets `CI=true` on its runners is from its documentation as I know it, not checked here (unverified).
  - Design and its counter-arguments:
    - S16 (a), steelman of the prototyper's approach: the goldens are the contract of what the CLI prints, the engine is built with whatever toolchain the machine has, and holding the words in two small functions keeps every test and golden unchanged, under both toolchains. Alternatives rejected: rewriting the goldens to go1.27's words (CI on go1.21 would then fail, and the words would follow the next toolchain again); normalizing the toolchain-dependent fragments in the tests (the user would still see texts that change with the toolchain, and the tests would stop pinning them); pinning the toolchain (`toolchain` in `go.mod` or `GOTOOLCHAIN`) would download go1.21 on every machine and fight `review sweep`'s analyzers, which need the toolchain they were built with (L78). Strongest counter-argument: this rewrites standard-library error texts, a shim that chases each Go release, and it holds only the two texts a test pins: other go1.27 wordings (a non-ASCII first character other than a byte order mark, a type error into another destination, such as `settings.Parse`'s `map[string]interface {}`) still drift, and no test would see it. Accepted: the shim is two places with a test each, the T13a survey lists what else drifts, and the differential check proves go1.21's output is unchanged.
    - S16 (b), steelman of the plain skip: a test that cannot run outside a full checkout should not fail where it cannot run, and the verifier's probes run in a `git archive` copy (the Delegated Verification Gate). Counter-argument: a skip is a silent pass, against S1, and a future shallow CI clone would skip it forever. The `CI=true` guard answers that: in CI the missing history fails with the same text. The tradeoff accepted: a person who sets `CI=true` in an archive copy gets a failure, which is what the variable asks for.
    - S17 (a), the words: "the first commit with unfinished review steps" is true of what `NextUnreviewed` does: a commit whose stored review blocks has done its steps and is not asked (`engine/review/app/unreviewed.go:49-53`), and a passive commit has none. Counter-argument: the phrase is new; the engine's usage and `README.md:1220` say "the first commit that misses anything `review check` asks of it", which has the same flaw in a milder form (a blocking result is something `review check` refuses, yet the hook does not ask for it), and S17 leaves it, since F1 named the "not ready" wording and `README.md:1227-1229` qualifies the other one two sentences later. Returned as a risk, not changed.
    - S17 (c), "until the copy streams": the README sweep entry took the words of S6 (h), and the only path to streaming is the one L63 rejected (the bound kept, "the owner may overturn"; no task holds it). A user cannot act on the phrase, so the README drops it and keeps the limit (`cannot be swept`, `stops`). S6 (h) is left as written: it records the accepted limit and the condition under which it would lift. Building the streaming copy is not shaped: it is unrequested scope, and the owner's call.
  - Checked by the parent (recorded here, no task): the GitHub merge commit on `main` (no `Risk:` trailer) is no problem for the stop hook. `NextUnreviewed` asks nothing on the default branch (`engine/review/app/unreviewed.go:55`, read), and on a feature branch it walks only the commits the default branch lacks (`:43`, read). Only a manual `review check` on `main` refuses it, which is outside the intended use.

  - Design of S7 and its counter-arguments:
    - Steelman of S7: once gentle is removed, a live openspec change archived afterwards has no gentle receipt, so its anchor could only be self-asserted through an owner override, and the gate's one verified outcome would be unreachable; reading our stored reviews keeps it reachable with no new record kind.
    - Chosen: the engine's chain check over the merge's own range (L's second parent from the merge base), and the tree of the reviewed head. Rejected: taking any stored result whose head is L or L's second parent (simpler, but it misses the usual case, a passive head with no record, and it would clear a head whose earlier commits nobody reviewed); taking the latest result of the folder by name, as the receipt branch takes the last lineage (it binds nothing to what landed); reading a tree out of a record (a result holds none, and S1 wants the binding recomputed, which `CheckChain` does).
    - Strongest counter-argument: the gate reads only `openspec/changes/archive/`, where nothing was archived after 2026-09-21 and where the ODD flow writes nothing, so T7 puts the engine's whole review use case into a required CI job's build for a path that no real report may ever take; and when one does, a merge made after the default branch moved has a tree other than the reviewed head's and comes out `rejected`, so the verified outcome is reached only for merges of an up-to-date branch. Accepted if the owner wants S7: the cost is one file and its tests, and the output on today's archive does not change (T7's differential check). Not accepted silently: it is the question below.

  - S18, the binding. Steelman: own-rdd builds mechanical guarantees and should run under them; a workflow adds a hash-chained record of every hop, the goal's digest held at `verify`, the stage log, and the session projection. Chosen: one shared goal `odd/goals/own-rdd.json` and one workflow and one role chain per remaining task (`own-rdd-t17`, `own-rdd-t7`, ...), because a chain ends at its delivery and a workflow follows one chain, so the engine needs no change. Rejected: one workflow for the feature with no chain (the hops would not be linked to it); one workflow for the feature that binds successive chains (a new event kind of the workflow log, a persisted-format change, Risk 1, before any remaining task). Strongest counter-argument: owner decision 1a chose role-chain records without a workflow; a binding is repository-wide and puts every session of every worktree of this repository under the workflow's projection and memory gate; the five early stages are recorded after the fact for each task; and per-commit reviews do not fit the one review hop of a chain, so only the last review of each task is a hop. Accepted: that ceremony and those limits, for a verifiable record of each task's hops; the directive narrows nothing, so the gate reads as the profile allows.
  - S19, the harness. Steelman: a gate that the harness checks cannot be forgotten, unlike a protocol line the parent reads. Chosen: the builder guard, the one point every large feature already passes with a citation of its feature document, gains the workflow check, plus one verb that opens a task's workflow so the deny message names a single command. Rejected: a new PreToolUse hook on Write of `odd/tasks/<f>.md` (it fires before any goal or chain can exist, so it could only nag); an extra need of the stop hook (it asks per commit, after the code is written); the orchestrator's ODD instructions (prose the runtime installs, not ours to enforce). Strongest counter-argument: work the parent writes inline never launches a builder and escapes the gate, and the guard now denies builder launches in every repository where `install-hooks` wired it, so a project with no goal file stops until it writes one. Accepted: the builder is the route of every shaped task (memories "Design is shaper work" and "Use our own agents"), and S14 already accepted a speed bump of the same kind.
  - S7, ODD. Steelman: a merge of a feature's pull request is the one moment the tree that lands can be held, mechanically and in CI, to what stored reviews cleared, without any report. Chosen: an engine verb `review anchor --merge HEAD` run by a CI job on pull requests (GitHub's prepared merge) and on pushes to `main` (the real merge), the feature named by the feature documents the merge changes, the chain judged by `CheckChain`, the tree of the merge compared with the tree of the reviewed head; the openspec gate unchanged. Rejected: L83's design (no ODD report reaches the openspec branch); a merge mode inside `tools/archive-anchor-gate` (its build would take in the engine's review packages and mix two sources; the domain and use case belong in the engine, where the delivery role can run the same verb, after the memory "Hexagonal and clean code at maximum"); the anchor written into the feature document (the delivery writes before the merge exists, `skills/inception-pipeline/SKILL.md:134`, and a commit after the merge would need its own review). Strongest counter-argument: once required, the job refuses every merge of a branch behind its base, and bringing it up to date adds commits that need records, which with per-commit reviews is costly; it duplicates the delivery's local `review check --base`; and it turns `main` red after a squash merge or a direct push. Accepted: S1 asks a machine to check what prose states, and CI cannot be skipped where a local command can; one slice at a time keeps branches up to date.
  - Order: T16, T17 (T17a, T17b, T17c), then T7 (T7a, T7b), T9, T8, T12. The binding first, so T17 runs inside a workflow; S19 before T7 as the parent asked, so T7 is opened with `workflow open` once T17 is installed. One reason to weigh otherwise, not taken: T9's S12 (no `reviewer` record for exempted work) makes the chain records honest for exempted heads, which S18 (d) records as `reviewer → delivery`; the owner's order (L79) keeps T9 after T7.

  - Steelman of the gap's premise: a task split into subtasks is one delivery (one PR per task, the header) and one chain ends at its first delivery (`engine/roles/chain.go:62-64`), so subtasks must share the task's chain; the clean review is the honest evidence that the builder may go on. Rejected: (1) a workflow per subtask (the guard's remedy today): a subtask has no delivery of its own, so its chain could never end, and it contradicts L86 and the T7 row; (2) a new forward edge, for example `reviewer → estimator` or `reviewer → shaper`, or a new terminal state other than `delivery`: a change of a contract `roles append`, `VerifyChain` and every chain reader consume (Risk 3) for a hop the existing edge already carries; (3) the guard allowing a builder when the chain is at `reviewer` and the last review is clear: it drops the record of the hop, so the chain would no longer say who handed the work on, against the guard's purpose (S19 a); (4) a verb, for example `labdrian workflow next --task T7b`, that checks the review is clear and the task a subtask and writes the hop: mechanical, as S19 wants, but a new verb with its own rules over a hop the parent already writes by hand for every other transition, and it needs a machine-readable parent-subtask relation the document does not have (D11); (5) a distinct `payload_kind` for the next subtask (`feature-doc` with the document's digest): it would tell the hop from rework, but `roles append` holds only a `review-report` to its evidence, so the clean review would go unchecked, against the owner's choice in L93 (D14).

  - Steelman of S9: Codex's native `spawn_agent` resets a child's sandbox to the parent's (D11), so a reviewer agent file cannot be read-only there; a separate `codex exec --sandbox read-only` is the only enforced read-only Codex offers, and an engine verb that binds, launches and records in one step turns three prose steps of the parent into one checked command (S1). Strongest counter-argument to the chosen design: the engine now starts a third-party agent CLI whose flags moved between 0.148.0 and 0.164.0, no test can prove the sandbox holds, and the instructions are read from a deployed file that can be stale; a mistake would let a "read-only" reviewer write, unseen. Accepted tradeoff: the verb owns only the argv (pinned exactly by tests) and the record (reused), the read-only claim is proved once by a live check (D25) and re-proved on a Codex upgrade, and the stale-file risk is the one `labdrian apply` and `status` already carry for every deployed agent. Rejected: the parent pasting the reviewer's instructions into the brief (prose again, D23 alt); the engine embedding the role sources (`go:embed` cannot reach `roles/` outside the `engine` module; a copy would be a second source); a `codex exec` without `--ignore-user-config`, which would load the person's MCP servers, a write path the shell sandbox does not cover (D30).
  - Open decisions (owner): **D23** where the Codex reviewer's instructions come from: designed, the deployed `<codex root>/agents/reviewer.toml` read by the inverse of the generator; alternatives the brief or an embedded copy. **D24** S10 counted as met without a live Pi verifier run: recommended. **D25** the live check: after T8c the parent, not the builder, runs one `review launch` against the real Codex on a scratch repository, with a brief that also asks the reviewer to create a file in its worktree, and S9 is checked off only when no file was made and the reply was recorded; it needs the owner's consent (it spends Codex credit and uses the person's Codex login). **D26** the polisher judges through the launcher (v3 `:67`) are out of T8: recommended, recorded as a limit. **D27** `--model` required with no default: recommended. **D28** a refused brief, runtime or model is exit 1 (an argument of the parent, as `--rows`), a refused reply exit 2 (as `review record`): recommended. **D29** the skill line may push `role-chain` over `BodyRecommendedTokens` (700; about 690 before it, L97 estimate): recommended to add it and stop if the budget test fails; the owner re-approves the skill either way. **D30** `--ignore-user-config` on every launch: recommended; tradeoff, a person whose model provider is set only in `config.toml` cannot launch (codex fails, exit 1).
